# v3.0 — Phase 44: v2.0 Carry-Forward & Debt Consolidation

> **Corrected 2026-08-25 (ADR 0018).** This is the **defect register**, not the plan
> of record. Phase 44 is not the current milestone and not the next action — the
> current milestone is **P2 — Web complete + deploy**. See `.planning/STATE.md`.
> Compiled 2026-07-28; entries below are dated as of that compile unless annotated.

Everything v2.0 left behind, as **one phase with 14 subphases**. Ordered by what
it costs to leave alone, not by which document it came from.

**Phase 23 (Gmail OAuth2) is dropped.** Outbound email keeps its working SMTP
app-password fallback. Note that 44.1's email-pipeline fix **survives that
decision** — the provider-agnostic Postmark webhook is already code-complete and
publishes `email.inbound.received`, so registering the consumers makes *that* path
work. Only the Gmail OAuth2 credential gate died.

## Three findings that appear in no prior planning document

Found by direct verification during this compile, not by reading docs:

1. **44.1a — `one-tap-actions` has no auth guard** and trusts a URL parameter.
2. **44.1b — wine-library duplicate-add silently loses stock** and announces the
   loss as a success to other pages.
3. **44.2a — five agents are registered and empty**, so the orchestrator reports
   them live.

All three are the same failure mode: **code that exists, is reachable, and does
nothing.** That is strictly worse than absent code, because absence is visible.
The v2.0 audit named this once (unregistered email agents) and it recurred five
more times.

---

## Track A — Live defects (do these first)

### 44.1 Auth and data-integrity defects

**44.1a — Close the `one-tap-actions` auth hole.** *(new)*
`apps/api-gateway/src/one-tap-actions/one-tap-actions.controller.ts:39` is
`@Controller("one-tap-actions")` with **no `@UseGuards`**; every route takes
`restaurantId` from the URL path, and `userId` is hardcoded `"system"` (TODOs at
:121, :174). The global TenantGuard deliberately returns `true` for
unauthenticated requests, so this is internet-reachable and writes for any tenant
named in the URL — identical to the ux-optimizer hole closed in v2.0.
→ Class-level `JwtAuthGuard`, identity from `@CurrentUser()`, test for 401.

**44.1b — Fix silent stock loss on duplicate-add. ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-25 — STALE.**
`apps/web/src/data/inventoryData.ts` **no longer exists**: deleted in `e92941cf`
(2026-07-30, two days after this item was filed; that commit names 44.1b). Zero
importers remain — the only matches are three comments in
`AddToInventoryFromLibraryModal.tsx` explaining why it must not come back. Both
write paths persist via `createInventoryItem` / `updateInventoryItem`. Verified
2026-08-25 during the P2.4 burn-down; the original text is kept below as the record.

**44.1e — `InvoiceScannerModal` posts to an endpoint that does not exist.** *(new 2026-08-25)*
Found while verifying 44.1b. `e92941cf` states the modal is NOT orphaned — it is
still reachable from `/inventory-legacy` — and it posts to
`/invoices/:id/add-to-inventory`, which no controller implements, so it 404s on
use. The debt register previously recorded this modal as dead code, which is
wrong. → Either wire it to the real procurement/document intake path or remove
the entry point; folded into the `/inventory-legacy` retirement question
([ADR 0019](decisions/0019-p2-build-scope.md) §B, held for the founder).

✅ **CLOSED — and this entry was wrong twice.** The modal was deleted in `e5402d67`,
so it was not reachable from anywhere when this was filed; `/inventory-legacy` itself
is retired as of 2026-08-26. Nothing to wire, no entry point to remove.

*(original 44.1b text follows)*
**44.1b — Fix silent stock loss on duplicate-add.** *(new)*
`apps/web/src/data/inventoryData.ts` holds an in-memory `inventoryStore`; its four
mutators push, `console.log`, return (TODOs: "Call Supabase API to persist").
`AddToInventoryFromLibraryModal.tsx:302` calls `updateInventoryQuantity` on the
duplicate-add path **then dispatches `dispatchInventoryUpdate`** — other pages sync
to a number the database never received; a refresh loses it.
→ Route through `apply_stock_movement`, then delete the module (see 44.5).

**44.1c — Revive inbound email consumption.**
`EmailIntelAgent` and `EmailParsingAgent` are fully implemented and **absent from
`services/agent-orchestrator/core/orchestrator.py:141 _register_agent_classes()`**
(verified against the live registry). Also `EmailIntelAgent` subscribes to
`email.inbound.raw`, which has **zero publishers**; the real webhook emits
`email.inbound.received`.
→ Register both, fix the key, add one integration test that publishes the real key
and asserts a consumer ran.

**44.1d — `notifications` writes. ✅ RESOLVED, and the documented symptom was the
wrong one.**

The register said the bug was `status:'unread'` versus `is_read:False`. Reading the
live schema settled that instantly — `status` exists (varchar, default `'unread'`),
`is_read` **does not exist at all** — but the `is_read` call site had already been
corrected before this pass, so that mismatch was stale.

**The real defect was bigger and neither agent's fault was the field name.** The
table carries two generations of columns, and three of the originals are still NOT
NULL with no default: `recipient_id`, `notification_type`, `channels`. **Both**
agents omitted **all three**. Proven against the live database:

```
INSERT INTO notifications (restaurant_id, type, title, message, status, metadata) …
ERROR: 23502: null value in column "recipient_id" violates not-null constraint
```

So every agent notification was rejected, inside a `try/except` whose message read
"Notification insert failed (non-critical)". It was not non-critical — it was
total. Provider-communication notices and PROVINT-04 unknown-sender alerts reached
nobody, and the only trace was one log line. **A caught exception around a write
that always fails is indistinguishable from a feature nobody triggers** — the same
shape as 44.1a's swallowed 404 and 44.1b's phantom stock event.

Fixed by adding `core/notifications.py`, mirroring the api-gateway's
`persistForRestaurant` (which had already solved this, with a comment naming the
legacy columns): resolve the restaurant's members, fan out one row per member, fill
the legacy columns, truncate `title` to its column width, and return an insert
count so "reached nobody" is distinguishable from "written". Both agents now route
through it.

Verified end to end: the fixed shape inserts against the live table, and the probe
row was removed. 9 new tests in `tests/test_agent_notifications.py` assert every
NOT NULL column is populated, so a future omission fails in CI rather than in
production silence.

**44.1e — Saved sommelier conversations never load. Same shape as OD-45, different
cause.** *(new — found by the OD-72 RLS census, 2026-08-25)*

`apps/web/src/pages/SommelierAI.tsx:91` renders saved conversations from
`useSommelierQueries.ts:26`, which queries `sommelier_conversations` on the browser's
anon-key Supabase client. The table is **not** one of OD-45's 142 naked-RLS tables —
it has two policies — and it returns `[]` anyway. Its policy is
`USING (user_id = auth.uid())`, and **this product does not use Supabase Auth**:
`AuthContext.tsx` holds a gateway-issued JWT in `localStorage` and never hands the
Supabase client a session, so `auth.uid()` is permanently NULL and the policy can
never match. Verified against production: `GET /rest/v1/sommelier_conversations`
with the real anon key returns `200 []`.

The failure is then swallowed twice — RLS denial is not an error, and
`useSommelierQueries.ts:31-34` catches any error, logs
`"sommelier_conversations table may not exist yet"` and returns `[]`. The write path
fails loudly instead: `upsertConversation` violates the `WITH CHECK`, so "save"
throws while "load" lies.

→ Route through the gateway, as ADR 0012 did for reports. This is the second and
last live anon-key read in the product; closing it empties `lib/supabase.ts` of
callers. **52 of 73 policies in the database reference `auth.*`** and are equally
unsatisfiable from this browser — see [OD-72](decisions/OD-72-rls-census.md) §3.
Founder call is [OD-72](decisions/OPEN-DECISIONS.md).

**44.1f — Adding a location locked the owner out of it. ✅ FIXED 2026-09-03.**
*(measured on a local gateway on `main` `47f971de`, real call)*

An owner added a location through the product's own door —
`POST /organizations/locations`
(`apps/api-gateway/src/organizations/organizations.controller.ts:119` →
`organizations.service.ts` `createLocation`). The `restaurants` row was written
(slug `sim-meyhouse-9ef3dde7`, org set) and **no `user_restaurant_access` row was**,
so the very next call on that restaurant —
`GET /restaurants/:id/operating-hours`, which goes through
`MembersService.assertMembership` (`restaurants/members.service.ts:25`) —
answered `403 "Access denied to this restaurant"`. `assertMembership` reads a URA
row and falls back only to `users.restaurant_id`, which still names the creator's
ORIGINAL restaurant; there is no org-level path. `registerRestaurant`
(`auth/auth.service.ts:759`) has always written that row for the founding owner —
the SECOND door into `restaurants` did not, so the two creation paths produced
differently-usable restaurants.

→ Fixed: `createLocation` now inserts `user_restaurant_access {role: "owner",
is_active: true}` for the creator after the restaurant insert, and on an insert
failure deletes the just-created restaurant and throws — the same rollback
discipline `registerRestaurant`'s catch block has, because a restaurant nobody can
reach is worse than no restaurant (it still occupies its slug and lists in the org).
Spec: `organizations/create-location-grants-access.spec.ts` (both assertions fail on
the pre-fix tree).

**Second finding, NOT fixed — the two authorisation paths disagree.**
`AuthService.switchRestaurant` (`auth/auth.service.ts:446`) *does* gate, but on a
weaker rule: URA row **or**, failing that, an org-level fallback — the target
restaurant merely has to sit in one of the caller's organisations (:475-511). That
fallback is why `POST /auth/switch-restaurant` happily minted a session for the
locked-out location while `assertMembership` refused every request made with it.
So the platform has two membership answers for the same pair: **org-level for
minting a session, row-level for using it.** The fix above removes the symptom for
newly-created locations, not the disagreement. Deliberately left open: the fallback
also carries legacy users who have no URA row at all, so deleting it is a data
question (backfill URA rows for existing org members) and not a one-line check —
worth a decision record rather than a drive-by edit.

**44.1g — `POST /auth/register` let anyone name a house and a role. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-18.**
The route was `@Public()` and wrote `restaurant_id` and `role` from the request body
onto a new `users` row (`auth.service.ts:368-369` at `3a752010`). The token it returned
was scoped to that house in that role: `generateTokens` keeps `users.role` when no
`user_restaurant_access` row exists (`:586-596`), and only the logs and members
endpoints re-check membership (`assertMembership`) **[corrected 2026-09-18, PR #393's
audit, second sweep in its third round. A grep of `apps/api-gateway/src`, with specs,
`testing/` and comment lines excluded, finds these membership re-checks:
`MembersService.assertMembership` (10 calls in 3 files: members, logs, operating hours);
`OrganizationsService.assertCanManageRestaurant` and `resolveRestaurantRole`, with the
private `assertManagerOrOwner` (40 calls in 15 files: billing, text credits, text
senders, catalog ingest, distributor feed, integrations OAuth, MCP connections ×2, MCP
keys, organizations, payment methods, procurement documents, procurement, providers,
settings); `TeamService.assertAccess` (35 calls in 5 team files);
`AuthService.getUserRoleAtRestaurant` (`auth.service.ts:1416`, 1 call: `GET /auth/me/role`);
`AuthService.switchRestaurant` (`auth.service.ts:443`, 1 call: `POST /auth/switch-restaurant`);
and two inline reads, `integrations-oauth.service.ts:837` (a house's members, for its
unattributed-grant count) and `price-index-review.service.ts:464` (the owners and
managers who may admit a refusal). The first three accept the `users` row when there
is no access row: the first two at the `users.role` it names, `assertAccess` only as
staff. `switchRestaurant` has no `users`-row fallback but an organisation one
(`:472-511`): with no access row it admits ANY house of an organisation the person
belongs to, taken from `organization_members` or, when they have none, from the house
`users.restaurant_id` names, and the token then carries `users.role` (`generateTokens`,
`:536-546`). `getUserRoleAtRestaurant` and the two inline reads read access rows only.
So the row this route wrote passed the first three and `switchRestaurant`. The first
sweep counted four helpers and missed the last four sites. PR #393's round-3 verifier
found one more, which that round's build had listed only in its evidence:
`ProspectsService.accessibleRestaurantIds`
(`common/orchestrator/prospects.service.ts:309`, 1 call: `GET /prospects?scope=all`)
reads the person's active access rows, then adds the token's house (`:323`) without
checking it is among them. So the multi-house view reaches whichever house the token
names: the `restaurantId` it was signed with or, with none, `users.restaurant_id`
(`jwt.strategy.ts:29-32`). `RolesGuard` does not read
the token's role. It gates on `users.role`, a GLOBAL column: `jwt.strategy.ts:59` sets
`role: user.role ?? payload.role` from `validateJwtPayload`'s unscoped `users` read]**.
So knowing a house's id was enough to mint an owner's token for it. No web or mobile surface called the route:
sign-up is `POST /auth/register/restaurant` (a new house) and joining is by
invitation. Found by the notify lane's confirmer (ADR 0149 wave 4). Production read
2026-09-18: 8 users; the only one with a house and no active access row is a manager
created 2026-05-09 during setup, and no owner was ever written this way. Closed by
answering 410 before the service is touched and deleting `AuthService.register`
(`register-is-closed.spec.ts`, red on the pre-fix files). The web's legacy
`authStore.register` and `AuthContext.register` still post to it and are called
from nowhere; they go with the ADR 0149 cutover manifest.

**44.1h — A manager could mint an owner's invite. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-18 for the
invitation door. What stays open is 44.1i–44.1o and 44.1q (44.1p closed in PR #393's fourth
round).**
**[Updated 2026-09-18, PR #393's sixth round: 44.1j and 44.1q are closed too, and so is
44.1n's Team-page bullet.]**
Found by the adversarial reviewer on PR #392. `POST /auth/invite` is
`@Roles("owner","manager")`. `RolesGuard` gates on `users.role`: `JwtStrategy.validate`
sets `role: user.role ?? payload.role` (`jwt.strategy.ts:59`) from
`validateJwtPayload`'s unscoped `users` read (`auth.service.ts:676`). That is one
GLOBAL value per person, not their role in the house the body names. `generateInvite`
(`auth.service.ts:1038-1061` at `09190ee5`) checked only that the caller had SOME
active access row there, or a `users` row naming the house. It then wrote `dto.role`
unchanged, and `InviteDto` allows `owner`. So a manager could mint an owner's invite,
and a manager in one house who is staff in another could mint any invite for the
second. `/auth/join` makes the redeemer whatever the invite says.

*What PR #393 closes.* `generateInvite` reads the inviter's role in the invited house
exactly as `MembersService.assertMembership` reads it (`members.service.ts:26-52`), and
then caps it by one rule:

- An active `user_restaurant_access` row there decides, and its role is the answer. A
  NULL role grants nothing.
- With no such row, a `users` row whose `restaurant_id` names the house is read at
  `users.role || "staff"`. Any other `users` row grants nothing.
- A failed read of either answers 503. That is the one named difference:
  `assertMembership` discards both errors, so a failed read looks like "no row".
- A value the rank table does not hold grants nothing. The table is a `Map`, so
  `"constructor"` is not a role.

Who may grant what is [ADR 0162](decisions/0162-managers-grant-manager-or-staff-on-both-doors.md)
(founder, 2026-09-18): an owner grants any role, a manager grants manager or staff,
and staff grant nothing. The rule holds on both doors, the invitation and
`MembersService.addMember`, which now share one implementation (`grantRefusal`,
`auth/role-grant.ts`). Before this, `addMember` refused a manager who added a manager.
Through `assertMembership`'s `users` fallback it also let an unknown role (`"admin"`)
add an owner.

Production, read-only on 2026-09-18 (the orchestrating session's read, re-run by the
round-3 build the same day):

- 8 users carry `users.restaurant_id`.
- 1 of them has no active access row for that house. That user is a manager created
  2026-05-09; the house their `users.restaurant_id` names (YAREN) was created the same
  day. They hold 0 access rows for YAREN, active or inactive, and active manager rows
  for two other houses (ALDEMIR, YARDOM), both created 2026-08-05. They are a
  setup-era member of YAREN whom `assertMembership` admits there, not someone who left.
  One limit on that reading: every removal path deletes the access row rather than
  deactivating it, and `system_audit_log` holds 0 rows in production, so a deleted row
  would leave no trace. `removeMember` is ruled out, because it clears
  `users.restaurant_id`; `leaveRestaurant` and `TeamService.deleteMember` are not.
- 0 users have no active access row anywhere.
- 0 access rows have a NULL role.
- 0 users have a role outside owner/manager/staff.

**[Corrected 2026-09-18, PR #393's third round.** The second round removed the
`users`-row fallback from `generateInvite` on the reading that no member relied on it
and that its only live effect was letting a person mint invites into a house they had
left. Both halves were false: the one user above relies on it, and 0 users in
production match the stale chain. Removing it would have locked a legitimate manager
out of inviting into YAREN while `assertMembership` still admits them there. The
fallback is back, capped by the same ceiling.**]**

Tests: `invite-role-ceiling.spec.ts` has 19 tests. Since PR #393's fourth round it
runs on the filter-honouring stub (`team/testing/supabase-stub.ts`). The chain it used
before answered every read with one canned row whatever the filters, so a read with
`.eq("restaurant_id", …)` or `.eq("is_active", true)` deleted passed the whole file
(the round-3 verifier). On the pre-fix service (`09190ee5`), 11 fail. On PR #393's
first cut (`d9fd8f1c`), 3 fail: the NULL role (which that cut read from the `users`
row) and `"constructor"` from either row. On its second cut (`3416205`), 2 fail: the
legacy member read from the `users` row, and the 503 on a failed `users` read. Four
tests each seed a row that grants the invite only if the read dropped a filter (the
house, `is_active` or the person on the access read; the person on the `users` read).
They pass on every cut, and each fails when its filter is removed (round-4 jest mutation
table). `members.service.spec.ts` gains 5 `addMember` tests; 3 fail on `09190ee5` and
on `d9fd8f1c`, and the other 2 pin behaviour this PR does not change: staff are
refused, and an owner may add an owner. It gains 7 `removeMember` tests: the 2
refusals fail on all three earlier cuts, and 2 more seed an inactive row here and a
member of another house only, so dropping either filter from the target read fails
them. It gains 13 `updateMemberRole` tests: 3 hand-over pins and 1 co-owner pin that
pass on every cut (44.1n), and 9 on a role change's scope (44.1p), 6 of which fail on
the round-3 source and on every earlier cut. All 52 pass on the round-4 source. Nothing the web's invite dialog
offers changes behaviour (manager or staff, `InviteTeamDialog.tsx:112-113`). No web
or mobile client calls `addMember`. The swallowed-read baseline is in
`.planning/03-scenarios/DELIVERY-AUDIT.md`.

*What stays open.*

- The stale chain is still possible in principle on every membership check that
  reads the `users` row, this door included (44.1i). 0 instances in production. The
  one member the read serves gets an access row from migration `20260918153000`; the
  read can retire after that is applied in production, not before (44.1i).
- The writers that would leave a stale row behind are unfixed (44.1j). **[Closed
  2026-09-18, PR #393's sixth round (44.1j).]**
- `users.role` is unconstrained (44.1k).
- `generateInvite` still swallows two reads (44.1l).
- `RolesGuard` still pre-filters this route, and every `@Roles` route, on global
  `users.role`. That is harmless here now that the service decides. **[Corrected
  2026-09-18, PR #393's sixth round: `RolesGuard` now reads the person's role in the
  house the token names, not `users.role` (44.1q, closed).]**

**44.1i — The `users`-row fallback admits a stale row as well as a legacy one.
⚠️ OPEN, theoretical: 0 production instances measured (filed 2026-09-18, PR #393;
corrected the same day in its third round).**
Three helpers and one door accept the `users` row when there is no active access row:

- `MembersService.assertMembership` (`members.service.ts:26`, fallback :43-52), at the
  `users.role` it names. It covers members, logs and operating hours, and backs
  `addMember`.
- `OrganizationsService.resolveRestaurantRole` (`organizations.service.ts:160`,
  fallback :175-181), at the `users.role` it names. It sits behind
  `assertCanManageRestaurant` (40 calls in 15 files, listed in 44.1g's bracket).
- `TeamService.assertAccess`, only as staff (ADR 0088).
- `AuthService.generateInvite`, since PR #393's third round, read the way
  `assertMembership` reads it and capped by ADR 0162.

Two more read the same row to decide whether a TARGET is a member here:

- `MembersService.updateMemberRole` (`members.service.ts:204-218`, since PR #393's
  fourth round): a target with no access row here is a member, at
  `users.role || "staff"`, when their `users` row names the house.
- `MembersService.removeMember` (`members.service.ts:323-335`): the same, at
  `users.role`. **[Sixth round, 2026-09-18: now `:323-353`, with both reads' errors
  bound.]**

**[Added 2026-09-18, PR #393's sixth round.]** A seventh site gives `req.user` its
role: `roleInHouse` (`apps/api-gateway/src/auth/house-role.ts:41-53`), called by
`AuthService.validateJwtPayload` for the house the token names (44.1q, closed). It
reads the fallback exactly as `assertMembership` does, at `users.role || "staff"`.
Claim `ADR-0162-ROLE-IN-TOKEN-HOUSE` pins it exactly, and it retires with the other
six, not before.

`AuthService.switchRestaurant` has an organisation fallback instead
(`auth.service.ts:472-511`): with no access row, any house of an organisation the
person belongs to. `getUserRoleAtRestaurant` and the inline reads in
`integrations-oauth.service.ts:837` and `price-index-review.service.ts:464` read
access rows only. The full sweep is in 44.1g's bracket.

The stale chain: a person removed from house A by a writer that leaves
`users.restaurant_id` naming A (44.1j), then promoted in house B, which raised
`users.role` everywhere (`updateMemberRole`, `members.service.ts:226-229` at round 3). Each
check above would still admit them to A. **[Corrected 2026-09-18, PR #393's fourth
round: since 44.1p closed, a change in house B writes `users.role` only when the
`users` row names B (`members.service.ts:255-259`), so a promotion in B no longer
raises what a stale row naming A claims. A stale row still admits the person to A at
the role it held when they left.]** Production on 2026-09-18 has 0 users of
that shape. The 1 user whose `users.restaurant_id` has no access row behind it is
the setup-era member of YAREN described in 44.1h, whom the fallback exists to admit.
**[Corrected 2026-09-18, PR #393's third round: this entry said production had
exactly 1 user of the stale shape and that no member relied on the fallback. Both
were false.]**

The fix cannot be to delete the fallback. That would lock the YAREN manager out of
every check above. It needs, first, a decision on what that membership is. The
choices are an access row written for YAREN, or a ruling that they are not a member
there. After that, the fallback can go and the writers in 44.1j can be closed.
**[Sixth round, 2026-09-18: the writers in 44.1j are closed. The fallback is not
retired; it still waits for the production row.]**
Neither is built here. **[Decided 2026-09-18, the founder's answer B, "Give them a
manager row" (ADR 0162, second addendum). Migration
`20260918153000_a_setup_era_manager_holds_their_house_by_a_row.sql` (PR #393's fourth
round) writes that manager's access row for YAREN: manager, active, one row at most,
matched on the whole measured tuple, and nothing on a fresh database or a second run.
It is not a generic backfill, because of 44.1j. Once it is applied in production, the
fallback can retire in a follow-up, from all six sites above. It must not retire
before: without that row the YAREN manager loses YAREN. The open claim
`ADR-0162-USERS-ROW-FALLBACK-RETIRED` fails the build as soon as any of three
admissions changes: `assertMembership`'s, `resolveRestaurantRole`'s or
`assertAccess`'s, each pinned exactly, so an admission removed while its `users` read
stays trips it too. The other three sites are pinned by resolved claims that fail
when they change (`generateInvite` by `ADR-0147-INVITE-ROLE-CEILING` and
`ADR-0162-ONE-GRANT-RULE-BOTH-DOORS`; the target reads by
`ADR-0162-ROLE-CHANGE-THIS-HOUSE-ONLY` and `ADR-0162-OWNERS-REMOVE-OWNERS`). So
whoever retires any of the six by editing those pinned spans meets this paragraph
first. The pins are textual: a guard added OUTSIDE a pinned span (for example an
`if (!access) throw` before `if (access) {` in `assertMembership`) retires a fallback
without tripping them; only the jest specs catch that (round-5 verifier, TE1-TE4,
2026-09-18).]**

**44.1j — Leaving or being removed leaves the `users` row pointing at the house.
✅ CLOSED 2026-09-18 (filed in PR #393, closed in its sixth round).**
Two writers delete the access row but leave `users.restaurant_id` and `users.role`:

- `AuthService.leaveRestaurant` (`auth.service.ts:2507`, delete at :2535-2539).
- `TeamService.deleteMember` (`team.service.ts:450`, delete at :499-503).

`MembersService.removeMember` does clear `users.restaurant_id` (`members.service.ts:362-365,
397-400`). It clears it unconditionally, even when the column names a different house.
`MembersService.updateMemberRole` writes `users.role` for the person everywhere
(`:226-229` at round 3), so a promotion in house B raises what a stale row claims in house A.
**[Corrected 2026-09-18, PR #393's fourth round: it no longer does. Since 44.1p closed,
it writes `users.role` only when the `users` row names the house being changed
(`members.service.ts:255-259`). These two writers are also why the YAREN row (44.1i)
was written for one fingerprinted tuple and not by a backfill: a backfill of every
`users` row without an access row would re-admit everyone they removed.]**
These writers could build the chain in 44.1i; production has 0 instances of it
(measured 2026-09-18). The fix is to clear
`users.restaurant_id` when it names the house being left, in both writers.

*The fix* (PR #393's sixth round, after the ADR 0090 merge audit of `dcf91322`
blocked on it, finding 2). `leaveRestaurant` (`auth.service.ts:2595-2609`) and
`TeamService.deleteMember` (`team.service.ts:549-560`) now clear `users.restaurant_id`
with an UPDATE filtered on `user_id` AND `restaurant_id` = the house being left, so a
`users` row naming another house is left alone. Each reads that write's error and
refuses on it. Each clears before it deletes the access row: if the delete then
fails, the person is still a member by their access row, which is true. The other
order would leave them a member by a row they meant to be gone. `removeMember`'s two
clears moved into `clearUsersRowHouse` (`members.service.ts:430-446`), with the
same filter and error check. Before, `removeMember` cleared the column whatever house
it named, so removing someone from house B also took away house A. Every
`users.restaurant_id = NULL` write in `apps/api-gateway/src` is now filtered to one
house; there are 3 of them, all listed here. Tests in `removal-only-that-house.spec.ts`:
after a leave or a Team-page removal, `updateMemberRole` on that person answers 404,
`generateInvite` refuses them, and `assertMembership` refuses them. Those tests, the
two clear-failure tests and `removeMember`'s other-house test fail on `dcf91322`;
the three tests pinning another house's row left alone, or this house's cleared, are
controls that pass on both. Production, read-only 2026-09-18 (Supabase MCP, SELECT
only): the only `users` row naming a house it holds no active access row for is the
setup-era YAREN manager that migration `20260918153000` covers (8 users with a house,
1 without a row there, 0 rows of any state for that pair). So there are 0 stale
leavers to repair. Claim: `ADR-0162-LEAVING-ENDS-MEMBERSHIP`.

**44.1k — `users.role` has no CHECK, and `user_restaurant_access.role` admits NULL.
⚠️ OPEN (filed 2026-09-18, PR #393).**
A production catalog read (`pg_constraint`) on 2026-09-18:

- `public.users` has no CHECK constraint. `role` is `varchar(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT
  'manager'` (baseline `20260805000000`, `public.users`).
- `user_restaurant_access.role` has carried `user_restaurant_access_role_known`
  (owner/manager/staff) since ADR 0088 (`20260902200000`). The column is nullable,
  and a NULL passes a CHECK.

Today there are 0 NULL access roles and 0 `users` roles outside the three, so nothing
is live. But `users.role` accepts any 20-character string. `RolesGuard` lets
`"admin"` through, and `assertMembership`'s `"owner|manager"` gate refuses only the
exact string `"staff"`, so an unknown value passes it as owner-or-manager.
`grantRefusal` (ADR 0162) gives an unknown value nothing. The fix is a CHECK on
`users.role` and NOT NULL on the access role, each measured first.

**44.1l — `generateInvite` still swallows two reads. ⚠️ OPEN (filed 2026-09-18,
PR #393).**

- The restaurant read (`auth.service.ts:1113-1122`) binds only `data`. A failed read
  answers 400 *"Restaurant has no organization. Complete registration first."*, which
  tells the person to do something that is not the problem.
- The code-collision read (`:1142-1147`) binds only `data`. A failed read looks like
  "code free" and ends the loop. The UNIQUE index `idx_org_invites_code` then turns a
  real collision into a generic 400 "Failed to generate invite".

Both are baselined in `scripts/read_error_baseline.json` (`restaurants::restaurant`,
`organization_invites::existing`).

**44.1m — Opening a house swallows the founding owner's three writes. ⚠️ OPEN
(filed 2026-09-18, PR #393's third round).**
`AuthService.registerRestaurant` (`auth.service.ts:740`) creates the owner's `users`
row and throws if that fails (`:830-831`). It then awaits three writes and reads none
of their errors:

- `organizations.owner_id` (`:834-837`);
- the `organization_members` owner row (`:839-843`);
- the `user_restaurant_access` owner row (`:845-853`).

If the access-row insert fails, the new house has no owner access row, and nobody is
told. The owner still gets in: their `users` row names the house at `owner`, and
`assertMembership`, `resolveRestaurantRole`, `assertAccess` and (since PR #393's third
round) `generateInvite` all read that row when there is no access row. The last-owner
guards count access rows only. So the founding owner could make someone else an owner and
still not step down: `updateMemberRole`'s guard (`members.service.ts:220-233`) would
count 1 owner, the other one. The fix is to read each error and roll back the registration, as the
`users` insert already does. These are writes, which `check_read_errors_not_swallowed.py`
does not scan, so there is no baseline row for them. Production shows 4 of 14 houses
with no active owner access row (44.1n); whether any of them came from this path is
not measured.

**44.1n — The owner rule holds on the Settings door only, and 4 houses have no owner.
⚠️ OPEN (filed 2026-09-18, PR #393's third round).**
The founder's rule (ADR 0162, dated addendum): owners manage owners, and every house
keeps at least one. What the code does, measured 2026-09-18:

- `MembersService.removeMember` (Settings' remove): a manager may remove a manager or
  staff, never an owner, since PR #393's third round (`members.service.ts:341-345`);
  the last owner cannot be removed (`:348-360`, `:370-383`).
- `MembersService.updateMemberRole` (Settings' role change): owner-only
  (`members.service.ts:176`); the only owner cannot step down (`:220-233`). An owner
  can make a co-owner, or promote someone and then step down, as two calls in that
  order. `members.service.spec.ts` pins both.
- `TeamService.deleteMember` (the Team page's remove, `team.service.ts:450`) is gated
  at manager (`:460`), so **a manager can remove an owner there** whenever another owner
  remains (`:484-496`). Its last-owner count does not filter `is_active` (`:485-489`),
  and `if (count && count <= 1)` (`:491`) skips the guard when the count read fails.
  **[Closed 2026-09-18, PR #393's sixth round. `deleteMember` (`team.service.ts:458`)
  now refuses a manager who removes an owner before any write, with the rule and
  message `removeMember` uses (`:519-526`). The owner is read the way
  `assertMembership` reads a member: an active access row, or with none a `users` row
  naming the house. An inactive owner row, which this removal would delete, counts as
  an owner too. The last-owner count filters `is_active` and refuses on
  `(count ?? 0) <= 1` (`:528-544`). Every read before the first write binds its error
  and answers 500: the roster row, the access row, the `users` row and the count. The
  access-row delete's error is read as well, so `accessRevoked: true` is not reported
  for a delete that failed. Production, read-only: 7 roster rows are linked to an
  active owner, and any of them could have been removed by a manager whenever another
  owner remained. 7 of the 10 tests under "the Team page's remove: owners manage
  owners" in `removal-only-that-house.spec.ts` fail on `dcf91322`. Claim
  `ADR-0162-TEAM-REMOVE-OWNERS`.]**
- Two owners stepping down at the same moment can both pass the count in
  `updateMemberRole`: the check and the write are separate statements with no lock.
  Theoretical.
- An owner known only by their `users` row can demote the only access-row owner.
  `updateMemberRole` checks the count only when the actor changes their own role.
  Theoretical.
- An owner can demote another owner without that owner's say: the count runs only
  when the actor changes their own role (`members.service.ts:220`), and demoting a
  co-owner leaves the actor as owner, so nothing refuses it. The founder's words speak
  of an owner degrading or handing over their OWN status; whether one owner may demote
  another is not decided. Open for the founder. **[Answered 2026-09-18, the founder:
  "Yes, any owner" (ADR 0162, second addendum). Owners are equals: any owner may demote
  or remove another owner of the same house while one owner remains, which is what the
  code already does. `members.service.spec.ts` pins the demotion. Closed.]**
- `updateMemberRole` does not check that the target belongs to this house, so an
  owner of any house can change any person's global `users.role` (44.1p). **[Closed
  2026-09-18, PR #393's fourth round (44.1p).]**
- **4 of 14 houses in production have no active owner access row, and no user
  reaches them as owner through the `users`-row fallback either** (read-only,
  2026-09-18). Nobody can perform an owner-only act there. `updateMemberRole` is
  owner-only, so nobody can be made an owner of those houses without a database
  write. The founder's "min:1, owner" does not hold for them today. **[Answered
  2026-09-18 (ADR 0162, second addendum): left untouched. Re-measured read-only that
  day, the 4 hold 0 access rows, 0 `users` rows naming them and 0 inventory rows
  between them: Gullit's Tavern, Yaren's Fine Dine, a Meyhouse Palo Alto duplicate and
  a Sim Meyhouse duplicate. They are empty shells, and nothing is written.]**

Apart from `removeMember`'s owner guard (the first bullet, built in PR #393's third
round), nothing here is built in PR #393. Which owner each of the 4 houses should have
is the founder's call. **[Updated 2026-09-18, PR #393's fourth round: the co-owner fork and the
4 houses are answered above, and 44.1p is closed. Still open: the Team page's remove,
its count, the hand-over race, and a `users`-row owner demoting the only access-row
owner.]** **[Updated 2026-09-18, sixth round: the Team page's remove and its count are
closed (above). Still open: the hand-over race, and a `users`-row owner demoting the
only access-row owner.]**

**44.1o — `addMember` overwrites the person's organisation role. ⚠️ OPEN (filed
2026-09-18, PR #393's third round).**
After inserting the access row, `MembersService.addMember` upserts
`organization_members` on `(organization_id, user_id)` with the role just granted in
this ONE house (`members.service.ts:470-479`). An organisation owner added as staff to
a second house of the same organisation is now organisation staff. The upsert's error
is not read either. No web or mobile client calls `addMember` today (ADR 0162), so
this is not live. The fix is to insert the organisation row only when there is none,
and to read its error.

**44.1p — A role change in one house rewrote the person's role everywhere, member
or not. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-18 (filed in PR #393's third round, closed in its fourth).**
`MembersService.updateMemberRole` (`PATCH /restaurants/:id/members/:memberId`, called
by Settings) checked that the ACTOR is an owner of the house (`members.service.ts:170`
at round 3) and never checked that the TARGET is a member of it. Its access-row UPDATE
is scoped to the house (`:213-217` at round 3) and matches no row for a non-member,
which is not an error. The `users` UPDATE that followed was scoped to the person only
(`:226-229` at round 3) and its error was not read. So an owner of house A could set
the global `users.role` of anyone whose `user_id` they held, and `RolesGuard` gates
every `@Roles` route on that column (`jwt.strategy.ts:59`). An audit row and a notice
were written as though a member's role had changed. Anyone with a mailbox can become
an owner by opening a house (`POST /auth/register/restaurant`, public, then email
verification). Measured on the stub database, not live: an owner of A set a manager
of B, with no row in A, to `staff` in `users.role`; their access row in B still said
manager, and one audit row was written. The same `users` write is what let a promotion
in one house raise a stale row in another (44.1i, 44.1j).

*The fix* (the founder's answer A, 2026-09-18: *"Only that house"*; ADR 0162, second
addendum). Before any write, `updateMemberRole` reads the target's membership in the
house the way `assertMembership` reads one: an active access row there
(`members.service.ts:189-199`) or, with none, a `users` row whose `restaurant_id` names
it (`:204-218`), at `users.role || "staff"`. Anyone else gets 404; a failed read of
either gets 500. That role is the audit row's before-state, so a legacy member's
change records the role they had instead of `null`. The global `users.role` is written
only through an UPDATE filtered on this house (`:255-259`), so a member whose `users`
row names another house keeps the role that house gave them. A failed `users` write
refuses the change for a member known only by that row, where it is their role here,
and is logged for a member with an access row, whose change has already happened and
is then recorded (`:260-268`). Tests: 9 in `members.service.spec.ts` (*"a role changes
in this house only"*), 6 of which fail on the round-3 source and on every earlier cut;
10 jest mutants of the fix all fail at least one of them. **[Fifth round, 2026-09-18:
the round-4 verifier's V1, the `users` write with `.eq("user_id", …)` dropped, passed
all 52 tests. It rewrote `users.role` for everyone whose `users` row names the house,
the owner included, and only the claim caught it. *"writes the global role too when
the member's users row names this house"* now also asserts that the owner's and a
legacy member's `users.role` are unchanged, and V1 fails it. Re-run on the round-5
tree, all 30 jest mutants fail at least one test: the 16 from the fourth round and
the verifier's V1–V14.]** Claim: `ADR-0162-ROLE-CHANGE-THIS-HOUSE-ONLY`.

Still open, and not decided here: whether a change in one house should write
`users.role` at all. It stays written when the `users` row names that house, because
`RolesGuard` still gates on that column. What that means for a change in any other
house is 44.1q.

**44.1q — A role changed in a house the `users` row does not name never reaches
`RolesGuard`. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-18 (filed in PR #393's fifth round, closed in its
sixth).**
Found by PR #393's round-4 verifier. It follows from closing 44.1p (ADR 0162, answer
A). `RolesGuard` gates every `@Roles` route on `req.user.role`
(`auth/guards/roles.guard.ts:27`), and `jwt.strategy.ts:59` fills that from the
global `users.role`. Since 44.1p closed, `updateMemberRole` writes `users.role` only
when the person's `users` row names the house being changed
(`members.service.ts:255-259`). A change in any other house writes the access row
there and nothing else:

- Made manager in house B while staff in their `users`-row house, a person is still
  turned away in B by `@Roles("owner", "manager")`, for example on `POST /auth/invite`
  (`auth.controller.ts:437-439`).
- Demoted in B, they keep their `users.role` and pass `RolesGuard` there. What
  refuses them is the route's own check in that house, where it has one
  (`assertMembership`, `generateInvite`'s role read). Which `@Roles` routes lack such
  a check was not swept. **[Swept 2026-09-18 by the ADR 0090 merge audit of
  `dcf91322` (adversarial reviewer, finding 1, BLOCK), re-counted in the sixth round:
  38 `@Roles` decorators in 9 controllers (a 39th match, `identity-curation.controller.ts:29`, sits inside a JSDoc comment). 36 of them sit in 7 controllers
  (vendor-intel 17, price-index 9, commodity 4, house-mail-archive 3, and 1 each in
  ask-ai, distributor-feed and sender-trust) that call no membership check of their
  own (the audit's grep). Some of their services read access rows for other purposes
  (price-index review, distributor-feed); this sweep did not trace each route through
  them.]**

Production, read-only, 2026-09-18 (Supabase MCP, SELECT only): 14 active access rows
held by 8 people. 7 of them, held by 4 people, are in a house other than the one the
person's `users` row names, and none of those 7 differs in role from `users.role`.
So nobody is affected today; the first change of that kind in a second house makes
it live. The fix is a decision, not a line. One option is for `RolesGuard` to read
the role in the house the request names, not `users.role`. The other is to retire
`@Roles` in favour of the per-house checks. The question is the one 44.1p left open:
whether a change should write `users.role` at all.

*The fix* (PR #393's sixth round; the founder's answer A, *"Only that house"*: a role in
one house must never change what a person may do in another). The first option,
applied once, at the point every consumer reads. `AuthService.validateJwtPayload`
(`auth.service.ts:695`) now reads the person's role in the house the token names,
alongside the `users` read (`Promise.all`, so no extra round trip). It reads it
exactly as `assertMembership` does (`roleInHouse`, `auth/house-role.ts:41-53`): an
active access row decides, and a NULL role is no role; with none, a `users` row naming
the house counts at `users.role || "staff"`; otherwise there is no role.
`JwtStrategy.validate` (`jwt.strategy.ts:62-64`) puts that on `req.user.role` and
never falls back to `users.role` or the token's claim, so `@Roles` refuses a person
with no role there. A failed read answers 503 and grants nothing. A 403 would claim
something about the person that nothing had measured. Setting no role would close
only `RolesGuard`, while `IdentityService` also reads `req.user.role`. A token that
names no house keeps the old behaviour (`users.role`, then the token's claim), which
is stated and tested. The second option was not taken: it means 38 decorators and a
membership read repeated in 9 controllers, and each new route would be one more
place to forget.

Every consumer of `req.user.role` in the gateway, swept 2026-09-18: `RolesGuard`
(`roles.guard.ts:27`, all 38 `@Roles`); `VendorIntelController.actorOf`
(`vendor-intel.controller.ts:747`) into `IdentityService.requireActor`, whose role
backs `undo`'s own owner/manager check (`identity.service.ts:1030`) and is logged as
`decided_by_role` (`:1402`). Both are about the house the session acts in, so a
per-house role is the correct one for both. Everything else that reads a role reads
its own row: `assertMembership`, `assertAccess`, `resolveRestaurantRole`,
`generateInvite`, `price-index-review`. Three `AuthenticatedUser` interfaces declare
a `role` that nothing reads (`team.controller.ts:50`, `operating-hours.controller.ts:20`,
`members.controller.ts:24`). Not changed and not a `req.user` read: `GET /auth/me`
still reports the global `users.role` to the web (`auth.service.ts:1965`); the token's
own `role` claim (`generateTokens`) is already per-house.

Production, read-only 2026-09-18 (Supabase MCP, SELECT only), so nobody's access
changes silently at deploy. 14 active access rows, 0 inactive, 0 with a NULL role,
and 0 whose role differs from the holder's `users.role`, so no member's role moves.
1 `users` row names a house with no active row behind it: the YAREN manager, who
keeps manager there through the fallback. `switchRestaurant`'s organisation fallback
(`auth.service.ts:474-513`) can mint a session for 7 (person, house) pairs where the
person has no membership. All 7 belong to 3 simulation accounts, the Sim Bistro owner,
manager and staff, in Sim Meyhouse, its empty duplicate and Sim Vanilla Kaleiçi. Those
sessions used to pass `@Roles` on their Sim Bistro role, and now pass none there, as
answer A requires. `assertMembership` already refused them there.

Tests: `auth/role-in-house.spec.ts` runs the real `updateMemberRole`, then
`JwtStrategy.validate` over the real `validateJwtPayload`, then the real `RolesGuard`
on a real `@Roles("owner", "manager")`. Someone demoted in B is refused in B and let
through in A. Someone promoted in B is let through in B only. No membership, an
inactive row and a NULL role are each refused. A failed read is a 503. 7 of its 10
tests fail on `dcf91322`; the other 3 (the setup-era member, a blank house and a token
with no house) pin behaviour kept on purpose. Claim `ADR-0162-ROLE-IN-TOKEN-HOUSE`.
Still open: whether a role change should write `users.role` at all. It now matters
only for tokens that name no house.

**44.1r — Leaving a house does not end the session there, and organisation membership
opens any house.** ⚠️ OPEN, decided (filed 2026-09-18, PR #393 round-6 verifier; existed
before #393). `refreshAccessToken` (`auth.service.ts` ~:393, `scopedRestaurantId =
payload.restaurantId ?? users.restaurant_id`) re-mints a token naming the house with no
membership check, and `JwtStrategy.validate` admits a token whose house gives no role
(role null), so every route not limited by `@Roles` keeps answering a removed person.
`switchRestaurant`'s organisation fallback (~:471-511) opens a session in any house of
the organisation without a membership row; production 2026-09-18: 7 such pairs, all
three simulation accounts of Sim Bistro. Founder, 2026-09-18: **"Membership only"** (ADR
0162 fourth addendum). Owed: a follow-up PR where a token naming a house with no
membership is refused (401) and the organisation fallback opens nothing.

**44.1s — "Owner only" admits managers.** ⚠️ OPEN, decided (filed 2026-09-18, round-6
verifier; existed before #393). `RolesGuard` (`roles.guard.ts:30-37`) passes owner,
manager and admin whenever owner or manager is required; 11 non-spec `@Roles('owner')`
decorators exist (vendor-intel scrape, sweep, site-sweep/run, outlier-rejudge/run,
shop-sweep/run, price-index reopen-challenge and others). Founder, 2026-09-18: **"Keep
managers in"** - relabel those routes owner-or-manager so the code says what it does.
Owed: that relabel, with a test that `@Roles('owner')` and the guard agree.

**44.1t — A leaver's next login carries the role the house gave them.** ⚠️ OPEN (filed
2026-09-18, round-6 verifier). After PR #393 a leaver's `users.restaurant_id` is cleared,
so a fresh login names no house and `JwtStrategy` keeps `users.role` (column default
`manager`, measured in production) for a token with no house. Owed with 44.1r: reset
`users.role` when the house it came from is left.

**44.1v — A conversation id opened for any house.** **CLOSED 2026-09-19
([ADR 0171](decisions/0171-a-conversation-id-opens-only-for-the-house-that-owns-it.md),
branch `fix/conversation-approve-tenant-scope`).** Found by the one-tap adversary pass
(D4). On `origin/main` six `ConversationsController` routes sat behind `JwtAuthGuard`
alone and acted on a bare id or on every house: `POST :id/approve`, `PUT :id/message`,
`POST :id/reject`, `POST :id/summarize`, `GET :id` and `GET pending/list`. Any signed-in
user of any house could rewrite, approve or reject another house's vendor message
(`manager_approved_message` is what the agent sends on any `conversation.approved`,
`provider_conversation_agent.py:2813`). The HTTP dispatch is dead, so the write was live
and the send was not. Every route now answers only for the token's house, a foreign id is
a 404, and approve, edit and reject take owner or manager. Spec
`conversation-routes-belong-to-the-callers-house.spec.ts`; claim
`ADR-0171-CONVERSATION-ROUTES-HOUSE-SCOPED`. **Not fixed here:** `by-order` and
`by-provider` (same shape; separate unmerged branch `fix/conversations-by-order-tenant-scope`,
which files its own tripwire and will need it flipped when this lands),
`listConversations` and `getStats` fail open for a session that names no house (a removed
member's next login), the write routes still return a service error string as a 400,
`getPendingConversations` still turns a failed read into `[]`, `approve` does not check
current status, and whether this path must also require the ADR 0112 seal is an open
founder fork in the one-tap lane.

### 44.2 Hollow features that report success

**44.2z — Retired Gemini model ids across the orchestrator. ⚠️ PARTIALLY FIXED
2026-08-24 ([ADR 0010](decisions/0010-gemini-model-retirement.md), OD-57).**

Same shape as 44.2a: a path that looks healthy from the outside and does nothing.
`gemini-2.0-flash` was **shut down by Google on 2026-06-01** and `gemini-pro`
returns 404; both were still hardcoded in live code. Verified against the live API,
not inferred.

Two sites are fixed. `EmailIntelAgent` was failing all 3 retries and dead-lettering
every inbound message — a loud failure. `calendar_agent._call_llm_for_dates` was the
quiet kind: retired model *and* a missing `genai.configure()`, both swallowed by a
broad `except Exception` into a regex fallback, so it had silently never used an LLM
at all. It now extracts dates correctly (verified live).

**27 further references remain across 11 live non-test files** — see OD-57 for the
file:line list (24 dead calls + 3 metadata defaults). Most sit behind the same
swallow-and-fallback pattern, so the blast radius is unknown until each is
exercised. The calendar path also has a separate pre-existing fallback bug
(OD-63): its regex extractor is fed the prompt template, so every fallback
fabricates 3 dated rows.

Two real spend-ledger defects were found while pricing the replacement, and both
are fixed: `_RATES_PER_M` priced `gemini-2.5-flash` **8.3x under** its real output
rate, and all 18 Gemini call sites dropped `thoughts_token_count`, which Google
bills at the output rate (measured 598 billed vs 113 logged). Spend figures will
rise sharply as a result — that is the correction landing, not a regression. A
third change, longest-match rate lookup, is a **guard** rather than a repair: the
old table's lite/flash collision cost ~25% under-pricing on an id no live site
passed, but without the guard the corrected rates would have over-charged lite
output 6.25x.

An adversarial review of this work caught a regression before commit: the
unpriced-model guard originally keyed on rate-table membership, which nulled
Serper's flat configured per-query fee across 5 live sites and labelled known
spend `unpriced_model`. Now gated on provider, with regression tests.

A follow-up check of the Anthropic rows found the **same bug a third time**:
`claude-haiku` was priced at Claude Haiku **3.5**'s retired `(0.80, 4.00)` while
the repo calls `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` at `1.00 / 5.00` — under-recording 20%
across 11 call sites, the orchestrator's highest-volume model. Corrected;
`claude-sonnet` verified correct. Three independent instances of "a superseded
model's price inherited by its successor" is a process gap, not three accidents.

**All three closed 2026-08-25** by [ADR 0016](decisions/0016-ledgers-must-express-unknown.md).
OD-61: `api_spend.cost_usd` made nullable (migration written, **not applied**) and
`DEFAULT 0.0` dropped; one `unpriced` flag now drives both ledgers. OD-62:
`gpt-4-turbo` verified **correct** at $10/$30 — the suspicion did not hold — and
the real defect fixed, a rate's `verified` date and `source` are now required
fields, so a fourth recurrence is a `TypeError` at import rather than a discovery.
OD-63: the fallback reads the email; production cleanup was **0 rows**, because
`provider_important_dates` does not exist in the production database — now **OD-68**.

**44.2a — Five empty agents. ✅ RESOLVED, and my original claim here was wrong.**

The five are real stubs — `ghost_inventory_agent` (37 lines), `auto_pilot_agent`
(36), `negotiation_playbook_agent` (36), `shrinkage_detective_agent` (35),
`compliance_agent` (35) — each logging the event and returning.

**But I claimed they were live and consuming events, and they were not.** All five
are `AgentTier.OPTIONAL`, and `is_enabled()` requires
`AGENT_<NAME>_ENABLED`, defaulting to False. `_create_lazy_proxies()` skips them,
so they never instantiate and never subscribe. Health checks did not pass for them
and logs did not show events received. The only true part was that
`registered_count` counted them, so "Registered 20 agent classes" overstated what
was running.

**The real defect was the opposite of hollow-and-running: the gate was
unreachable.** `_build_feature_flags()` populated only three `FEATURE_*` keys and
never any `AGENT_<NAME>_ENABLED` key — so the switch the OPTIONAL tier documented
was one nothing read. `AGENT_GHOST_INVENTORY_AGENT_ENABLED=true` in the
environment did precisely nothing, silently.

Fixed: the flag builder now sweeps `os.environ` for `AGENT_*_ENABLED` (parsing the
string, since a truthiness check reads `"false"` as enabled); the five declare
`IS_STUB = True` and the orchestrator **refuses to start a stub even when its flag
is on** — now that the gate works, an operator could otherwise switch one on and
get an agent that subscribes to real events and discards them, which does look
healthy from every dashboard; and boot logging reports enabled-vs-gated rather
than a single count.

`auto_pilot_agent` is deliberately left unimplemented: its TODOs are "evaluate
`auto_pilot_rules`" and "create procurement orders", and autonomy stays
human-gated until the benchmark numbers are met. An empty auto-pilot that refuses
to boot is the correct state, not a gap.

Covered by 20 tests in `tests/test_agent_registry_gating.py`, including one that
fails if a future agent is OPTIONAL *and* implemented — the moment to revisit
whether the marker or the tier is wrong.

**44.2b — Settle the `ReportingAgent` contradiction.**
Phase 21 criterion #2 claims `stock.events → NotificationAgent + ReportingAgent`;
`ReportingAgent` only subscribes to `reporting.events`. The real golden path is
`POSIntegrationAgent → BufferManager → InventoryEngine`, not the ROADMAP's
simplified chain.
→ Subscribe it, or correct the criterion and topology. Prefer correcting the doc
if scheduled reports are the intent — but pick one.

**44.2c — `deploy.yml`'s "The deployed build IS the merged build" reported a
correct Railway skip as a failure. ✅ RESOLVED 2026-09-03 (ADR 0097
Correction; found alongside
[ADR 0090](decisions/0090-pr-audit-gate-autonomous-merge.md)'s merge).**
The inverse of this section's usual shape: not a hollow success, a hollow
*failure*. `@wineops/api-gateway`'s Railway service only rebuilds when a push
touches its own `watchPatterns` (`apps/api-gateway/**`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`,
`pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `package.json` — confirmed via `railway deployment list
--json` → `.meta.serviceManifest.build.watchPatterns`). `check_deployed_sha.py`
was called with `--expect` set to the raw merged sha regardless, so any
docs/CI/tooling-only merge — where Railway correctly returns
`skippedReason: "No changes to watched files"` and keeps serving its last real
build — reported MISMATCH for a deploy that behaved exactly as designed.
Confirmed live on two consecutive merges: PR #284 (docs only) and PR #261
(CI/tooling/`.claude`/`.planning` only) both skipped correctly and both then
failed this check.
→ Fixed: `scripts/resolve_watched_commit.py` (new, `--self-test` covers it
against a real git repo) resolves the last commit at-or-before the merge that
actually touches the watched paths; `deploy.yml`'s Stage 2 now expects THAT
commit, not the raw merge sha. Verified against the real PR #261 case before
landing: resolves to `2e9d0723` (the actual last api-gateway change), not
`ce519d42` (the merge that tripped this the second time).

**Not resolved on the first pass — caught by PR #291's own real audit before
merge, same day.** Two BLOCKs: (1) the resolver's first version used plain
`git log <sha> -- <paths>`, which applies git's default history
simplification and can silently skip a real merge commit for a side-branch
commit that was never `main`'s pushed head — reproduced against 4 real merge
commits in this repo's own history; fixed with `--first-parent`, now covered
by a self-test case that builds a real `git merge --no-ff` commit rather than
only a linear history. (2) `CLAIMS.jsonl:154` and
[ADR 0097](decisions/0097-the-gateway-says-which-build-it-is.md)'s Decision
section both stated the pre-fix invariant ("compares against the merged
sha") as still true after this PR changed it — the claim's own `verify`
command kept passing because it only grepped for the substring `head_sha`
anywhere in the job, which simply moved to a new step. Both corrected (ADR
0097 gets a `## Correction` section, matching this repo's established
convention rather than silently editing the original text); the live-vs.
hand-mirrored watch-path fork the resolver's own docstring names is filed as
[OD-122](decisions/OPEN-DECISIONS.md), not defaulted.

**44.2d — [ADR 0097](decisions/0097-the-gateway-says-which-build-it-is.md)'s
Stage 2 still reports a CORRECT production state as a deploy failure, in two
further shapes. 🔴 OPEN, measured 2026-09-06, one of them
confirmed LIVE against production.** 44.2c fixed the watch-path half. The next is a
*later* merge landing while Stage 2 is still polling: Railway rebuilds to that
newer commit, so the running build becomes a **descendant** of the audited sha
rather than the resolved watched commit, and the strict `running == resolved`
comparison calls it a MISMATCH. `poll()` returns its *last* verdict, so a race
that starts mid-poll decides the whole run.

Measured by replaying every `running` sha the three historical runs actually
reported, through `resolve_watched_commit.py` → `check_deployed_sha.verdict` as
they stand on `main` today:

| run | merged | running | 44.2c's verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 33706023515 (#238) att 1–35 | `3f796269` | `bc0b1498` (watched ancestor) | MATCH ✅ |
| 33706023515 (#238) **att 36–60** | `3f796269` | `671c647d` (**a later merge**) | **MISMATCH 🔴** |
| 33704594104 (#235) att 1–16 | `01841db9` | `07698dee` (watched ancestor) | MATCH ✅ |
| 33704594104 (#235) **att 17–61** | `01841db9` | `bc0b1498` (**a later merge**) | **MISMATCH 🔴** |
| 33699054120 att 1–19 | `e08a6aaa` | `bc2f570e` (watched ancestor) | MATCH ✅ |
| 33699054120 **att 20–61** | `e08a6aaa` | `ca964189` (**a later merge**) | **MISMATCH 🔴** |
| control: `bc0b1498` merged, `07698dee` serving | — | — | MISMATCH ✅ correct |

So **all three runs would still fail**: each one's *final* attempts saw a
superseded build — 25, 45 and 42 of them respectively — and that last verdict is
what the step returns. 44.2c is a real fix for the shape it names; it is not the
whole defect.

**A correction is on the record here, and it is this section's own subject one
level up.** The first version of this table listed run 33706023515 as passing and
concluded "two of the three." That run reported **two** running shas, not one —
`bc0b1498` for attempts 1–35 and `671c647d` (PR #280, a later merge touching 18
watched files) for 36–60 — and the sentence above the table claimed to have
replayed *every* `running` sha the three runs reported. It had not. Caught by
this PR's own pre-merge audit, correctness angle, before merge. A document whose
entire deliverable is a measured replay cannot assert "every" and drop the one
observation that changes its own headline count; the omission understated the
defect it was filing, which is the direction that gets a defect deprioritised.

This is not exotic here. `main` is `strict: true` and three or four sessions
merge concurrently ([[merge-races-need-sequencing]]), so a second merge inside a
600-second poll is ordinary traffic.

→ **The invariant that closes it** — worked out and measured under PR #282, which
was closed rather than landed because it overlapped 44.2c's implementation:
accept `FLOOR ⪯ running ⪯ tip(main)`, where `FLOOR` is what
`resolve_watched_commit.py` already returns. `running == FLOOR` is the current
check; the added upper bound admits a descendant that is genuinely on `main` and
nothing else. It does **not** weaken the failure the step exists for — a build
older than `FLOOR` is still a MISMATCH, which is the deploy that did not land.
Report the two passes distinguishably (the resolved build vs. a superseded one)
rather than flattening them into one boolean; #282's closing comment carries the
full replay, and commit **`c4f0df8b`** (branch `claude/serene-goldstine-d57f87`,
PR closed) carries a working implementation with a 32-case self-test if someone
wants the code rather than the rule. The commit is the durable reference —
§4's retirement convention is a recovery *commit*, because a branch is
deletable by anyone at any time.

**LIVE INSTANCE, and it is a THIRD shape — #291's own merge failed its own
audit.** Measured 2026-09-06T13:44Z, run
[34037029134](https://github.com/aldemirkonuk/RestaurantAIAutomation/actions/runs/34037029134),
auditing `b664c1bd` — the commit that landed 44.2c:

```
Merged: b664c1bd
api-gateway's watched paths were last touched at: 39fea7b2
attempt 1..60: MISMATCH — a DIFFERENT build is serving:
               running 9a0c00d0, merged 39fea7b2
```

Production at that moment, asked directly:

```
GET /api/v1/health/live   200  {"commit":"9a0c00d0…","bootedAt":"2026-09-06T12:54:33.693Z"}
GET /api/v1/health/ready  200  {"injector":"resolved","supabaseClient":"initialised",
                                "database":"reachable"}
```

The gateway is **up, ready, and reachable to its database**, and `9a0c00d0` is on
`main` and is a **descendant** of `FLOOR` — so no build was missed. What breaks
the equality is that `9a0c00d0` (#308) is a commit that touches **no watched
path**, sitting one commit *after* `FLOOR` `39fea7b2` (#310). The running build's
sha is therefore newer than `FLOOR` without being a watched commit at all.

`resolve_watched_commit.py` assumes the running sha is always the newest watched
commit. It is not. **Why Railway labelled the deployment `9a0c00d0` was not
determined from here** — push-head labelling, a restart re-tagging against the
latest push, or a cached build are all consistent with what is observable, and
this repo has no Railway project token in CI to ask (the same limitation OD-122
names). That mechanism is unknown; the *invariant* is not:

    FLOOR ⪯ running ⪯ tip(main)      holds for every observation above
    running == FLOOR                 does not

which is the same fix 44.2d already names, arrived at from a second direction.

So the count of shapes this step gets wrong is **three**, not one: the watched
ancestor (fixed by 44.2c), the mid-poll supersession (above), and this one. All
three are the same error — treating a *point* as the answer where the truth is a
*range*.

→ **If you are staring at a RED deploy audit right now, do not assume it is
this.** Filing a false failure as known is only safe if a real one is still
separable from it, so here is the separation, three commands:

```sh
curl -s "$API_GATEWAY_URL/api/v1/health/live"          # -> the running sha
git merge-base --is-ancestor $FLOOR    $running        # false => a REAL failure
git merge-base --is-ancestor $running  origin/main     # false => a REAL failure
```

`$FLOOR` is what the audit log prints as *"watched paths were last touched at"*.
**Both** must be true for the RED to be 44.2d. If the running build is not a
descendant of `FLOOR`, the build for `FLOOR` never landed — that is the deploy
failure this step exists to catch, and it is not this section. If it is not on
`main` at all, something unmerged is serving, which is worse. Also read
`/api/v1/health/ready`: 44.2d has only ever been observed with a **200 and
`database: reachable`**.

→ **Why it is filed here and not as an OD:** this is an unfixed defect with a
known fix, not an undecided fork. The genuinely open fork in this area is
OD-122 (hand-mirrored watch paths), already filed.

## Track B — Foundations

### 44.3 Schema truth

**44.3a — Capture 13 ghost tables** (live in DB, in no migration):
`_migrations`, `conversation_embeddings`, `enrichment_queue`,
`event_dead_letters`, `event_replay_jobs`, `event_schema_registry`,
`inventory_events`, `keyboard_shortcuts`, `manager_preferences`,
`negotiation_facts`, `restaurant_providers`, `vendor_promotions`, `wine_aliases`.
A fresh environment lacks all of them, so dependent code works in production and
fails locally — this blocks onboarding any new developer or environment. Read each
real definition from `information_schema` + `pg_constraint`; **never guess from the
code that uses it**, which is how the drift arrived. `_migrations` is likely a
droppable leftover.

**44.3b — Make `approveDraft` atomic.** Order status, shadow-stock reservation and
calendar event are split across `approveOrder()` and `approveDraft()` with no
shared transaction. Safe today, but a partial failure leaves order APPROVED + email
sent + conversation stuck PENDING_APPROVAL, and it contradicts Phase 34's
"atomically" wording.

**44.3c — Two informational schema gaps.** `pos_webhook_logs` missing
`restaurant_id` (complicates synthetic-tenant teardown); `scheduled_reminders`
exists but CalendarAgent never writes it.

**44.3d — Adopt the rule: read the live schema, not migrations.** v2.0 produced
three wrong assumptions from migration files: `procurement_order_items` in no
migration; `procurement_orders.wine_name` in a migration and not in the DB;
`public.users` keyed on `user_id` so `REFERENCES users(id)` fails outright. Also
`procurement_order_items.total_bottles` is `GENERATED ALWAYS` and rejects explicit
inserts — mentioned nowhere. `npm run db:drift` checks state.

### 44.4 Verification and tracking reconciliation

Not code — but it is why the v2.0 audit could not tell finished from unfinished.

| Item | Phases |
|---|---|
| No `VERIFICATION.md` despite passing UAT/VALIDATION | 18, 19, 20, 22, 28, 35 |
| Stale `VALIDATION` frontmatter (`nyquist_compliant:false`) superseded by VERIFICATION | 25, 36 |
| Prose-only `VALIDATION`, no locked frontmatter | 26, 32, 34 |
| ROADMAP claims UAT passed; the UAT file reads `partial`/`[pending]` | 27 (5 tests), 30 (4 tests) |
| REQUIREMENTS checkboxes `[ ]` despite VERIFICATION marking SATISFIED | 18, 19, 20, 21, 25 |
| ~100 ROADMAP-referenced REQ-IDs never entered REQUIREMENTS.md | 23, 24, 26–35 |
| ~~Duplicate files in Phase 24~~ — **NOT duplicates.** `24-03-PLAN 2.md` was 0 bytes (deleted); `24-03-SUMMARY 2.md` was the **only** summary for plan 24-03 — `24-03-SUMMARY.md` did not exist — and is now renamed into place. Deleting both as "housekeeping" would have destroyed the plan's sole completion record. ✅ 2026-07-31 | 24 |
| Quick tasks with no terminal status | `260401-x24`, `260402-kj0`, `260403-dgf`, `260502-00z`, `260502-06m` |

**Phase 33 drift — ✅ RESOLVED 2026-07-28. It was pure drift; zero work remained.**

Verified against the running system rather than the SUMMARY files: migration
`20260514200000` is applied to production with 11 columns, 2 RLS policies and **7
real rows** (an applied migration over an empty table would mean the backfill never
ran); `MembersController` is **registered** in `restaurants.module.ts` and its
routes answer **401, not 404**; `InviteLanding`/`NoAccess` are **routed** in
`App.tsx`; `services/api/team.ts` calls the same endpoint shapes the controller
declares, so client and server agree.

Five plans of apparent work turned out to be none. `33-VERIFICATION.md` written,
ROADMAP checkboxes corrected.

**The lesson generalises to the six phases still listed above:** a phase with
working code and no VERIFICATION artifact is indistinguishable from one that was
never started, and that ambiguity nearly cost a full re-plan of finished work. Each
of the three wiring checks above (registered / routed / 401-not-404) is one this
codebase has failed somewhere in the last two days.

**Still with teeth:**
- **Phase 30 iCal** — code scored 10/10 but **no external calendar client has ever
  confirmed the feed subscribes** in Outlook/Apple/Google. The thing users actually
  do is untested.

Phase 20 is the strongest-evidenced phase in the milestone (74 automated tests,
18/18 security closed, 2× UAT) missing only the top-level artifact — cheapest close.

### 44.6 Auth-context placeholders

`ManualOverrideModal.tsx:112-113` hardcodes `managerId:'MGR_001'`,
`managerName:'Current Manager'` — an override ledger where every entry names the
same fake manager is not an audit trail, which is the entire point of the record.
`one-tap-actions.service.ts:383,388,395` leave the three action bodies
unimplemented (reorder workflow, inventory update, price update) — the actions the
feature is named for. Pair with 44.1a, same controller.

**44.6b — persist the override audit trail — ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-05, ALREADY BUILT.**

No table needed, no decision needed. I was about to build a sibling of
`override_events` when checking the live page first showed the work was done.

The live `/inventory` route serves `InventoryCommandPage`. Its manual adjust
(`RowExpansion.tsx:85`) calls `reconcileItem` →
`POST inventory/:inventoryId/reconcile` →
`inventory-ledger.service.ts#reconcileInventory` → the `apply_stock_movement`
RPC, which writes `inventory_lots` and the `inventory_transactions` ledger
atomically with `performed_by` and `reason` (baseline migration lines 281, 338,
341). The actor comes from `@CurrentUser()`, i.e. the JWT, not the client.

So a manual adjustment on the live page already records **who, what, why and
when**, through the one sanctioned write primitive.

The unpersisted modal is `ManualOverrideModal` in `pages/Inventory.tsx`, which is
routed at `/inventory-legacy` (`App.tsx:202`) — and **nothing in the app links
there**. Grep returns the route definition and nothing else. Its
`handleManualOverride` does persist the stock numbers via `updateInventoryItem`,
but drops reason/category/notes/actor into local state and a client-side event.

**This is the third time a task in this register described the wrong page** (see
the UX catalogue's L136 note, and 44.5's InvoiceScannerModal). The register was
compiled against `Inventory.tsx` because that file is named `Inventory`. Checking
the route before the filename would have caught all three.

Follow-up, not urgent: `pages/Inventory.tsx` and `ManualOverrideModal.tsx` are now
dead weight shipping in the bundle, the same case 44.5 retired
`InvoiceScannerModal` for. Deleting them is a separate cleanup — confirm nobody is
using `/inventory-legacy` as a manual fallback first.

✅ **Done 2026-08-26.** `/inventory-legacy` retired: route, `pages/Inventory.tsx`
(1,928 ln) and `components/inventory/ManualOverrideModal.tsx` (356 ln) deleted after
the page's exclusive capabilities were ported onto `/inventory`. The modal itself was
**not** ported, for the reason this entry gives: `/inventory`'s manual adjust already
records who/what/why/when through `reconcileItem`, the sanctioned write primitive.
See [ADR 0019](decisions/0019-p2-build-scope.md) §B-parity.

| `INVENTORY_SOTA_PLAN.md` (362 ln) | "Phase 0 pending" | ❌ **Phase 1 partly live.** `/inventory` serves `InventoryCommandPage`; the page this plan describes was parked at `/inventory-legacy` and is **deleted** (2026-08-26, ADR 0019 §B-parity) | Phases 2–3 (§6, §7). Phase 0's ground-truth check is still worth running — against the *new* page |
| `PROSPECTS_ATTRIBUTION_ARCHITECTURE.md` (204 ln) | "Approved (2026-07-08)" | ✅ **Built, wired, dormant by config.** Both controllers registered `orchestrator.module.ts:25-26`. Gated on `INBOUND_EMAIL_DOMAIN`; unset ⇒ safe no-op | **Nothing to build.** Ops task: domain + MX + 3 env vars |
| `SYNTHETIC_DATA_AND_DOCS_PLAN.md` (175 ln) | "in progress / uncommitted" | ❌ **Shipped 2026-07-29** and committed. My register entry was stale within days | Three deliberate gaps, below |

**Why four headers were stale:** each was written when the plan was written and never
touched again. A plan's status line ages the moment work starts, and nothing in the
workflow updates it — the author is in the code, not the planning doc. The
`ANALYTICS_FEATURE_CATALOG` case is the clearest: someone appended Batch 6 on
2026-07-21 *without noticing* the engine shipped that same day, because appending a
batch doesn't require reading the header.

**Genuinely outstanding, by plan:**

1. `ANALYTICS_FEATURE_CATALOG` — unbuilt batches, count unknown until reconciled against
   `insight-catalog.ts`. **Do that reconciliation before quoting any number from this file.**
2. `INBOUND_EMAIL_INTELLIGENCE_PLAN` — Phases 1+.
3. `INVENTORY_SOTA_PLAN` — Phases 2–3 (1–2 quarters and 2–3 quarters by its own estimates).
4. `PROSPECTS_ATTRIBUTION` — ops only. Plus one hardening: the webhook accepts its shared
   secret as `?secret=` as well as the `x-inbound-secret` header, putting it in access
   logs. The endpoint is otherwise fail-closed (no secret configured ⇒ refuses everything).
5. `SYNTHETIC_DATA_AND_DOCS_PLAN` §6 "not done, deliberately" — (a) the loop has never run
   end to end; both ingresses are dry-runnable but payload shapes are verified *by reading
   the receiving code*, so "depletion triggers a reorder" remains unobserved; (b) packing
   slips, delivery receipts and credit memos are modelled but only the invoice renders;
   (c) a 35% detection gap means 65% of simulated wine sales land as food until
   `pos_item_mappings` is seeded — this blocks any meaningful analytics run on a fresh tenant.

Item 5(a) is the same work as Track C's SimPOS end-to-end run. **Do not schedule them twice.**

## 44.5 correction — InvoiceScannerModal was orphaned after all

An earlier pass in this register claimed `pages/Inventory.tsx` imports
`InvoiceScannerModal`, so it was left in place as "reachable, needs the legacy page
handled first". **That was wrong.** The grep that produced it matched the local state
variable `_showInvoiceScannerModal` — a substring — not an import. Checked precisely:
nothing imports the component, it is never rendered, and its setter is never called.
The underscore prefix was this codebase's own marker for intentionally-unused, and I
read past it.

Deleted (487 lines) along with the dead `useState` that was its only remaining trace.
`/inventory-legacy` is also linked from nowhere but its own route definition — worth
retiring, tracked separately. ✅ **Retired 2026-08-26** (ADR 0019 §B-parity).

Worth recording because the mistake is the one this whole phase is about: a
substring match reported as a dependency, believed, and written into a planning
document as fact.


## 44.15 — UX catalogue audited 2026-07-31: 4 of 6 sampled entries were stale

Sampled 6 of the 15 ❌ "dead button" entries against current code. **Four were
stale or partly stale** — the catalogue describes shipped features as missing:

- **L173** Recommendations "entirely read-only" — stale; `recommendation-actions.service.ts`
  and migration `20260720120000` both exist.
- **L151** `ActiveConversationsPanel` "unreachable" — stale; `Orders.tsx:1512` sets
  its open state.
- **L253** Login "no OAuth buttons" — partly stale; OAuth/One Tap shipped, but
  "Remember me" is genuinely unbound and `/forgot-password` genuinely routes
  nowhere.
- **L187** Promotions "non-interactive" — partly stale; `onDismiss` is wired.
- **L162** menu-scan batch-add "doesn't persist" — likely stale, work in flight.
- **L102** dashboard profile card — could not locate; unverified.

The remaining 9 ❌ and all 9 ⚠️ entries are **unverified, not confirmed**.

**The structural problem, which outlives these six lines:** the catalogue has no
per-entry date. It is a point-in-time snapshot in which every line reads as
current, and it was cited as a 16-item backlog by both the v2.0 audit and this
register. A banner is now at the top of the file; entries should carry a verified
date when re-checked.

This is the same failure as the Phase 27 UAT claim and the Phase 33 checkboxes:
a document that was true once, never re-dated, and later read as fact.


## Data integrity — `api_spend.restaurant_id` holds wine UUIDs (historical rows) — added 2026-08-24

Four Python call sites passed `restaurant_id=wine_id` (`jobs/web_verify_tasks.py`,
`jobs/score_tasks.py` ×2, `services/haiku_enrichment_service.py`). **Writers fixed
2026-08-24 on `feat/p1-nf-instrumentation`** (wine_id now rides in NF `context`),
but every pre-fix `api_spend` row for providers `serper` and the enrichment Haiku
still carries a wine UUID in the `restaurant_id` column — wine ids are valid UUIDs,
so nothing in the schema can distinguish them. Any per-restaurant spend query over
historical data is polluted for those providers. Remediation (unowned): null the
column where the value joins `master_wine_library*` ids, or cut the query window at
