## CI — `Lint TypeScript` is red for **two** errors, not one; turbo hides the second — added 2026-08-24

`ci.yml`'s `lint-typescript` runs `pnpm run lint` → `turbo run lint` without
`--continue`. When `@wineops/web#lint` fails, turbo stops scheduling and kills the
in-flight tasks, so CI's log ends at `Tasks: 3 successful, 5 total` and only ever
showed the web error. `@wineops/api-gateway#lint` had already started (`cache miss,
executing`) and was cut off mid-run — its own error never reached the log.

1. **`apps/web/src/lib/devAuthBypass.ts:43` — fixed** on `fix/web-lint-unused-disable`.
   `Unused eslint-disable directive`. `no-console` is `'off'` repo-wide
   (`.eslintrc.cjs:33`, no `allow` list, no apps/web override), so the directive was
   dead; only apps/web's lint script passes `--report-unused-disable-directives`
   (`apps/web/package.json:12`), which is why only this one was reported. The other
   four `no-console` directives in the repo (`apps/api-gateway/src/openapi.ts` ×2,
   `apps/mobile/src/guidance/analytics.ts`, `apps/mobile/src/lib/mmkv.ts`) are equally
   dead but unreported. Deleting was right — turning `no-console` on for apps/web
   would hit 243 call sites and is a policy decision, not a lint fix.

2. **`apps/api-gateway/src/wines/wine-submissions.service.ts:78` — fixed** on the
   same branch. `no-misleading-character-class` (error, from `eslint:recommended`):
   *Unexpected combined character in character class*, on the `DIACRITICS` regex.
   Verified pre-existing on pristine `main` source, not an artefact of that script's
   `--fix`. Cause: adjacent combining-mark **ranges** — `ͯ` (end of range 1) sits next
   to `᪰` (start of range 2) in pattern order and the pair segments as one grapheme.
   Probed on ESLint 8.57.1: each range alone is clean, any two adjacent ones error;
   `\u`-escaping alone does **not** silence it (the `allowEscape` option is ESLint
   9.7+), and neither does the `u` flag. Rewritten as `\u`-escaped, `|`-alternated
   single-range classes in the original order — which also ends the "unreadable
   source" problem the rule is actually about. Equivalence proven, not assumed: the
   old and new patterns were compared over every code point U+0000–U+FFFF (both are
   non-`u` regexes, so that is the whole domain) — 364 code points matched, 0
   disagreements — and `wine-submissions.service.spec.ts`, which asserts parity with
   `public.wine_normalize_text`, is green (27 passed; the 11-test integration spec
   beside it skips without DB credentials).

**After both:** `pnpm run lint` is 0 errors across all 5 packages (198 warnings —
131 web + 67 api-gateway — all pre-existing and untouched).


## Tooling blind spot — `.gitignore` hides macOS `" 2"` duplicate files from every `git status` sweep — added 2026-08-24

`.gitignore:71-73` carries `# Accidental duplicate filenames (editor copies)` /
`* 2.py` / `* 2.md`. These files are therefore **ignored, not untracked**, so
`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` does not list them — `--untracked-files`
never reports ignored paths. Any audit built on `git status` reports a clean tree
while the duplicates sit on disk. Finding them requires `--ignored=matching` or a
filesystem walk:

```bash
find . \( -name node_modules -o -name .git -o -name venv -o -name __pycache__ \) -prune \
  -o -type f -regex '.* [0-9]+\.[A-Za-z0-9]+$' -print
```

The rule was added `1f96a78` (2026-04-13), one day after both strays below were
written (Apr 12); they stayed hidden ~4 months. Whatever the intent, the effect was
to silence the symptom rather than remove the files.

**Two Python strays found and deleted 2026-08-24** (both Apr 12, vs Jul 8 for their
tracked counterparts — one duplication event):

1. `services/agent-orchestrator/tests/test_golden_path_e2e 2.py`. Compared by AST,
   not by eye: across all 24 top-level statements the only difference from the
   tracked file was an unused `patch` import — all 4 fixtures and all 5 test bodies
   were identical, the rest of the diff being `black` reformatting. `python_files =
   test_*.py` (`pytest.ini`) matches a name with a space, so pytest collected the
   five `test_e2e_*` cases **twice**: 840 locally vs 835 from a clean checkout of the
   same branch. Note this was double-execution, **not** untested coverage — the same
   five bodies were always collected from the tracked file, so CI did run them.
   After deletion: 835 collected, `test_golden_path_e2e.py` 5/5 green.
2. `services/agent-orchestrator/api/pos_routes 2.py`. The pre-refactor Toast-only
   router (`POST /webhook/toast`, `process_toast_webhook`), superseded by the generic
   provider-registry version in the tracked `pos_routes.py` (`/webhook/{provider}`,
   adapter lookup, `process_pos_event`). Inert at runtime — a module name containing
   a space is not importable, and it is not a `test_*.py` — so the cost was grep
   pollution: a plausible-looking file describing a routing architecture that no
   longer exists.

**Left in place:** `.vercel/project 2.json` (local Vercel link metadata, under the
already-ignored `.vercel/`, no bearing on the repo).

**Open, unowned — the ignore rules are narrower than the problem.** Only `* 2.py`
and `* 2.md` are named. A `* 3.py` (Finder's next collision), or any `* 2.ts`,
`* 2.tsx`, `* 2.json`, `* 2.sql`, is neither ignored nor flagged — it surfaces as an
ordinary untracked file and would be swept in by `git add -A`. That is arguably worse
than the hidden case: a duplicate that gets **committed**. Two directions, not yet
decided: widen the ignore globs (keeps hiding them, keeps the audit blind spot), or
drop the rules entirely so duplicates show up in `git status` and get deleted on
sight. The second matches how these files actually arise — they are never wanted.


## Security — Google sign-in self-provisioned a manager of a real tenant — CLOSED 2026-09-01

Found while preparing to put the app on a public branded domain
(`mudavym.com`), which would have advertised it. **Verified against
production, not reasoned:**

- `POST /api/v1/auth/oauth/google` carries no guard — probed live, it answers
  the service's own `"Failed to verify Google token"` (401), not a guard
  rejection, so it is reachable by anyone.
- `verifyGoogleToken` checks only the token audience and `email_verified`.
  There is no domain restriction or allowlist.
- `findOrCreateOAuthUser` then INSERTed any unknown address as
  `role: "manager"` with `restaurant_id = DEFAULT_RESTAURANT_ID`.
- `DEFAULT_RESTAURANT_ID` **is set** in Railway production, and resolved to
  a live tenant holding real inventory — not a demo row.

Net effect: anyone on the internet holding any Google account could mint
themselves a manager of a real restaurant. **No account had come in that way**
— the only `oauth_provider = 'google'` row belonged to a different tenant and
predated it — so this was a latent hole, not an incident.

**Fixed** (`fix/oauth-no-self-provision`): the create branch is removed
outright rather than re-gated on the env var — an unset variable was the only
thing standing between the public internet and a manager role, and a stray
value in a future environment must not be able to reopen it. OAuth sign-in now
only ever resolves an existing account; registration is where a restaurant is
created or an invite redeemed. The same change normalises the email before
lookup (`users.email` is UNIQUE and case-sensitive, so a mixed-case stored
address against Google's lower-cased claim used to miss the row and fall
through into the create branch — the same defect wearing a different hat).
Five tests added to `identity-first-signin.spec.ts`; four were observed
FAILING against the reverted implementation before being kept.

**Open, and it is a product decision, not a repair:** whether Google
self-signup should exist at all in some gated form (invite-only, domain
allowlist, or provisioning into a sandbox tenant at a low-privilege role).
Today the answer is simply "no self-signup".

~~Also open and separate: `findOrCreateOAuthUser` signs an existing account in
on an email match **without writing a `user_oauth_accounts` row**, so
`resolveSignInMethods` keeps reporting that identity as password-only while
Google demonstrably works for it — an ADR 0024 truthfulness gap pointed the
other way.~~ **CLOSED 2026-09-12 by ADR 0139** (PR #357) — **and closed by the
opposite arm, which is why the sentence above is struck rather than ticked.**
The gap was real: signing in on an address match while writing no row left
`resolveSignInMethods` telling a user "password only" about an identity that
demonstrably had a working Google flow. Two repairs were available: write the
missing row on sign-in, or stop signing in without one. ADR 0139 took the
second, because the first would have persisted a link off the back of a check
that had already been passed by address alone — the very thing that made the
Microsoft endpoint a takeover (`verifyMicrosoftToken` replayed the body string
to Graph and trusted the address it returned). **After that PR, an OAuth
sign-in returns an account only when `user_oauth_accounts` has a row for
`(user_id, provider)` — or, with zero rows, a legacy `users.oauth_provider`
pair that is BOUND by a matching `users.oauth_id`.** So `resolveSignInMethods`
and the sign-in path now read the same fact and cannot disagree: an identity
reported as password-only IS password-only, because Google would refuse it.

The population this entry described is empty either way. Measured read-only on
production 2026-09-12: 8 users, all 8 with a password hash, 1 row in
`user_oauth_accounts` (google), 0 Microsoft links, 1 user with the legacy
column — and that user is the same user who holds the link row, so no account
reaches the legacy branch at all.

**Continuity with the closure above (2026-09-01, PR #179).** That fix removed
the auto-create and left the resolve-by-email underneath it untouched; ADR 0139
removed the resolve-by-email. Read together: #179 stopped OAuth from CREATING
an account, #357 stopped it from RESOLVING one it has no link to. Two further
things #179's entry did not have to say, now true: the Microsoft endpoint
verifies an ID token itself (RS256 against the published JWKS, `aud` equal to
`MICROSOFT_CLIENT_ID`, exact `iss`, `ver`/`scp`, `xms_edov`) instead of
replaying a token to Graph, and Google's audience check no longer disappears
when `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` is unset. Still open from #179 and untouched by #357:
whether gated Google self-signup should exist at all.

## Convention — `toLocaleDateString` on wire timestamps needs explicit `timeZone: "UTC"` — added 2026-08-31

`studio-invite.controller.ts:98` formatted `data.expires_at` (a UTC ISO wire
timestamp) with `toLocaleDateString("en-US", {month, day, year})` and no
`timeZone`, so a UTC-midnight expiry rendered as the **prior day** on any
server west of UTC — and `studio-invite.controller.spec.ts` only caught it
because CI runs in UTC (`TZ=America/New_York npx jest
src/common/orchestrator/studio-invite.controller.spec.ts` failed before the
fix, passes after). **CLOSED 2026-08-31** (`fix/studio-invite-expiry-tz`):
added `timeZone: "UTC"` to the options object; full gateway jest suite green
(109/110 suites, 1 pre-existing skip, 1363/1374 tests).

~~**Open, unowned — same bug class found in a second file.**~~ **CLOSED
2026-08-31** (`fix/email-template-date-tz`, #175 — merged hours after the
entry above, which is why the paragraph shipped reading "open"). The open
question it named — whether every call site feeds a UTC wire timestamp or an
already-local `Date` — was answered by tracing them, and the answer was
**both**, which is why the fix is not a blanket `timeZone: "UTC"`:

- `scheduled-tasks.service.ts` passes DB-column ISO strings
  (`scheduledDate`/`expectedDate`/`dueDate`/`eventDate`) — genuine wire
  timestamps that need the UTC pin.
- The same service's inventory-audit reminder builds its date with
  `new Date(); .setDate(+2)` — local-time calendar arithmetic for "the
  upcoming Wednesday" in the server's own clock. Forcing UTC there would
  re-derive a different calendar day on any host whose process TZ is not
  already UTC: the opposite bug, introduced by too broad a fix.

So `formatDate`/`formatShortDate` now branch on input type — a `string` is a
wire timestamp and is pinned to UTC, a `Date` object is left in local time —
with `template-config.spec.ts` pinning both halves, including a test that
fails if someone later "simplifies" it to an unconditional UTC option.

**The convention this section names still stands** for new code:
`toLocaleDateString` on a wire timestamp needs an explicit
`timeZone: "UTC"`, and a spec that only runs in UTC CI cannot catch its
absence — pin the suite's TZ (as `apps/web` now does in
`src/__tests__/setup.ts`) or the assertion is vacuous.

## Formatting — `apps/web` is outside Prettier, and only the gateway is inside it — added 2026-08-27

Found while shipping `GET /ask-ai/candidates`, and it is not a partial drift —
measured 2026-08-27, `npx prettier --check "src/**/*.{ts,tsx}"` in `apps/web` reports
**531 of 534** files unformatted. In the Ask AI directory alone it is 10 of 10, five
of which (`AskAiSurface.tsx`, `AskAiBar.tsx`, `events.ts`, `page-context.ts`,
`page-context.test.ts`) no in-flight branch has touched. So this is not one branch's
mess: the web app has never been Prettier-formatted, and nothing asks it to be.

The 3 files that *do* pass are almost certainly incidental — short enough to already
match — not evidence of a formatted subset.

The asymmetry is in `.eslintrc.cjs`. The `apps/api-gateway/**/*.ts` override extends
`prettier` and loads the `prettier` **plugin**, so a formatting deviation there is a
lint error and CI catches it. The `apps/web/**/*.{ts,tsx}` override does neither —
it carries `react-hooks`, `react-refresh` and `jsx-a11y` only. `apps/web/package.json`
has no `format` script either (`api-gateway` has `"format": "prettier --write ..."`).
Nothing is broken and CI is not red: `pnpm run lint` is green because the web app is
simply not being asked the question.

**Deliberately not fixed 2026-08-27 (founder call).** Formatting the web app is a
whole-tree diff across every `.tsx` in it, which would bury the reviewable content of
whatever branch carried it and collide with every open PR. It is queued to be taken
as its own operation.

Two directions when it is taken, not yet decided:

1. **Adopt Prettier for `apps/web` in one commit** — add the `prettier` extends +
   plugin to the web override, add a `format` script, run it once, land the whole-tree
   diff on a branch that does nothing else. Cheapest to reason about afterwards;
   worst possible merge conflict surface on the day.
2. **Ratchet it, the way OD-105 ratcheted `label-has-associated-control`** — turn the
   rule on, allowlist the existing files, delete a line when a file is touched. No
   big-bang diff, and the mechanism is already proven in this repo (47 files on the
   OD-105 allowlist). Slower, and leaves a second allowlist to maintain.

Until then the rule for anyone touching `apps/web`: **do not run Prettier on it.**
Matching the surrounding style by hand is correct; reformatting a file you edited
produces a diff nobody can review.

## UI — `HoursHeatmap` ramp inverts salience on the dark ground — added 2026-08-31

`apps/web/src/pages/inventory/command/bits.tsx` (`HEAT_SHADES`) anchors its ramp
on near-white `#F1F7F8` for QUIET hours, so on Warm Charcoal the quiet cells
glow brightest and salience inverts; its labels are `text-gray-400` on a page
that has İznik-era dark grounds. Found by session -9e's cross-page pass on the
P3 wave (pre-existing, outside the wave's diff). Fix direction, token-true on
both grounds: paint cells as `var(--seal)` at graduated opacity over the page
ground (color-mix or rgba), which also retires the ramp's raw hexes; labels →
ink-4. Owner: the inventory re-skin pass.

page. Recorded in providers.md §9.

## A11y — the honesty idiom's em dash is silent to assistive tech; sheets lack focus traps — added 2026-08-31

Two wave-level findings from the Opus honesty review, systemic rather than
per-page: (1) the em-dash "unknown" glyph carries no accessible semantics — a
screen reader reads punctuation or nothing, so the pages' central honesty
device does not reach AT users; candidate fix: an `aria-label="unknown"` span
component in lib/mudavym (one place, every page). (2) TwinSheet and
TemplateSheet manage Escape and initial focus but neither traps Tab nor
restores focus on close. Both need a foundation-level fix, not five copies.

## Tests — the document edit route and targeted-notification opts have no service-level specs — added 2026-08-31

`editLine` (document-intake.service.ts) is covered only by a pure applyTieOut
cast spec — the TOCTOU restore path, the qty/lineTotal precedence and the
whitelist guards have no test; `persistForRestaurant`'s `onlyUserIds` and the
narrowed socket emit have none either (Opus correctness review). Both need a
mocked-client spec harness; the registry's readBy guard
(scripts/check_flag_readby_anchors.py, CI-wired) covers only the anchors.

## Analytics — four defects found while chartering domain quant, adversarially verified — added 2026-08-31

> **Partly superseded — read this first.** Defects **1 and 2 are CLOSED** (2026-09-01);
> see the two `CLOSED 2026-09-01` sections below, which carry the fixes, the judgement
> calls, and the regression tests. Defect **3 is CLOSED** (2026-09-02) — see the
> `CLOSED 2026-09-02` section below and
> [[0064-a-fitted-value-is-a-prediction-not-a-memory]]. **Defect 4 remains open** (the
> identically-zero `maxDrawdown`). This banner exists because CLAIMS.jsonl cannot see
> prose: a fixed-but-unstruck entry is itself a defect (CLAUDE.md §5b), and leaving all
> four reading as open would have been exactly that.

Found by a six-pass research sweep for [ADR 0048](decisions/0048-domain-quant-under-research-math.md)
(five parallel finders + one dedicated adversarial verification pass). **Only findings
that survived the adversary are listed.** Six other claims from the same sweep were
killed or downgraded and are recorded in ADR 0048 §"What the adversary killed" —
including a "margin math is inverted" claim and a "12× invoice under-receipt" claim,
both of which were reported to the founder before verification and are wrong as stated.

**1. `getVendorScorecard` and `getCashflow` return empty for every restaurant, always
— and `getCashflow` reaches the UI.** `advanced-analytics.service.ts:100-110` selects
`provider_name` / `wine_name`, columns that do not exist on `procurement_orders`; the
error is swallowed to `[]`. Same bug shape as the dead purchasing family in
`insight-generator.service.ts:227`. This is the most serious of the four because it is
**rendered**: `getCashflow` feeds the Recommendations surface, so a manager sees an
empty panel that is indistinguishable from "nothing to report".

**2. `deadStockCapital` is the only financial field that reaches the UI, and it does not
measure what it is labelled.** `analytics.service.ts:210-214` defines it as *overstock*
(`qty > max(thresholdMin*3, 12)`) with **no consumption join** — so a fast-moving item
held deep counts as dead capital. `recommendations.service.ts:169` presents it to
managers as capital locked in **slow** inventory. The fix is either the join or the
label; shipping both definitions is the defect.

**3. The forecast "backtest" is an in-sample fit with a look-ahead leak, and the
leakiest model is the only one that runs.** — **CLOSED 2026-09-02, see below.**
`analytics.service.ts:476` scores against
`result.fitted`. In `engine/forecasting.ts:125-140` (`holtWintersAdditive`) `fitted[i]`
is pushed *after* level/trend/seasonal absorb `series[i]`, so it is not the
one-step-ahead `ℓ_{t−1}+b_{t−1}+s_{t−m}`; `holtLinear:74-79` leaks through the updated
`b_t`. Only SES is clean — and `holtWintersAdditive` never returns null against a dense
120-point series, so the milder fallbacks are dead code. **Every accuracy number the
product reports is optimistically biased by construction.**

**4. `maxDrawdown` is identically zero by construction.** `analytics.service.ts:383-388`
builds `levels` as a cumulative sum of non-negative demand; `engine/risk.ts:155-170`
computes drawdown over it. A monotonically non-decreasing series has no drawdown, so the
metric can never be nonzero and reports a clean risk profile for every restaurant.

**Not defects, recorded so they are not re-filed:** `yield_factor ≤ 1` is *correct* —
it is purchase/trim yield, ≤1 by definition (cooking gain is a different quantity whose
column does not exist, which is a gap, not a bug). And the engine test count is 141 in
`analytics/engine`, 149 across `analytics/` — two sweeps counted different scopes and
both were right.

**Context that decides priority:** the four metrics in the killed "margin inversion"
claim have **zero hits** across `apps/web` and `apps/mobile`. Defects 1 and 2 are on a
rendered surface; 3 and 4 are not, but 3 invalidates any forecast claim the product
would make the moment forecasting is wired up — which is Lane A of ADR 0048.

## Corpus — `decisions/0032-vault-cleanup-cut-line.md` has 36 committed conflict-marker lines — added 2026-08-31

`git show origin/main:.planning/decisions/0032-vault-cleanup-cut-line.md` carries **12
unresolved conflict blocks** (36 marker lines) around `:81-97` and `:127+`, four of them
nested — **inside the Tombstone index**, the table whose entire job is making a deletion
recoverable. Nothing catches it: no guard greps for conflict markers in `.planning/`.

`07-reference/INDEX.md` had the same corruption. **Fixed in this pass** — its markers
enclosed *no content* on either side (pure noise from repeated identical merges), which
meets ADR 0032's own "AUTO only when provably zero-loss" bar. 0032's do not: the blocks
contain real tombstone rows, so resolving them needs someone who can say which side is
right, and it is left open deliberately rather than guessed at.

**Suggested guard:** a `check_no_conflict_markers.py` in the house style (0 pass /
1 violation / 2 cannot-check, never vacuous) over `.planning/**/*.md`. Cheap, and this
class of corruption is invisible to every existing check.
---

## Analytics — `procurement_orders.provider_name` never existed — CLOSED 2026-09-01

`AdvancedAnalyticsService.loadOrders` selected `provider_name` **and** `wine_name`,
and `InsightGeneratorService.loadBundle` selected `provider_name`, from a table that
has neither column
(`supabase/migrations/20260805000000_baseline_from_production.sql:4514-4568` — the FKs
are `provider_id → providers(id)` and `inventory_id → restaurant_inventory(id)`).
PostgREST rejects the **whole** query with 42703 on one unknown column, and both call
sites discarded `error` and fell back to `[]`. `getVendorScorecard` and `getCashflow`
therefore returned zeroes for every restaurant since they shipped, the purchasing
insight family never emitted anything, and `getCashflow` feeds the Recommendations
page — where an empty panel is indistinguishable from "nothing to report". Fixed by
reading the vendor label through the FK embed `providers(name)`; `wine_name` was
selected but never read, so it is simply gone.

**Judgement call — the swallow-to-`[]` pattern was kept, not replaced with a throw.**
Measured 2026-09-01: 30 of 46 `.from()` query sites under
`apps/api-gateway/src/analytics` discard `error` this way. Throwing at all 30 is the
sweeping version and it is wrong on the merits, not just on risk: several of those
tables (`restaurant_venue_profiles`, `analytics_insight_prefs`, `analytics_goals`) are
legitimately empty for a restaurant that has not configured them, and a 500 on one
dead lens is worse for a manager than a page that renders the other five. Throwing
would not even have surfaced *this* bug — `RecommendationsService` wraps every lens in
`Promise.allSettled` and maps a rejection to `null` just as silently. The real defect
was **silence**, not resilience. So the loaders now log the PostgREST code and message
at `logger.error` and still degrade to `[]`. Scope held to the two files that carried
the bug (plus `AnalyticsService.loadConsumption`, which the dead-stock fix below now
depends on); the other ~26 sites are open.

Regression guard: `apps/api-gateway/src/analytics/order-schema-drift.spec.ts`. The
Supabase stub is schema-aware — it parses the real column lists out of
`supabase/migrations/` and reproduces PostgREST's all-or-nothing 42703 — so it fails
against any select naming a column that does not exist, not just these two. Verified
failing against the pre-fix tree (4 of 5 cases) before it was made to pass.

## Analytics — `deadStockCapital` measured stock depth, not movement — CLOSED 2026-09-01

`AnalyticsService.getFinancialSummary` defined dead stock as
`qty > max(thresholdMin * 3, 12)` with **no consumption join at all**, while
`recommendations.service.ts` rendered the total to managers as capital "locked in slow
inventory" and advised discounting the top names to cost. A wine that sold out nightly
but was stocked deep counted as dead capital, and that advice applied to a best seller
destroys margin. This is the only financial field that reaches the UI.

**Judgement call — the join, not the label.** Relabelling to "overstock" would make the
*number* honest and leave the *recommendation* attached to it wrong: "build a weekend
flight from the top three idle wines, then discount to cost" is only sound advice for
bottles that are genuinely not selling, and there is no rewrite of that copy that makes
it sound for a fast mover. The action is the thing a manager can act on, so the action
defines which metric belongs under it. Cost was low — `loadConsumption` was already a
method on the same service, and `getInventoryScience` already ran the same join.

Second-order consequence, handled: zero consumption rows must **not** mean "everything
is dead". Against the pre-fix definition a restaurant with no POS feed put its entire
cellar value in front of a manager as idle capital (measured in the spec: $1,700 of
$1,700). `deadStockCapital` is now `null` when no movement is recorded anywhere, per
ADR 0020 — absence of evidence is not evidence of absence — and the recommendation rule
already guards with `(deadStockCapital ?? 0) > 0`, so it stays quiet.

Regression guard: `apps/api-gateway/src/analytics/dead-stock.spec.ts` (5 cases, all
verified failing against the pre-fix tree).

## Analytics — the forecast "backtest" was an in-sample fit — CLOSED 2026-09-02

Closes **defect 3** above. Fixed on `fix/holt-winters-lookahead-leak`; recorded in
[[0064-a-fitted-value-is-a-prediction-not-a-memory]].

`fitted[i]` on every smoother in `engine/forecasting.ts` is now the one-step-ahead
prediction of `series[i]` from `series[0..i-1]` — pushed **before** the state update
rather than after it. Measured as ∂fitted[k]/∂series[k] on a 56-point weekly series
at k=40, pre-fix vs post-fix: `holtWintersAdditive` **0.525 → 0** (the closed form was
α+βα+γ(1−α)), `holtLinear` **0.04 → 0** (α·β), `simpleExponentialSmoothing` 0 → 0.

**Two corrections to the entry above, both verified.** Defect 3 was right that
`holtLinear:74-79` leaked and the brief that reopened it was wrong to call that sibling
correct — it read pre-update *level* but **post-update trend**. And the second consumer
it named, `insight-generator.service.ts:488`, was never leaked: it trains on
`values.slice(0, -7)` and scores `hw.forecast` against the held-out week, a genuine
holdout. Its *comment* said "fitted"; the arithmetic did not. Only
`analytics.service.ts` consumed `fitted`, and `fitted` never leaves the API response.

Every model now returns its own `warmup` (SES 1, Holt 2, Holt-Winters 2·period), and
the accuracy block scores `fitted.slice(warmup)` — because seeding reads the first two
seasons, so those fitted values stay in-sample however the recursion is written
(verified post-fix: ∂fitted[3]/∂series[3] ≠ 0 while k ∈ {14,21,33,55} are all 0). The
spec pins each warmup at its exact boundary — `warmup − 1` must still leak — so a value
that is merely large enough does not pass.

`accuracy` gained `basis` and `scoredPoints`. **Read that as a qualifier, not as an
honesty feature in itself** — an independent adversarial pass showed it cut the other
way on the no-data path, where a stated evidence count of 106 made an empty claim read
as a well-grounded one. Two defects that pass found and closed on the same branch:

- **MASE spanned two different windows.** The numerator moved to `[warmup, n)` while
  the denominator stayed on the full series, so the benchmark priced days the numerator
  refused to score. `mase` now takes a `from` bound. The error runs **both** ways:
  measured over 400 simulated series of each shape, an opening-blitz head flatters MASE
  by a mean 4.5% (worst 13.9%, 22/400 beats-naive verdict flips) and a leading-zero
  "POS went live on day 20" head punishes it by a mean 6.6% (143/400 flips). Both
  shapes are ones `toDailySeries` produces directly.
- **The no-data series certified itself.** Zero-fill means a restaurant with no
  consumption yields 120 zeros, not a short series, so Holt-Winters never returns null
  and MAE/RMSE both answer **0** — a perfect forecast over a series holding no
  observation (`mape` and `mase` already refused, both dividing by zero). All four now
  return null with `basis: "no_observations_in_scored_window"`, `scoredPoints: 0`, per
  ADR 0051's rule that an unknown is an em dash and never a zero. The zeros are
  pre-existing; what the ADR 0064 fields added was the false confidence around them.

**The numbers this invalidates.** On a simulated 120-day series of the shape
`toDailySeries` produces, the reported figures move MAE 1.606 → 3.550, RMSE 2.106 →
4.546, MAPE 6.95% → 15.31%, MASE 0.402 → 0.889, scored points 120 → 106. The forecast
still beats seasonal-naive, by 11% rather than 2.5×. No forecast accuracy figure was
ever rendered to a user (`fitted` has zero hits outside `apps/api-gateway`), so nothing
shipped needs correcting — but any such number quoted in a doc or a session predating
2026-09-02 is wrong by roughly 2×.

Regression guards: `apps/api-gateway/src/analytics/engine/forecasting.spec.ts` (7 new
cases) and `apps/api-gateway/src/analytics/forecast-accuracy-honesty.spec.ts` (4 new
cases, service-level through a mocked client). **7 of the 11 are verified failing**
against the pristine tree with the specs in place — measured, not projected: the source
files were reverted and the suite re-run, 3 against `origin/main` and 4 against the
pre-adversary commit. The rest are must-pass-both-ways guards that the fixes are not a
blanket mute — that an honest `fitted` is still a *useful* fit, that `from` clamps
rather than reading off the front of the array, and that a restaurant which *does* have
consumption still gets scored. The leak assertion compares all three models as a single
object, so the pre-fix failure reads `holtLinear: false, holtWinters: false, ses: true`
— naming every model that broke instead of short-circuiting on the first.

## ~~Analytics — the `delivered` filters are the wrong case, so the fix above is necessary but not sufficient~~ — added 2026-09-01, CLOSED 2026-09-02

**CLOSED.** [[0058-order-status-is-an-enum-not-a-string]] (PR #222) removed every literal
below and replaced them with the shared sets in
`apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/order-status.ts`
(`ORDER_SPEND_STATUSES` / `ORDER_ARRIVED_STATUSES` / `ORDER_OUTSTANDING_STATUSES`, read
through `hasStatus()`), and `scripts/check_order_status_literals.py` fails the build if
one reappears. The entry is kept struck through rather than deleted because the sentence
below — *do not close the forecast entries on "the panels are populated"* — was the whole
reason it was written, and that instruction has now been discharged rather than dropped.

Per-site disposition, verified on `main` at `77eb7888`:

- `analytics/analytics.service.ts:145` — now `.in("status", ORDER_SPEND_STATUSES)` (`:190`)
- `analytics/insights/insight-generator.service.ts:239` — now `.in(...)` (`:254`)
- `analytics/goals.service.ts:320` — now `.in(...)` (`:321`)
- `analytics/advanced-analytics.service.ts:247, 394` — now `hasStatus(o.status, …)` (`:303`, `:454`)
- `analytics/advanced-analytics.service.ts:413` — **this one survived #222 and was closed
  separately on 2026-09-02.** It is the only site of the five written as an array
  membership test rather than as a comparison, so neither #222's sweep nor its guard —
  whose patterns were `.eq` / `.in` / `.not(...in...)` / `=== "…"` — could see it, and
  `committedOpenOrders` and `openOrderCount` on the cashflow panel stayed a structural
  zero after the rest were fixed. Now `hasStatus(o.status, ORDER_OUTSTANDING_STATUSES)`,
  pinned by `order-schema-drift.spec.ts` ("counts committed open orders instead of
  reporting a structural zero", proven failing against the pre-fix line), and the guard
  reads the `[…].includes(o.status)` form so the next one cannot hide the same way.

The lesson worth keeping: **a guard that reads one spelling of a membership test and not
the other has the same shape as the bug it hunts.** Four of five sites were fixed and the
panel that motivated the entry — cashflow — still showed a zero.

Two adjacent findings from the same sweep:

- ~~`analytics/insights/insight-generator.service.ts:308` maps
  `wineId: c.master_wine_id`~~ — **CLOSED 2026-09-01, in this same PR.** Its select
  embeds `restaurant_inventory(master_wine_id)` (correctly — that table has no
  `master_wine_id` of its own, and `analytics.service.ts:158` documents why), so
  PostgREST returns the value **nested**; reading it at the top level gave `undefined`
  on every row and the per-wine sub-families (`:394`, `:578`) `continue`d past
  everything. `analytics.service.ts` and `advanced-analytics.service.ts` both read the
  embed correctly; this file was the outlier. Taken at the ecosystem session's request
  — it reached the same measurement independently and judged a follow-up from the
  session already holding the file to be cheapest. Same failure mode as the 42703
  defects (permanent emptiness, no error) but **invisible to the schema-aware stub**,
  because the query is valid and only the read of its result was wrong — so the
  regression test asserts the mapped shape directly, and was proven failing pre-fix.
  **Census consequence:** of 573 enumerated insight types ~24 have an implementation,
  and the number a restaurant can actually *receive* was lower still, since purchasing
  had never emitted and the per-wine families skipped every row. Counting types that
  cannot fire overstates coverage.
- `providers/providers.service.ts:955-960` **inserts** `wine_name`, `actual_delivery`
  and `source` into `procurement_orders`. None of the three exist on that table, so
  `createRetroactiveOrder` should 42703 on every call. It at least throws rather than
  swallowing. Unverified against a live call.

## Four defects surfaced by the motion-canvas codebase sweep — added 2026-08-27 — **ALL FOUR CLOSED 2026-09-12** on `fix/motion-sweep-defects`

Found while five agents read the web app to ground sketch 087's motion set in real
behaviour. Design work, but the findings are engineering ones. All four were
**re-verified by hand in the repository** rather than taken on an agent's report — one
agent's count was wrong (it said 15 `data-ux-key` markers; there are 11), which is the
reason for checking each claim independently.

> **CLOSED 2026-09-12.** Every one of the four is fixed on `fix/motion-sweep-defects`:
> (1) `fca5e8a0` + `2a0e9e5c`, (2) `5ba2972a` (and hardened by `e93f368c`, `57e59ae2`,
> `f324633a`), (3) `6499c8a9`, (4) `6499c8a9`. Each subsection below keeps the original
> diagnosis — it is why the fix looks the way it does — and closes with what HEAD
> actually does. **The line numbers in the diagnoses are as-found on 2026-08-27 and are
> stale by design; the closures cite grep anchors.** This block exists because the
> section shipped for a day asserting four fixed defects as open, inside the very PR
> that fixed them — CLAUDE.md §5b's named rot, found by the gate, not by the sweep that
> claimed to have looked.
>
> **Not closed, same family, untouched here:** `apps/web/src/lib/spotCountOutbox.ts`
> still has defect 2's exact shape — grep `if (permanent || m.retryCount + 1 >=
> MAX_ATTEMPTS)` there: it deletes the entry and returns only `{ sent, failed }`, with
> no record of which count was lost. Nothing on this branch touched it.

### 1. Every `stock:updated` rewrites the row someone is reading

`apps/web/src/lib/websocket.tsx:489-491` invalidates three whole query trees on every
stock event:

```ts
queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.inventory.all })
queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.dashboard.all })
queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: queryKeys.wines.all })
```

One bottle moving anywhere in the restaurant refetches and re-renders inventory, the
dashboard and the wine library — including the row a manager is mid-way through
reading. The blast radius is the whole page, and the event carries enough information
to patch a single row instead.

**CLOSED 2026-09-12** (`fca5e8a0`, narrowed again by `2a0e9e5c`). HEAD: grep
`stock:updated` in `apps/web/src/lib/websocket.tsx` — the handler now invalidates with
`predicate: isQueryAffectedByStockUpdate` — the predicate is exported from
`lib/websocket.tsx` itself and also used by `hooks/queries/useInventoryQueries.ts` —
instead of three whole trees. The predicate scopes on `restaurantId` and defaults to **`true`** for a key
shape it does not recognise, so an unknown key costs a refetch and never a silent skip.
`['wines']` was dropped from the set on evidence, not taste: the `Wine` read model
carries no stock field, so a stock event cannot stale a wine row.

### 2. A dropped door receipt is indistinguishable from a delivered one

`lib/doorOutbox.ts:113-118` gives up after 8 attempts or a permanent 4xx. **The drop
itself is correct and deliberate** — the comment argues it well ("a queue that never
drains stops being watched, and then a real failure hides behind the stuck one") — and
the function reports the outcome, returning `{ sent, failed }` at `:129`.

The defect is that nothing reads it. `watchDoorOutbox:137-139` calls
`flushDoorOutbox().then(() => onChange?.())`, discarding the result, and
`pages/receiving/DoorReceipt.tsx:70-71` only re-reads `pendingDoorCount`. So when a
porter's receipt is discarded after 8 failures, the pending badge **decrements exactly
as it does on success**. The person at the door cannot tell a delivered receipt from a
lost one.

`lib/spotCountOutbox.ts:110-115` has the identical shape, so a lost spot count behaves
the same way.

**The fix is small** — surface `failed` where `pendingDoorCount` is already rendered.
Sketch 087's `inv-09` shows the shape: the receipt lands in a needs-attention rail as a
pinned task, deliberately not a modal, because this fires mid-service.

**CLOSED 2026-09-12** (`5ba2972a`, then hardened three times). HEAD: grep
`onChange?.(result)` in `lib/doorOutbox.ts` — `watchDoorOutbox` hands the full
`DoorFlushResult` to its callback, and the result separates `dropped` (given up on,
permanently) from the retryable `failed`. But the count was never the fix: the flush
now WRITES each dropped receipt down before deleting the queue entry, keyed on the
queue id and scoped per restaurant (grep `DROPS_KEY_PREFIX`), so both door screens name
the order that was lost and survive the navigate that Finish triggers. Three later
rounds closed what that fix itself broke — a cross-tenant key, a receipt destroyed when
the record could not be written, a strand counted once per pass and then once per
reload. See the 2026-09-12 entry at the end of this file for what is STILL open in this
family (401/403 classified as permanent) and for the offline-storage swallow underneath
it.

### 3. A docstring asserts a wiring that does not exist

`hooks/useUxOverrides.ts:12` reads *"Mounted once in DashboardLayout."* The hook has
**zero importers** (grep across `apps/web/src`), and 11 `data-ux-key` markers sit in
the tree waiting for a reporter that never runs. The dead code is a known deferral;
the false comment is not, and it is the more expensive half — it tells the next reader
the gap is already closed.

Fix is one line: say what is true.

**CLOSED 2026-09-12** (`6499c8a9`). HEAD: grep `NOT MOUNTED ANYWHERE` in
`apps/web/src/hooks/useUxOverrides.ts` — the docstring says what is true. The hook still
has zero importers; that is a known deferral, and now a stated one. The marker count is
not restated here, because it decays: measure it with
`grep -ro 'data-ux-key' apps/web/src | wc -l`.

### 4. `Notifications.tsx:231` discards `foldedById`

`lib/notificationStack.ts`'s `collapseStackedNotifications` returns a `foldedById` map
describing what each fold swallowed. The page throws it away, so the "N earlier
duplicates grouped" string the library already computes is rendered only by the header
bell — the inbox itself silently hides the count.

---

**CLOSED 2026-09-12** (`6499c8a9`). HEAD: grep `foldedById` in
`apps/web/src/pages/Notifications.tsx` — the page destructures it out of
`collapseStackedNotifications` and renders it, so the "N earlier duplicates grouped"
string the library already computed reaches the reader instead of being recomputed or
dropped.

Sketch 087 carries a motion for each of these; the motions are proposals, but the
defects are independent of whether any of that design ships.

---

## Two defects surfaced by the P2 door build — added 2026-08-30

Found while building DoorNext/ReceivingNext (ADR 0044 P2); re-verified in the
tree by the coordinating session, not taken on the builder's report.

**SyncManager silently deletes every queued door receipt.**
`apps/web/src/lib/doorOutbox.ts:23` enqueues mutations as type
`'receiving.door'`, but the handler map in `apps/web/src/lib/sync-manager.ts`
(lines 52–91) knows only `calendar.*`, `provider.*`, and
`notification.archive`. An unknown type errors on flush, is retried three
times, then discarded (`sync-manager.ts:234–252` — "Discarding dead
mutation"). Proven live during the P2 build: a queued door receipt vanished at
boot with no network call. This is a second drop path that bypasses the
doorOutbox's own flush entirely; the receipt looks saved on the phone and is
deleted before it is ever sent. **FIXED 2026-08-31** on branch
`fix/sync-manager-foreign-mutations` (`415f7173`, pushed, unmerged): the
manager now skips any mutation type it has no handler for — before the
dead-mutation discard too — leaving ownership with the outboxes
(`inventory.spotCount` was exposed to the same drop path and is covered by
the same fix). Three regression tests pin it; the foreign-mutation test was
proven to fail against the pre-fix file.

**`globals.css` paints every input white with `!important`.**
`apps/web/src/styles/globals.css:349–359` forces `background-color: white
!important; color-scheme: light !important` (plus text fill) on every
`input/textarea/select`. Any dark-ground page gets white input wells it
cannot override without its own `!important` rule (DoorNext ships a
page-scoped `.door-input` counter-rule). Every Mudavym charcoal-ground page
will hit this; it belongs in the design-foundation cleanup, not in
per-page counter-rules. **Scope note (2026-08-30):** this cites the tree the
P2 build audited (`feat/mudavym-design-p1`, cut from main). `feat/mudavym-brand`
already carries `.dark` counterparts for these rules and closed the last gap
(`select option` popping white lists in dark) in `e21bd452` — verified against
`origin/feat/mudavym-brand`. Do not re-fix there; main and this branch inherit
the fix through the planned rebase.
## Absence-as-health — the three acting instances, CLOSED 2026-09-01

Three defects that did not merely *report* absence as health but **acted** on it. Split
out from the wider read-path population (`03-scenarios/DELIVERY-AUDIT.md` §6) because the
remediation clock differs: a read path misinforms until fixed, an acting path writes —
or fails to write — a row that a later code fix cannot undo.

**Production damage measured BEFORE fixing, which is the reason all three could be fixed
in one pass:**

| Path | Damage found |
|---|---|
| `pos_checks` with `table_id IS NULL` | **0 of 66** |
| duplicates sharing a `catalogue_vendor_id` (the only key the guard checks) | **0** |
| `wine_consumption_log` rows | **0** (table empty; `pos_item_mappings` also 0) |

The 2 name-duplicate providers that *do* exist (`"ALDEMIR DISTRIBUTION"` 12h apart, `"q"`
3.2s apart, both 2026-05-11) all carry `catalogue_vendor_id = NULL`, so they came through
the manual-add path and are **not attributable to this guard**. They are a separate
pre-existing data-quality item, not repaired here, and not mine to delete.

**1. `providers.service.ts` — the dedup guard failed OPEN.** `maybeSingle()` returns
`data: null` for both "no row matched" and "the query failed", so a failed lookup read as
"no duplicate exists" and the insert proceeded. Now throws `ServiceUnavailableException`
— a guard that cannot check must refuse, never wave through. 409 and 503 stay
distinguishable to the caller: "already yours" is the user's problem, "we could not
check" is ours.

**2. `pos-hub.service.ts` `loadTables` — wrote a wrong row and reported success.** A
failed lookup became an empty table list, every check resolved `table_id: null`, and the
ingest returned zero errors — indistinguishable from a restaurant with no tables, and the
mechanism that manufactures the exact "table_id is null on every row" symptom the POS
bridge was diagnosed on. Now returns `{ tables, error }` and the caller pushes the error
into the ingest's existing `errors` channel. **Degrades rather than rejecting** — table
resolution is enrichment, so a failed lookup must not become a 500 the POS retries
forever — and an *empty* result is still reported as clean, so the fix does not trade a
silent failure for a permanent false alarm.

**3. `pos-hub.service.ts` `recordConsumption` — SILENT OMISSION, and the worst of the
three.** The upsert result was discarded entirely and the wrapping `try/catch` is inert,
because supabase-js resolves with `{ data, error }` rather than throwing. No bad row is
written; a good row is **missing**. That is harder to live with than corruption: a wrong
row can be found and repaired by querying for it, whereas nothing anywhere records that a
consumption event failed to land — so the damage cannot be enumerated, bounded or
repaired, while velocity, XYZ, reorder points, Holt-Winters and goal progress all quietly
under-count over a ledger that looks complete. The comment directly above the call
already said *"Drifting the invisible one is the worse failure"*; the code did not guard
the risk it named. Now logs at `error` with the PostgREST code and the idempotency key.

**Category note, worth keeping:** read / write / **omission** are three different
remediation problems, not two. Omission is the one with no forensic trail.

**Evidence:** `pos-hub.fail-open.spec.ts` (5) and `providers.fail-open.spec.ts` (3), each
proven failing against the pre-fix code first — the providers guard by reverting it in
place and re-running. Full gateway suite 1499 passed / 11 skipped / 121 suites, tsc clean.

**Not fixed here, still open:** the `delivered` vs `DELIVERED` case mismatch (filed
earlier today), the read-path instances in `DELIVERY-AUDIT.md` §6, and the 2 pre-existing
duplicate provider rows.

## 🔴 `procurement_orders.quantity_received` has two units, and `verifyReceipt` assumes one — added 2026-09-02

**LIVE DEFECT. Not repaired — the repair is a founder decision, stated below.**

Found reviewing the six procurement PRs merged 2026-09-02 against each other. Neither
PR is wrong on its own; the column is.

`quantity_received` is a single `integer` column with four parties. Three say it is
stated in the ORDER's own unit, beside `quantity`:

- `procurement.service.ts:1602` — `markDelivered` writes `quantityReceived ?? existingOrder.quantity`
- `procurement.service.ts:1128` — `updateOrder` writes `quantityReceivedInOrderUom`; the
  DTO field name #233 chose is itself the claim
- `procurement.service.ts:2353` — `verifyReceipt` writes back `acceptedQty + rejectedQty`
  "in the COUNTED unit as submitted", and says so in a comment

The fourth writes **bottles**: `receiving.service.ts:504` (#228, ADR 0062) sets
`quantity_received = totals.receivedBottles`, a sum of
`counted_qty_bottles − rejected_qty_bottles`.

`verifyReceipt` then reads the column as `stockedQtyInCountedUom`
(`procurement.service.ts:2178`) and `conv(rawStocked, counted)`
(`invoice-match.ts:558`) multiplies it by the pack size — so a door-written value is
converted to bottles a **second** time.

*(Line numbers are as of this entry. All seven were recomputed after the comments
that cite them were inserted — a comment that cites lines in its own file shifts
them, which is how the first version of this entry came to be 30–41 lines out.)*

**Measured**, by calling `toBottleOperands` / `computeMatch` directly with the exact
operands a 5-case order of a twelve-pack produces (door-counted 5 cases, desk-verified
5 cases, neither desk client sends `countedUom` so it falls back to the order's):

```
no invoice on file   accepted 60 · stocked 720 · ledgerDelta −660 · verdict "unmatched"
matching invoice     accepted 60 · stocked 720 · ledgerDelta −660 · verdict "matched"
```

**The invoice is a precondition of the WORD, not of the damage.** `−660` is identical
on both rows, and the gate at `procurement.service.ts:2267` fires on
`match.ledgerDelta !== 0`, so `applyReceiptAdjustment` removes 660 bottles from live
stock either way. What the invoice changes is what the manager is told: without one the
screen says `unmatched`, which someone might question; with a matching one it says
`matched`, which nobody would. `invoice-match.ts:706` is the single line that makes that
difference (`if (!hasInvoice) verdict = "unmatched"`), and it touches no operand.

The first version of this entry quoted only the `matched` row without saying an invoice
was needed for it — recorded here because the omission made the scenario sound both more
alarming and less likely than it is.

The `?? quantity` fallback carries the same assumption for an order nothing has booked
at all.

**Reachability.** Exactly the ordinary door→desk path the two PRs were built for, and
only when the data is GOOD: a multiplying unit with no pack size already throws
`MatchUnitError` before any write (ADR 0062), so the corruption needs a correctly
created order.

**Nothing is corrupted retrospectively, and that claim is now measured rather than
asserted.** Production project `exzueerziesmczwlhomd`, queried by the coordinating
session via SQL on 2026-09-02:

| relation | measured |
|---|---|
| `procurement_receipt_events` | **0 rows** |
| `procurement_orders` with a non-null, non-zero `quantity_received` | **0 of 2 orders** |

So no door receipt has ever been written in production and no order carries a
door-written `quantity_received` for `verifyReceipt` to misread. The exposure is
prospective: the first real door→desk delivery. (The earlier version of this entry
stated the 0-rows figure with no provenance at all, which is the one thing this
register exists to refuse.)

**The fork, and why it was not guessed at.**

| | make the door write the order's unit | teach `verifyReceipt` the value is bottles |
|---|---|---|
| agrees with | the column name, `quantityReceivedInOrderUom`, three writers, every client that renders it | the ledger, which only ever speaks bottles |
| costs | the column is `integer`, so `bottles / packSize` rounds a part-case delivery away | `computeMatch` has no bottles-native stocked operand; adding one touches the hash-checked backtest fixture |
| does not fix | `markDelivered` books `resolvedQuantity` as a bottle delta regardless of `unit_type` (`procurement.service.ts:1715`) — five CASES book five bottles, pre-existing and separate | the same |

The door's own arithmetic is unaffected either way: it reads the column only on the
FIRST receipt for an order (`receiving.service.ts:369`, guarded by `priorEventCount`),
so it never reads back its own write.

**No guard covers this.** Neither `check_quantity_units.py` nor the other five
procurement guards check cross-writer unit agreement on a shared column — they check
that a DTO field *declares* a unit, not that two writers *agree* on one. Whichever way
the fork is resolved, the resolution wants a guard, or the next writer reopens it.

Both ends now carry a comment pointing here. **Do not close the ledger-unit work
(ADR 0070) without resolving this** — it is the same question one table over.
## CI — the nightly production E2E has reported `failure` for `not configured` since it was written — added 2026-09-02

**`Production E2E Tests` (`.github/workflows/e2e-prod.yml`) has never run with
credentials.** `gh run list --workflow=e2e-prod.yml --limit 200` returns **120 runs
spanning 2026-05-06 to 2026-09-02 — 118 `failure`, 2 `success`, every one a `schedule`
event.** Not one `workflow_dispatch`, so `deploy.yml:279` ("Trigger production E2E gate
(non-blocking)") has never produced a run here either; its `gh workflow run` is
`|| echo`-swallowed, which is why nobody noticed. The cause is not production: **none of
the secrets the job maps exists.** `gh secret list` on the repository holds exactly
`ADMIN_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `API_GATEWAY_URL`, `RAILWAY_ORCHESTRATOR_URL`,
`SUPABASE_DB_URL`, `SUPABASE_DIRECT_CONNECTION_STRING`, `SUPABASE_POOLER_URL`.

**Why it matters:** the red said "Production E2E Tests failed" every morning while
meaning "this workflow is not configured". That is the [[absence-reported-as-health]]
shape inverted — absence reported as *illness* — and it costs the same thing: the signal
is unreadable either way, so the nightly has been noise since 2026-05. Directly adjacent
to **OD-120** in `.planning/decisions/OPEN-DECISIONS.md`, where a *skipped* health audit
renders as "nothing happened"; same family, opposite rendering, and both leave production
unverified. (OD-120 is not edited by this entry — inserting a register row shifts ~170
citations.)

**What the founder must set** (Settings → Secrets and variables → Actions). Required —
without any one of them the run asserts nothing about production, with the file that
reads each. **Table re-derived 2026-09-12, not copied forward from the 2026-09-02
original** — waves D, E and G are retired (ADR 0137, below), so every "Gates" cell
naming them, and every line number, needed re-checking, not just the two spots an
earlier round of this same fix already touched:

| Secret | Read by | Gates |
|---|---|---|
| `SUPABASE_URL` | `services/agent-orchestrator/tests/e2e/conftest_prod.py:131`, `:196` | `prod_jwt` + `prod_supabase` — Waves A, B, C |
| `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | `conftest_prod.py:132` | `prod_jwt` |
| `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` | `conftest_prod.py:197` | `prod_supabase` — same as `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, this gates ALL of Waves A, B and C, not just the teardown sweep: `teardown_e2e_records` (`:216-217`) is `scope="session", autouse=True` and takes `prod_supabase` as a parameter, so its skip (when the key is absent) propagates to every test in the session, not only to cleanup. Corrected 2026-09-12 — a prior draft of this row understated it as "the teardown sweep only." |
| `E2E_TEST_EMAIL` | `conftest_prod.py:133`, `apps/web/e2e/prod-smoke.spec.ts:33` | every authenticated assertion, Waves A and F |
| `E2E_TEST_PASSWORD` | `conftest_prod.py:134`, `apps/web/e2e/prod-smoke.spec.ts:34` | same |
| `VERCEL_PRODUCTION_URL` → `E2E_BASE_URL` | `apps/web/playwright.prod.config.ts:21–26` — the config **throws** | Wave F cannot start at all |

`RAILWAY_ORCHESTRATOR_URL` (`conftest_prod.py:103`, gates Waves A–C) and `ADMIN_API_KEY`
(`conftest_prod.py:178`, gates all of Wave B) are also required and are **already set** —
listed so a future deletion is caught rather than silently skipping waves.

Optional, each skipping exactly one wave, so a partial run stays legitimate:
`RABBITMQ_URL` (`tests/e2e/wave_c_agent_triggers.py:100` → Wave C),
`SENTRY_DSN` (`conftest_prod.py:36` → failure alerting only). `TOAST_WEBHOOK_SECRET`
and `GMAIL_USER` are gone from this list — their only readers, waves D and E, are
retired (ADR 0137, below); see that addendum before assuming either still gates
anything.

**Fixed on `fix/mc-e2e-honesty-2026-09-02`:** a first step named
`Required secrets are present` runs before any install, prints
`CANNOT CHECK -- missing secrets: <names>` as the first line of its log plus a
`::error` annotation, and exits 1 — values are never printed, and the five branches
(none set / partially set / all required / all set / empty-string secret) were each
executed against the extracted step script before commit. The stale header comment
("STATUS: schedule-present / capability-unverified until one wave XML lands") was
replaced with the measured 118-of-120 fact. **Also fixed:** the workflow pinned
`pnpm/action-setup@v2` + `version: '8'` while `package.json:10,12` declares
`engines.pnpm >= 9.0.0` / `packageManager: pnpm@9.15.9`, so `Install frontend
dependencies` died with `ERR_PNPM_UNSUPPORTED_ENGINE` on every run (the actual failing
step on run `33600086809`, 2026-09-02) — a second red that would have surfaced the moment
the secrets landed. Aligned to `@v4` unpinned, matching `ci.yml:770` and `deploy.yml:217`.

**Still open, not fixed here (needs a founder decision, both out of a bounded change):**

1. ~~**Wave G can never run.**~~ **Superseded 2026-09-12 (ADR 0137) — no longer "can
   never run," now deleted.** Its gate read `GOOGLE_CALENDAR_CREDENTIALS`,
   `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` or `CALENDAR_REFRESH_TOKEN` (`tests/e2e/wave_g_calendar.py:52–54`,
   pre-deletion) and none of the three was ever mapped into the job's `env:` block — but
   the deeper reason, found while deciding what to do about that, is that the Google
   Calendar sync it named was investigated and explicitly cut on 2026-08-26 — the file
   is live on `main`; what was removed is the `enable_calendar_sync` **flag**
   (`apps/api-gateway/src/settings/feature-flag-registry.ts`, the `enable_calendar_sync` comment block and its `REMOVED_FEATURE_FLAGS` entry — no line number, since this file has already drifted under two merges during this ADR's own review). See ADR
   0137 for the full evidence and the two sibling waves (D, E) retired alongside it.
2. ~~**`GMAIL_PASSWORD` is mapped but read by no wave**~~ **FIXED 2026-09-12, same PR
   that retired D/E/G.** It was never read by any wave (only by
   `services/agent-orchestrator/config/settings.py:48`, the runtime service, not this
   CI job) and is now removed from `e2e-prod.yml`'s `env:` block entirely, alongside
   `TOAST_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and `GMAIL_USER` — least-privilege: an unused secret mapped
   into a job env is readable by every step and third-party action in it.
3. **The deploy dispatch is invisible.** `deploy.yml:287–290` ends in
   `|| echo "E2E workflow dispatch skipped …"`, so a dispatch that never fires is
   indistinguishable from one that did. Not touched — it is deploy.yml's, and its
   non-blocking contract is deliberate (D-13/D-14).

### 2026-09-11 amendment — the nightly was rebuilt ([ADR 0135](decisions/0135-the-nightly-reports-four-states-and-walks-the-gated-pages.md), branch `test/nightly-e2e-modernised`)

The founder's instruction (2026-09-06): *"nightly e2e account: improve backtests and
improve overall tests its outdated"*. What the rebuild measured and changed, so the entry
above reads as history rather than as the current state:

- **Four latent defects the old suite carried, beyond "no secrets":** (a) Wave F asserted
  **≥7 "Active" agent cards** on `/admin/health` and (b) **wrote** an `onboarding_sessions`
  row through `/studio` with a best-effort teardown (`prod-smoke.spec.ts` F-2, F-4);
  (c) `playwright.prod.config.ts` asked for `channel: 'chrome'` while the workflow installed
  only bundled Chromium, so the wave could never launch a browser; (d) **the session
  teardown in `tests/e2e/conftest_prod.py` called `teardown_sim(client, apply=True)`**,
  which resolves every restaurant whose slug starts with `sim-` and deletes it with its
  write-set — and all four production simulator houses carry such slugs. The first
  nightly to receive `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` would have deleted them. **Fixed:**
  opt-in via `E2E_SIM_TEARDOWN=1`, dry-run otherwise (a write-path instance of
  [[absence-reported-as-health]], so fixed on its own merits, not deferred to the shape).
- **The orchestrator is 404** at the URL the corpus records
  (`agent-orchestrator-production.up.railway.app` → "Application not found",
  2026-09-11). `RAILWAY_ORCHESTRATOR_URL` is set but unreadable. Waves A–E, G now run only
  after a preflight that probes `/health`; otherwise they are reported as `cannot_check`
  with the HTTP code in the reason, not as six failures.
- **Replaced:** `prod-smoke.spec.ts` + `playwright.prod.config.ts` → `apps/web/e2e/nightly/`
  (`manifest.json`, `lib.ts`, `nightly.spec.ts`, `honest-reporter.ts`) +
  `playwright.nightly.config.ts`; new read-only Wave H at
  `services/agent-orchestrator/tests/e2e_gateway/`; `scripts/e2e/nightly_summary.py`
  (exit 0 pass · 1 fail · 2 cannot_check). Required secrets shrank to `API_GATEWAY_URL`
  (already set), `E2E_TEST_EMAIL`, `E2E_TEST_PASSWORD`; `E2E_BASE_URL` defaults to
  `https://mudavym.com`. Item 2 above (`GMAIL_PASSWORD` mapped, read by nothing) is
  resolved by not mapping it; item 1 (Wave G's three unmapped Google secrets) and item 3
  (the deploy dispatch's swallowed `gh workflow run`) are **still open**. **[CORRECTED
  2026-09-17: item 1 was closed 2026-09-12 by ADR 0137, which deleted Wave G; see the
  struck item above. Item 3 was not re-measured.]**
- **Backtests:** the ADR 0093 canned day is pinned by content hash
  (`datasets/sim/fixtures/scenario-canned-day.json`, asserted in
  `scripts/test_simulate_scenarios.py`), and the forecast chain has a pinned seven-series
  fixture (`datasets/sim/fixtures/forecast-backtest.json`,
  `forecasting.backtest.spec.ts`) whose drift assertion was proven red on a 1e-6
  perturbation. Wave H reads the live forecast and insight-catalog endpoints for the
  same honesty shapes.
- **Still cannot be verified until the founder acts:** `aldemirkonuk@mudavym.com` does
  not exist in `public.users` (2026-09-11), so no production run has happened; the
  first `workflow_dispatch` after `E2E_TEST_EMAIL` / `E2E_TEST_PASSWORD` land is the
  evidence ADR 0135 is missing. Locally (worktree Vite → local gateway `:4010` at main's
  `fed2b7ca` on the shared database, sim-owner, Sim Bistro) the browser walk ended
  **48 pass · 1 fail · 28 absent · 0 cannot_check** — 8 pages `absent` in both passes
  (PR #289 not merged), the door/document routes `absent` (no order, no document in the
  house) — and Wave H passed 9 of 10 with `h.build.live` `cannot_check` (local
  `commit=unknown`).
- **A production read the walk caught on its first honest pass — `/reports/schedules` is
  500.** `/communications` (Mudavym) rendered *"Saved schedules could not be loaded …
  (Request failed with status code 500)"*, and `GET /api/v1/reports/schedules` as the
  Sim Bistro owner answers `500 "Could not find the table 'public.scheduled_reports' in
  the schema cache"` on the shared database — the gateway reads a relation production
  does not hold (`reports.ts:195`). The same page's conversation-history read answered
  404 in the walk (a direct `GET /procurement/conversations/history` answered 200 `[]`
  a minute later; the hook's exact request is the next thing to pin down). Both are
  filed here as findings, not fixed in the nightly's PR; `check_queried_tables_exist.py`
  passed on this tree, so the table's migration exists in the tree and is the kind of
  thing the schema-parity job should have caught.
- **A legacy-copy defect the walk surfaced:** `apps/web/src/pages/team/command/ManagerShiftDesk.tsx:128`
  sets `rosterKnown = membersQ.data !== undefined`, so the sentence *"The roster could not
  be read — who is on this team is unknown, not empty"* renders while the members query is
  still **loading** — a loading state wearing a failed read's words. The walk reads such
  pages twice with a pause; the page should distinguish `isLoading` from `isError`.

### 2026-09-11 addendum — the pr-audit-gate ran on #349 and returned BLOCK

**Not merged.** Three parallel Opus angles + a possibly-adversarial pass, per ADR 0090.
Correctness and compliance both came back APPROVE WITH NOTES; security came back
**BLOCK**, which per the skill's own step 6 means the adversarial pass never ran. Full
report and every finding from all three angles: the PR comment https://github.com/aldemirkonuk/RestaurantAIAutomation/pull/349#issuecomment-5643186310, the durable SHA-stamped
record **[the committed copy was deleted 2026-09-17 on the founder's call; recoverable at `881fec82`]**. Founder-relayed answers
to forks F2/F3/F4 and the escalation approval for the ADR-index-row edit are recorded in
[ADR 0135](decisions/0135-the-nightly-reports-four-states-and-walks-the-gated-pages.md)'s
"Founder's answers (relayed)" section, not repeated here.

**The blocking finding, independently reproduced by this session, not only asserted by
the auditor:** `apps/web/playwright.nightly.config.ts:49` sets
`trace: 'retain-on-failure'`. A Playwright trace is a full network capture, so a failed
nightly run — which, at 127-of-130 red, is the common case — writes the plaintext test
password and session JWTs into the trace artifact, and the workflow uploads that artifact
for 30 days on a repository this session confirmed is **PUBLIC**
(`gh repo view --json isPrivate,visibility` → `visibility: PUBLIC`). Proof, not
inference: a run with `E2E_TEST_PASSWORD` set to the sentinel
`PW_SENTINEL_ZQ7X_DO_NOT_MATCH` failed at login as designed and left that exact string
inside `test.trace`, `0-trace.trace` and a `resources/*.json` blob. The leaked local
traces were deleted (`rm -rf apps/web/test-results/nightly-traces apps/web/test-results/nightly`)
— nothing reached origin.

**Fix list for the next session, before re-running the gate (none of this is done yet):**

1. `apps/web/playwright.nightly.config.ts:49` — `trace: 'retain-on-failure'` → `'off'`.
   This alone clears the blocking finding.
2. `.github/workflows/e2e-prod.yml` — guard `API_GATEWAY_URL`'s host the same way
   `E2E_BASE_URL` already is (currently only the base URL is checked for
   `localhost`/`127.0.0.1`; the API URL, where credentials actually go, is not).
3. Constrain both walked houses (the sim-owner house and, once F3's second house exists,
   the legacy house) to `slug LIKE 'sim-%'`, `cannot_check` otherwise — candidate read:
   `GET /api/v1/organizations/branches` (`apps/web/src/contexts/AuthContext.tsx:365`).
4. `scripts/e2e/nightly_summary.py::_load_json` — a read/decode error should record
   `cannot_check`, not crash the summary step.
5. Optional hardening the auditor suggested: a `page.route('**', …)` interceptor that
   aborts and records any non-GET request, making the walk's "read-only" claim something
   the suite itself checks rather than only something the manifest promises.
6. The workflow header's comment claiming secret values are "never printed" should be
   re-verified against the actual step script before being repeated in a fix commit.
7. Lower-priority doc nits from the compliance angle, still open: `ADR-0135-f`'s claim
   holds `open` for a reason unrelated to the debt it names (this PR's diff deleted the
   comment its `verify` greps for — restoring an equivalent comment, without changing any
   wiring, would fix this); commit `5e4f21f1` carries the wrong co-author trailer
   (`Claude Fable 5.1` where CLAUDE.md §7 wants `Claude Opus 5`) — unfixable after the
   fact except in the eventual squash-merge body.

**What re-measuring turned up while writing this addendum, filed here rather than left
to rot per §5b:** the original "118 of 120 runs red" (this section, above, dated
2026-09-02) is unchanged and still an accurate historical snapshot for that date — it is
NOT being corrected here. What was wrong is that a later ADR 0135 draft and
`decisions/README.md:126` had presented that same 2026-09-02 number as if it were a fresh
2026-09-11 measurement; a fresh 2026-09-11 measurement is **130 runs, 127 failure, 3
success**, and both downstream citations are now fixed to say so.

### 2026-09-12 addendum — waves D, E and G retired, not repaired ([ADR 0137](decisions/0137-legacy-e2e-waves-d-e-g-are-retired-not-repointed.md))

Measured 2026-09-11 while auditing the unmerged nightly-E2E rebuild (PR #349): waves D
(`wave_d_toast_pipeline.py`), E (`wave_e_gmail_pipeline.py`) and G
(`wave_g_calendar.py`) were each written against a table or endpoint production does
not have (`pos_webhook_logs`, `inventory_stock`, uuid-typed `calendar_events` columns
holding string literals) — and the isolation keeping them from acting on production was
the schema disagreeing, not a guard. Anyone repairing the table names or the id format —
an obvious-looking cleanup — would have re-armed a real production write, and Wave E's
would have attempted to trigger a real low-stock email.

**Founder's task, verbatim:** *"decide per wave — repoint to the real tables, delete as
tests of a schema that no longer exists, or gate behind an explicit opt-in ... Do NOT
simply repair the table names without deciding whether a production write (and a
production email) is wanted."*

**Decided (ADR 0137, `Proposed`, awaiting founder review): delete all three, not
repoint or gate.** Three independent research passes (one per wave, reading the full
wave file plus the current live architecture) converged: Wave D's endpoint has zero
product caller and its table is deliberately omitted from the current POS-bridge
design ([[pos-bridge-state]]); Wave E's trigger mechanism (a raw table upsert publishing
`stock.threshold.breached`) was **never wired to anything** in this codebase — no
Postgres trigger or webhook connects a table write to `BufferManager`/`InventoryEngine`
anywhere in `supabase/`, so repointing the table would not by itself make the alert
fire, and `MOCK_NOTIFICATIONS` (not `GMAIL_USER`) is the actual send-gate, defaulting
`true`; Wave G's Google Calendar sync was investigated and **explicitly cut** on
2026-08-26, naming this exact test as the only thing keeping the illusion alive, and its
own assertion never exercised real `CalendarAgent` behavior even before that. None of
the three is "a real feature that drifted" — each tests something that either never had
a live consumer or was deliberately retired. Full per-wave evidence, file:line, is in
the ADR; not repeated here.

**Cleaned up alongside the deletion — full list, kept in ONE place to stop this
addendum and the ADR from drifting apart the way this PR's own review trail shows they
did four times:** see [ADR 0137](decisions/0137-legacy-e2e-waves-d-e-g-are-retired-not-repointed.md)'s
"Cleaned up alongside the deletion" paragraph for the complete, current list. Do not
re-enumerate it here — a second copy is exactly what kept going stale.

**Filed, not built here:** if real E2E coverage of low-stock email alerts or calendar
reminders is ever wanted, it needs a fresh design against the actual live event chain
(`pos.sale.completed` → `BufferManager` → `InventoryEngine` → the notification
consumer, or `CalendarAgent`'s real `reminder.important_date` RabbitMQ message) — new
test design, not a repair of the deleted files. No OD filed for this (a single
build-someday item, not a founder-vote fork); revisit only if the founder actually wants
that coverage.

### 2026-09-16 addendum — the nightly rebuilt on #349 for the new pages ([ADR 0135](decisions/0135-the-nightly-reports-four-states-and-walks-the-gated-pages.md) § Rebuild)

**Correction first:** the 2026-09-12 "fixed and merged" state was never true. The fix commit
sat unpushed in `wt-e2e`, with a half-done merge. #349 stayed OPEN at `82add0f6`. Status of
the audit fix list (numbered as in the audit report's §6, which has 8 items; this
register's own list above has 7), by item: **1, 2, 4 fixed** (reached the PR 2026-09-16). **3 fixed:**
the walk refuses any house not on `apps/web/e2e/nightly/sim-houses.json`, and the gateway
must name the branch `Sim …`. **5 fixed:** `ReadOnlyGuard` aborts browser writes. **6
corrected:** the target URLs ARE printed, unmasked; they are public. **7 closed by ADR
0137.** **8** is the squash body's trailer.

**New defect class, found and guarded:** the manifest's sentences rot silently. Nine had
stopped rendering from their page within five days, and `/logs` was enrolled without an
entry. `scripts/check_nightly_manifest.py` now fails CI on either.

**Still open:**
- (a) **[UPDATED 2026-09-17]** The signed-in walk has now run twice against production
  from a laptop: 100 pass · 2 fail · 20 absent, then 101 · 2 · 20 after the audit fixes.
  Two audit rounds also found, and this PR fixed, two ways a credential could reach the
  public artifact: a bearer token through Playwright's call log (B1), and the password
  through `error-context.md` (B2). CI has still never run it, because the
  account secrets are unset. The 2 fails are production defects, and both are still open:
  - `GET /reports/schedules` → 500, `public.scheduled_reports` missing in production
    (first seen 2026-09-11).
  - `text-senders.controller.ts` reads `user.id`, but the JWT user carries `userId`, so

    shape. Filed as its own task; CLAIMS `TD-2026-09-17-TEXT-SENDERS-USER-ID`.
  - The legacy app sends `PATCH /users/:id/preferences` just from being viewed. The walk
    aborts it.
- (b) `design-verdicts.json` is not re-derived in CI until the ADR 0148 snapshots reach
  main (CLAIMS `ADR-0135-h`).
- (c) The guard cannot see a generic sentence that a shared component also renders
  (`nothing here` renders from `HouseUserMenu.tsx:54`).
- (d) Every signed-out page sends Sentry envelopes. 48 were aborted in one walk. That
  is not a defect by itself, but it is the first measurement of that traffic.
- The pr-audit-gate must re-run on the new head before merge.

Full account: `apps/web/e2e/README.md` §9.

## Dev tooling — the dev bypass minted a session that could not open a page — FIXED 2026-09-03

`POST /api/v1/auth/dev-bypass-login` mints a real session for
`DEV_AUTH_BYPASS_EMAIL` so localhost testing never needs a password. That
account's `public.users.email_verified` is **false**, and
`ProtectedRoute` (`apps/web/src/components/ProtectedRoute.tsx:42`) redirects on
`user?.emailVerified === false`. So the bypass worked exactly as designed and
was still useless: every route went to `/verify-email`.

**Fixed** by making the *session* claim what it is, not by editing the row.
`devBypassLogin` now signs `emailVerified: true` plus a `devBypass: true`
marker (`auth.service.ts` — `generateTokens(user, devBypass)`); `JwtStrategy`
projects the marker onto `req.user`; `GET /auth/me` returns `true` only when
the marker is present **and** `devBypassEnvEnabled()` (NODE_ENV ≠ production
**and** `DEV_AUTH_BYPASS=true`) holds *at read time*. The env gate is re-checked
by the reader rather than trusted from the token, so the same validly-signed
token is inert against a production server — the shape the OAuth self-provision
hole (above, 2026-09-01) taught: an unset env var must never be the only thing
between a caller and a privilege. A normal token omits the key entirely; the
database column is untouched and still answers everywhere else.

**Completed in a second pass on the same branch,** because reporting the
session as verified to `/auth/me` was necessary and not sufficient: it got the
founder past ProtectedRoute and onto a page, where every data call then took
`403 EMAIL_NOT_VERIFIED` from `assertEmailVerified` in `JwtAuthGuard`
(`guards/jwt-auth.guard.ts:82`), which reads `req.user.emailVerified`. The same
two-gate condition is now applied in `JwtStrategy.validate` where that field is
built, and in `refreshAccessToken`, which otherwise rebuilt the payload from the
row and dropped the marker — the override lapsed after 15 minutes, which is the
worst shape of the bug: it looks like the fix never worked rather than like it
expired. Both gates are re-checked per request / per refresh, never inherited.

The `devBypass` marker itself stays an ungated report of what the token claims.
Collapsing the env gate into it too would make "not a dev session" and "a dev
session this server refuses to honour" the same value, and the second is the one
worth being able to see in a log.

**Evidence:** `apps/api-gateway/src/auth/dev-bypass-verified.spec.ts` (25).
Proven failing against the pre-change tree twice, by reverting the source files
in place and re-running: 5 of 13 for the first pass, then 3 of 25 for the
second. (e) and (f) drive the real `JwtStrategy.validate` INTO the real
`JwtAuthGuard.canActivate` rather than hand-seeding `req.user` — a correct check
in unreachable code is the shape that made the tenant comparison inert on every
authenticated route. `npx jest src/auth` → 106 passed / 12 suites;
`tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.spec.json` clean;
`check_read_errors_not_swallowed.py` → 0 new sites; prettier clean.

**Not verified:** no runtime or browser check — no database and no network were
available, so this is jest, typecheck and the static guards only. The end-to-end
claim ("the founder can now open a page and its data loads") is inferred from
the guard test, not observed against a running stack.

## POS bridge — four defects the Square day measured — added 2026-09-03

Context: [ADR 0105](decisions/0105-a-pos-connection-is-a-row-not-an-env-var.md); the
generator and `EVIDENCE.md` live in `scripts/simulate/square_day/`. One real Meyhouse
Friday (42 checks, 243 Square-shaped events) posted to a local gateway on `main` +
PR #285, orphan sim tenant only, database reads only. None of these is the adapter fork
itself — each is a defect to fix regardless of when the `pos_connections` build opens.

1. **The discard-all path logs nothing.** `pos-hub.service.ts:457-465` returns
   `received: 0 … "No recognizable checks in payload"` before the ingest log at `:552`
   ever runs. Run (ii): 243 events, 201 × 243, **zero** PosHub log lines. A provider
   whose envelope we mis-read is indistinguishable in the logs from a provider that
   never called. *Absence reported as health, log-shaped.* Fix: log the discard with
   provider, restaurant, byte length and the top-level keys seen, at `warn`.
2. **243 rejections produced one success-shaped line.** Run (i): the only PosHub line
   is the deduped `legacy_global` warning ("is authenticating with the legacy
   process-wide POS_HUB_WEBHOOK_SECRET") — secret resolution runs and logs before the
   signature check; the 401 at `:410` (signature `undefined`) never names the missing
   header. Fix: log every signature failure with the header names present and the
   scheme tried; never dedupe rejections.
3. ~~**`covers: null` is stored as `0`.** Run (iii): all 42 canonical checks sent
   `covers: null` and read back `0`. A POS that structurally cannot report covers
   (Square: not on the Order) becomes a venue with zero covers in analytics.~~
   **FIXED (#310).** The cause was a coercion, not the column: `pos-adapters.ts`'s
   `num()` was `Number(v)` guarded by `isFinite`, and `Number(null)` is `0`, which is
   finite. An **explicit** null became 0; an **omitted** key survived
   (`Number(undefined)` is NaN) — which is why the SimPOS lens read 44 nulls on the
