enumerate (no `gh`, no token, an API error, an unfetched ref), because zero open PRs and no
collision are indistinguishable otherwise. A collision names both files.

Two things this does **not** cover, stated rather than implied: a version taken by a branch with no
open PR is invisible to it (that branch cannot merge without a PR, so the collision is caught when
the PR opens, not before); and it says nothing about whether two migrations with *different*
versions conflict semantically.

**Note on the reproduction:** by the time the guard was written, both PRs had already been renamed
by hand (#309 → `20260905231500`, #310 → `20260905174500`), so the live tree no longer collides.
The guard's `--self-test` rebuilds the exact 2026-09-05 pair in a scratch repo with a stubbed `gh`
and asserts it fires.

## Library identity — a generic name bound one venue's stock to another's wine — FIXED — added 2026-09-05

Antalya night (PR #314, finding 4 🔴, main `528f13d1`). The Vanilla / Kaleiçi
bulk-add draft **"House White Wine"** — no producer, no vintage, no region —
auto-linked to `ac6a550f-…` (`HOUSE WHITE`, tier 4, created by the Sim Meyhouse
load: United States / California / 2023). The venue's Turkish house white was
stocked as a 2023 California wine and its own name disappeared from every
screen.

Reproduced on a Postgres 17 built from all 100 migrations
(`memory/local-postgres-from-migrations`), Meyhouse row inserted as that load
writes it:

```
match_library_wine('House White Wine', NULL, NULL, NULL, NULL, NULL)
  -> HOUSE WHITE   confidence 90   name_sim 1   producer_sim 1
```

`AUTO_LINK_CONFIDENCE` is 85. `producer_sim` is 1 because of one branch in the
scorer — `WHEN q.np = '' AND m.normalized_producer = '' THEN 1.0` — so **two
rows that state no producer score a perfect producer match**. Absence read as
agreement, in SQL (`memory/absence-reported-as-health`).

**Fixed** by ADR 0130 / `20260906010000_a_generic_name_stays_the_venues_own_wine.sql`:
`match_library_wine` returns no rows for a query that is not specific
(`wine_identity_is_specific`: a name plus a producer, or a vintage and a
region); a generic line becomes the venue's own provisional row
(`master_wine_library.provisional_for_restaurant_id`, keyed on `venue:<id>|` so
two venues coexist under the existing UNIQUE index) and is never a match target
for anybody; and bulk receive stores the draft's own label rather than
overwriting it with the resolved row's name (`inventory.service.ts:1137` on
main was the second, persisting write).

### Still open, filed here rather than fixed

1. ~~**The 26 Antalya rows and the earlier Meyhouse rows keep their wrong links.**~~
   **CLOSED 2026-09-05** — founder-authorised, measured, dry-run on a local build
   from all 102 migrations, then applied to production in one transaction.
   `datasets/library/REPAIR-2026-09-05.md` (record),
   `…-measure.md` (census), `repair-2026-09-05.sql` (the operation).
   **77 rows repaired, 0 deleted, 0 rows of any other tenant altered.** 48 whose
   fabricated producer was the only thing making them "specific" became their
   venue's own provisional rows (producer and country nulled, rekeyed under
   `venue:<id>|`); 29 with a real producer had only `country = 'Unknown'` nulled
   and stayed shared. Antalya's "House White Wine" is on its own row and its
   inventory, lot and 2 pour events moved onto it; Sim Bistro's `ac6a550f` row is
   md5-identical with its inventory, lot, pour event and 4 `menu_items` intact.
   Production after: sim rows with `country = 'Unknown'` **0**, with
   `producer = name` **0**, `v_signature_drift` **0**, duplicate `signature_hash`
   **0**, orphaned inventory **0**, and `match_library_wine('House White Wine',
   NULL…)` returns **0 candidates**.
   Two corrections it produced: `ac6a550f` was written by the **Sim Bistro** load,
   not Sim Meyhouse as ADR 0130 and the Antalya audit both say (its `created_at`
   is byte-identical to that tenant's and the submission row carries its id); and
   `sku_mappings` — named in this item as an FK to read — holds **0** rows for any
   of these wines, while two `master_wine_id` columns that carry **no FK at all**
   (`inventory_lots`, `pour_events`) do, and are invisible to `pg_constraint`.

2. ~~**A second import of the same countryless wine raises an error on the
   SHARED path.**~~ **CLOSED by #318.** Measured while building ADR 0130: the app
   computed the key from the draft (`signatureHashFor`) while the row was
   written with `country: item.country || "Unknown"`, and
   `trg_sync_signature_hash` rehashes from the **stored** fields, so the
   read-back missed and the resolver threw *"insert was skipped but no row
   carries signature …"*. Writing NULL made the row agree with its own key.

3. **The `CHECK` that would make an unowned generic row impossible is not
   added — and the blocker changed.** `CHECK (provisional_for_restaurant_id IS
   NOT NULL OR wine_identity_is_specific(producer, name, vintage, region))` used
   to be unenforceable, because `producer = the wine's own name` made every
   generic row look specific to SQL. #318 removed that, so it is now
   enforceable. It is still not added, for a smaller and nameable reason:
   `processPendingSubmissions` upserts into `master_wine_library` with no venue
   to own a row, so the constraint would turn a bad row on the
   submission-promotion path into a `23514` on a path ADR 0130 does not wire.
   Wire an owner (or an explicit "this submission is shared" assertion) there
   first, then add the constraint `NOT VALID`.

4. **🔴 Removing the fabrication widened the hole it was fixing, until ADR 0130
   lands.** Measured on a DB built from `main f4f9e4a6` — every migration except
   `20260906010000` — with the row main's own writer now creates for a generic
   menu line (`producer` NULL, `country` NULL):

   ```
   match_library_wine('House White Wine', NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL) -> confidence 100
   match_library_wine('House White',      NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL,NULL) -> confidence 100
   ```

   Up from 90, and now through the **exact-signature** branch as well as the
   fuzzy one: with producer and country both absent, two venues' "House White
   Wine" hash identically, so the second venue's row is refused outright
   (`duplicate key … idx_master_wine_library_signature_hash`). The fix was
   right and had to happen — `NOT NULL` on a column the writer cannot fill is a
   schema that requires a lie — but between #318 and ADR 0130 every venue's
   house wine collapses onto whichever venue wrote it first. Closed by
   `20260906010000_a_generic_name_stays_the_venues_own_wine.sql`; re-measured on
   the merged tree: **0 candidates**, and two venues hold two rows with two
   hashes.

## The orders wire — four things the routes did not send; ~~two~~ still open (2026-09-05)

Context: `apps/web/src/services/api/types.ts` `Order` declared nine keys
`OrderResponseDto` has never sent. Fixed, audited across sixteen consumers, and guarded
by `scripts/check_web_reads_gateway_dto_keys.py` — the table is
`.planning/06-pages/orders.md` §13.16. What the audit could NOT fix is below: each is a
gateway read-shape change on a route four surfaces share, so each is its own decision.

**Items 1 and 3 were decided by the founder and BUILT the same day (2026-09-05,
batch 40).** They are struck through in place below rather than deleted, because the
measurement each records is the reason the shape is what it is. Items 2 and 4 are
untouched and still open.

### 1. ~~No vendor name on any order route~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-05

**Founder's decision, 2026-09-05:** *"join the provider name into the orders routes and
the DTO — one embed on the list and detail routes, one field on the DTO, the four
surfaces and the letter fixed at once; the DTO-key guard pins it."* Built: three routes
(`listOrders`, which also serves `/orders/history`; `getOrder`; `listPendingOrders`)
select `provider:provider_id(name)` in the statement they were already making, resolved
through `procurement_orders_provider_id_fkey`
(`20260805000000_baseline_from_production.sql:13158`) — the only FK between the two
tables, so the embed needs no disambiguating hint. `OrderResponseDto.providerName` is
`string | null` on an optional property with the three-state rule the price pair
already uses: a name; `null` (joined, found nothing); the key ABSENT (this route does
not join). `mapOrderRow` decides which by testing whether the ROW carries
`provider_name`, not by testing its value. `apps/web/src/lib/mudavym/vendor.ts` is the
one place those three become words, so the surfaces cannot disagree about what a
missing vendor means.

**The second option was rejected and it is worth saying why:** resolving `providerId`
through `useProviders` per surface needed four more queries and the receiving door has
no providers query at all, so the door — the surface whose letter leaves the building —
would have been the one left unfixed.

The original entry, kept because it is the measurement:

`OrderResponseDto` carries `providerId` and no `providerName`. Four surfaces printed a
vendor clause off a key that was always `undefined`:

- `apps/web/src/pages/receiving/next/DoorModel.ts` — the credit-note draft is addressed
  **"To the vendor"** and has never named them, on every order the door has opened.
  This one leaves the building: it is a letter a receiver sends a distributor.
- `apps/web/src/pages/dashboard/next/WaitingOnYou.tsx` and `DayDetail.tsx` printed the
  literal word "vendor" on every row.
- `apps/web/src/pages/receipts/next/ReceiptsNext.tsx` — the pairing line's vendor clause
  never rendered.

Two fixes, and they are not equivalent: join `providers(name)` in `listOrders` /
`getOrder` and add `providerName` to the DTO (one place, four surfaces, one more embed
on a hot route); or have each surface resolve `providerId` through `useProviders`, as
`pages/orders/next` and `pages/orders/useOrdersPage.ts` already do (no gateway change,
four more queries, and the receiving door has no providers query at all). ~~*Blocker:
founder — a DTO shape change on a route four surfaces share.*~~ **Decided and built;
the first option.**

**What is NOT proven, said out loud.** The embed's cost was not measured with `EXPLAIN`.
The local gateway reads PRODUCTION and `GET /procurement/orders` answers **500,
`column procurement_order_items_1.price_uom does not exist`** there (ADR 0119 phase 1's
migration is unmerged), so no plan could be taken against a real page of orders, and no
PGlite fixture of this schema exists on this branch. What IS established is structural:
one to-ONE embed on `providers.id`, the table's primary key, inside the statement the
route already issues — so it adds a join over the page's own rows and NOT a round trip
per order. Treat that as an argument, not a measurement.

### 2. ~~No recurrence, so the rebuilt page's Recurring station is structurally empty~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-05

**Founder's decision, 2026-09-05 (batch 40):** *"Build recurrence on the order"* — a
recurrence rule on the order with its next date, and the seal on each recurrence's
approval. Recorded as an ADDENDUM to ADR 0125 (an order's states), because recurrence
adds no state to the twelve and no edge to the transition table.

~~`Order.recurrence` was declared and never sent. `useOrdersNextData.toRow` set
`recurring = !!o.recurrence`, so it has always been `false`: `OrdersNext.tsx:122`'s
"recurring" station shows nothing and every order falls into "one-time"
(`OrdersNext.tsx:123`). Nothing in `procurement_orders` records a recurrence today, so
this is a product decision before it is a wire one. *Blocker: founder.*~~

What shipped:

- **Nine columns on `procurement_orders`**
  (`supabase/migrations/20260905235800_an_order_that_repeats_says_so_on_itself.sql`):
  `recurrence_frequency` (the five members `recurring_orders.frequency` already has),
  `recurrence_anchor_day`, `recurrence_anchored_on`, `recurrence_next_due_on`,
  `recurrence_status` (active/paused/ended) with `recurrence_status_by` +
  `_at`, and `recurrence_parent_order_id` + `recurrence_occurrence_on` on each child.
  Four CHECKs, two FKs, a partial unique index over (parent, occurrence).
- **`is_recurring` and `cron_schedule` were already on the table and are DEAD.** Measured
  2026-09-05: `grep -rn "is_recurring\|cron_schedule" apps/api-gateway/src apps/web/src
  apps/mobile services/agent-orchestrator packages | grep -v node_modules` returns 13
  lines, of which **two** name `procurement_orders` and both are column inventories
  inside test fixtures (`providers/retroactive-order.spec.ts:248-249`,
  `procurement/verify-receipt.spec.ts:63,77`). Zero writers, zero readers, three
  languages. Both are tombstoned with a `COMMENT ON COLUMN` rather than written to.
- **Six DTO keys** (`recurrenceFrequency`, `…AnchorDay`, `…NextDueOn`, `…Status`,
  `…ParentOrderId`, `…OccurrenceOn`), mirrored on the web `Order` type and pinned by
  `scripts/check_web_reads_gateway_dto_keys.py`. Read with the key test, so **absent**
  (this route does not read recurrence) stays distinct from **null** (it read, and this
  order does not repeat) — the distinction whose absence caused this item.
- **The station fills, and says "none" only from a measured read.**
  `emptyStationSentence` (`apps/web/src/pages/orders/next/recurrence.ts`) is handed the
  row count and the count of rows that actually carried a reading, and prints one of four
  sentences. It will not say "there are none" off a book that never answered.
- **A generator on the existing morning cadence** (`@Cron("15 8 * * *")`,
  `order-recurrence.service.ts`) that mints ONE child per due series, born **PENDING** —
  a recurrence never auto-approves; each occurrence takes the ADR 0116 gate and the ADR
  0125 `approve` seal.

**Still open, and now filed as its own item — see "A recurring_orders schedule with
`auto_approve` spends money with no seal" below.**

The legacy desk half of this item is NOT closed: `mapApiOrderToUi` still never sets a
recurrence, so `Orders.tsx:2121`'s notification still always reads "Pending Approval".
The rebuilt page is the one the founder is shipping; the legacy desk is left as it was
rather than half-migrated. *Blocker: none; it is one mapping in `mapApiOrderToUi`.*

### 3. ~~`quantity_received` is a column the DTO does not map~~ — CLOSED 2026-09-05

**Founder's decision, 2026-09-05:** *"`quantityReceived` and its `uom` travel together
on the DTO (ADR 0070); mobile pre-fills from what was actually received."* Built as a
pair of keys with a pure reader, `apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/quantity-received-unit.ts`.

**What the column carries TODAY, re-measured on this tree** by
`grep -rn "quantity_received" apps/api-gateway/src` (4 write sites, none in a spec) —
the two-units defect filed above is NOT repaired and this entry does not claim it is:

| writer | unit it writes |
|---|---|
| `markDelivered` (`quantity_received: resolvedQuantity`) | the order's own `unit_type` |
| `updateOrder` (from `quantityReceivedInOrderUom`) | the order's own `unit_type` |
| `verifyReceipt` (`acceptedQty + rejectedQty`) | the COUNTED unit, which defaults to the order's |
| `recordDoorReceipt` (`receiving.service.ts:504`, `totals.receivedBottles`) | **bottles** |

Nothing on the row records which of the four wrote it, and `status` cannot stand in —
`updateOrder` writes the column under any status a caller sets, so PARTIALLY_RECEIVED
is not a door signature. So the DTO states the unit only where the arithmetic makes the
two agree: the readings differ by `toBottles(1, unit, packSize)`
(`documents/document-types.ts:227`), which is 1 for `bottle`, `each`, `keg` and `liter`
and the pack size for `case`, `pack` and `split_case`. A non-multiplying unit gets
`quantityReceivedUom` = the order's own unit; a multiplying one gets `null` and the
sentence in `QUANTITY_RECEIVED_UNIT_UNSTATED`. An ABSENT `unit_type` is `bottle` (the
column's own declared default and the identity of this arithmetic); an UNRECOGNISED one
is refused. That is ADR 0011's rule, applied to a read.

`apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/cellar/receive/[orderId].tsx` pre-fills from `quantityReceived`
ONLY when the wire states a unit and it is this order's — the screen counts in the
order's unit — and otherwise falls back to `quantity` and prints why under the count.

The original entry, kept because it is the measurement:

`procurement_orders.quantity_received` exists and `mapOrderRow`
(`procurement.service.ts:4119`) does not map it.
`apps/mobile/app/(tabs)/cellar/receive/[orderId].tsx` read `order.quantityReceived ??
order.quantity`, so the physical count pre-fills from the ORDERED quantity on a
partially-received order — the receiver counts against the wrong default. The read is
now `order.quantity` with the reason named. Note the unit hazard already filed above
(`quantity_received` has two units): adding it to the DTO must carry its unit with it.
~~*Blocker: the two-units defect must be settled first.*~~ **Decided and built without
settling it** — the DTO REPORTS the ambiguity instead of resolving it, so the column's
two units remain a live defect (the LIVE-DEFECT entry above still stands, unchanged) while no
screen any longer receives a count with no unit attached.

### 3b. A `recurring_orders` schedule with `auto_approve` spends money with no seal

Found 2026-09-05 while building recurrence on the order (item 2 above). **Not fixed here
— it is a different subsystem's decision, and it is the founder's.**

`recurring-orders.service.ts:888` (`executeRecurringOrder`):

```ts
if (recurringOrder.auto_approve) {
  await this.procurementService.approveOrder(
    recurringOrder.restaurant_id, order.id, userId,   // <- no `challenge` argument
  );
```

`approveOrder(restaurantId, orderId, userId, challenge?)` calls
`assertApprovalAllowed` and then `redeemOrderSeal`, and a `challenge` of `undefined`
is what a seal-less call looks like. So a `recurring_orders` row with `auto_approve =
true` moves an order to APPROVED — reserving shadow stock and committing the spend —
with nobody having held anything. That is the exact inverse of ADR 0116 and ADR 0125,
which exist because approving is the act that spends money.

Three facts that bound the blast radius, all measured:

- `auto_approve` **defaults to false** (`recurring-orders.service.ts:409`,
  `auto_approve: template.auto_approve ?? false`).
- Production held **0 rows** in `recurring_orders` on 2026-09-01, recorded in that
  file's own header comment — the create path was broken until
  `20260901180000_recurring_orders_shape.sql`. *(That count is quoted from the
  existing comment, not re-measured by this pass.)*
- The new recurrence-on-the-order path has **no `autoApprove` field at all**, by
  design — `dto/order-recurrence.dto.ts` says why, and the gateway runs
  `forbidNonWhitelisted`, so a body naming one is 400'd.

Two ways to close it, and they are not equivalent: make `approveOrder` refuse a call
with no challenge outright (correct, and it would break this caller loudly, which is
the point); or drop `auto_approve` from `recurring_orders` and route every schedule
through the PENDING-then-seal path the new recurrence uses. *Blocker: founder — the
second retires a shipped column.*

### 4. `CreateOrderRequest` / `UpdateOrderRequest` have the same disease, on the write side

`CreateOrderRequest` declares `wineId` and `unitPrice`; `CreateOrderDto`
(`procurement.dto.ts:35`) declares `inventoryId` and no `unitPrice`, and the gateway
runs `forbidNonWhitelisted`, so such a body would be **400'd**, not silently ignored.
`UpdateOrderRequest.unitPrice` is the same question against `UpdateOrderDto`. Not fixed
here for two reasons, both measured: `ordersApi.createOrder`, `useCreateOrder` and
`useOrdersData.createOrder` have **zero live callers** outside their own plumbing
(`grep -rn 'useCreateOrder\|createOrder(' apps/web/src`), and `CreateOrderDto` was being
extended by a concurrent pass while this ran. The guard's mapping table takes request
types as-is — the direction it checks (client key must exist on the DTO) is the correct
one for a request too. *Blocker: none; it is one more mapping-table row plus the
callers' shapes.*

### What the guard cannot see, recorded so nobody reads its green as more than it is

- **A widening cast puts a key back beyond its reach.** `raw as Order & { … }` is legal
  TypeScript and invisible to a guard that reads the declared type. One such
  intersection is live in `apps/web/src/pages/orders/next/useOrdersNextData.ts`, added
  by a concurrent pass to keep its file compiling while the shared type narrowed; its
  own comment says it is a no-op to delete now that the shared type has settled.
- **It compares key EXISTENCE, not type.** `Order.status` was the lowercase UI
  vocabulary while the wire sends `ProcurementOrderStatus` in SCREAMING_SNAKE — so
  `o.status === 'approved'` in `OneTapActionCenter` was false for every order ever
  fetched and that card's whole branch produced nothing. Fixed here by typing
  `Order.status` as `OrderWireStatus`, but the guard would not have caught it and will
  not catch the next one.
- **A test fixture cast as the shared type defeats both.** `{ … } as Order` in a test
  compiles with any keys at all; `WaitingOnYou.seal.test.tsx` passed for months on a
  fixture carrying six keys the wire cannot send.
5. **Every `menu_import` row is `classification_status = 'unclassified'`, and
   `scripts/check_beverage_kind_regression.py` has been red on that since
   2026-09-03.** Measured 2026-09-05 after the repair: **78** unclassified rows,
   all `source = 'menu_import'`, all `beverage_kind = 'unknown'`, all
   `primary_type = 'unknown'`. **77 of the 78 predate the repair** (the guard was
   already failing at 77); the 78th is the provisional row the repair created for
   Antalya's house white, left unclassified deliberately — nobody has said what
   that wine is, and `primary_type` is a vocabulary whose `'unknown'` means
   *unclassified*, so filling it would be the same fabrication in a different
   column. The guard is **not** wired into CI (`schema-parity.yml` runs
   `check_beverage_identity_parity.py`; `ci.yml` runs `test_synth_identity.py`;
   both pass). The real fault is upstream: the bulk-add path writes a library row
   without ever asking the classifier, so a venue's whole list arrives
   unclassified. Fix the writer, then re-run the guard over the 78.

   **WRITER FIXED 2026-09-22 ([ADR 0186](decisions/0186-a-menu-upload-classifies-every-drink-not-only-the-wine.md),
   `feat/menu-classify-beer`), BACKFILL STILL OPEN.** The upload path now
   carries the menu's own category to the row it creates, as
   `data_enrichment.menu_category` — the second input
   `wine_classify_beverage_kind()` was never being given
   (`apps/api-gateway/src/wines/wine-submissions.service.ts`,
   `resolveLibraryWinesBatch`). A menu upload therefore classifies beer,
   cider, sake, cocktails, spirits and non-alcoholic as well as wine, and the
   founder's "what this house pours" reveal reads them. Guarded by
   `apps/api-gateway/src/menus/a-beer-line-classifies-as-beer.spec.ts`, which
   parses this migration's own `WHEN` branches rather than restating them.
   **Three things this did not do, so nobody re-reads the entry as closed:**
   (a) the **78 existing rows are untouched** — a backfill is a data
   operation against the shared catalogue and has not been run, so the guard
   is still red; (b) `resolveOrCreateLibraryWine`, the single-row path used
   by `inventory.service.ts:1157` for a manual inventory add, still writes no
   `menu_category`; (c) the guard is still not wired into CI.

## Dev tooling — a conflict-marker guard that only read the planning corpus — FIXED 2026-09-05

`scripts/check_no_conflict_markers.py` was added 2026-09-01 after
`.planning/decisions/0032-vault-cleanup-cut-line.md` sat on `main` for days with
12 unresolved conflict blocks inside its Tombstone index. Its CI step
(`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, job `decision-claims`) was named *"No planning
document carries an unresolved conflict marker"* — but the script's own name, and
the way everyone read the step, promised the repository.

It scanned `.planning/` and nothing else. **Measured twice on 2026-09-05:** a
builder pushed `apps/web/src/pages/inventory/useInventoryPage.ts` carrying two
unresolved hunks and the guard printed

```
== Conflict markers: 1369 files scanned, none corrupt
PASS -- every planning document still parses as a document.
```

and exited 0. Reproduced deterministically with a staged probe file
(`apps/web/src/pages/inventory/__probe_conflict.ts`, three marker lines) against
the unmodified script: same PASS, same exit 0.

This is the absence-reported-as-health fault in its purest form.
Every number in that output was *true* — 1369 files really were scanned and
really were clean. The verdict it implied was false, because the denominator was
the wrong corpus and nothing in the output said so. A conflict marker in
TypeScript is exactly as much a broken file as one in Markdown, and it reaches
production faster.

**Fixed** by giving the guard two sections that both always run and report
separately, so neither can hide the other:

1. **Repository** — every tracked text file, enumerated with `git ls-files`
   (not a filesystem walk: the thing that must not carry a marker is the thing
   that gets committed). Binaries are dropped by extension *and* a NUL sniff of
   the first 8KB, so an extensionless binary cannot slip through on its name;
   lockfiles, `node_modules`, `dist`/`build`/`.next`/coverage are skipped and
   **counted as skipped in the output**, because the whole defect above was an
   unstated exclusion. 4267 files scan clean on `main` today.
2. **Planning corpus** — the original `.planning/` document-parse check, kept
   verbatim and reported under its own heading.

One true positive surfaced and was excluded deliberately:
`.planning/.obsidian/plugins/obsidian-git/main.js:371` is a vendored, minified
third-party bundle whose source embeds a fenced ` ```diff ` block showing the
user what a merge conflict looks like. Those marker lines are *documentation
inside a dependency*, not a broken file here. It is skipped via
`VENDORED_PREFIXES`, an exact path prefix rather than a `plugins/` directory
rule — "we ship someone else's bundle at this path" is a much narrower claim
than "skip any directory called plugins", and the narrow claim is the one that
stays true. A self-test invariant proves the skip does not leak to a same-named
path elsewhere.

Exit codes are unchanged in spirit and now cover the new failure: **0** clean,
**1** markers found (every one printed as `file:line`), **2** cannot check —
which now includes *git cannot enumerate the tracked files*, not only a missing
corpus. Exit 2 still blocks; a guard that could not read its corpus has not
passed.

`--self-test` grew from 9 invariants to 18. The load-bearing addition builds a
real temp git repo, commits a clean `.ts`, asserts exit 0, then stages a
conflicted `.ts` and asserts exit 1 *with the file named* — the exact miss of
2026-09-05, now proven to fire.

**Not verified:** no `paths:` filter exists on this workflow, so the step already
ran on every push and PR to `main`/`develop` — no trigger widening was needed,
and none was made. `scripts/` is outside the `lint-python` job's scope (it runs
`black`/`ruff` inside `services/agent-orchestrator` only), so the guard's
formatting is not CI-enforced; it was made `black 24.10.0` and `ruff 0.8.4` clean
by hand, which the pre-existing file was not.

## Canonical document — slice 3 stop 1 (corrections): three findings — added 2026-09-05

Found while building the correction door of ADR 0104 D5
(PR: `feat/canonical-document-slice-3-corrections`). All three were MEASURED, not reasoned.

1. **🔴 Two spec harnesses resolve every `Promise.all` read against the LAST table named — FIXED
   in the new spec, OPEN in the two that already existed.** The supabase mock in
   `apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/canonical/canonical-document.service.spec.ts:163` and
   `.../price-base-round-trip.spec.ts:256` keeps ONE shared `currentTable` and reads it *when the
   chain resolves*, not when `from()` is called. supabase-js chains are built eagerly and awaited
   later, so the moment a service issues two reads under `Promise.all` — as `readCorrections` now
   does — both chains come back with the answer canned for whichever table was named second. The
   first symptom was a correction-log fixture silently arriving as the revision fixture, which made
   four new assertions pass against an implementation that was not doing what they claimed. The new
   `document-correction.service.spec.ts` captures the table per chain
   (`makeChain(table)`); the two older files still share `currentTable` and are one `Promise.all`
   away from the same silent agreement. **This is the absence-as-health fault inside the test
   harness: the mock answered a question it had not been asked, and the suite went green.**

2. **`kasa` and `koli` ARE readable units — the first draft of the "refuse an unconvertible unit"
   test asserted the opposite.** `normalizeUom`
   (`apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/documents/document-types.ts:156`) folds the Turkish words for
   a shipping case to `case`, and `şişe`/`adet` likewise. A test written from the assumption that a
   Turkish word is unreadable would have passed for the wrong reason and would have started failing
   the day someone *removed* that folding. Measured, not assumed; the test now uses a word the
   normaliser genuinely cannot read.

3. **The next revision number was computed in two places, and they disagreed by one.** The
   correction door needs the number BEFORE the row exists (the corrected field's envelope carries
   its own `revision`), while `persistRevision` computed its own from `max(revision)`. On a document
   with no revision rows the envelope claimed 2 and the row was written as 1. Fixed by giving
   `CanonicalDocumentService` one public `nextRevision()` that both callers use, and by having the
   first correction write the pre-correction document down as revision 1 before taking revision 2 —
   so the log opens with what the machine read rather than with somebody's correction of it.

**Deviation from the brief, stated (CLAUDE.md §0.5).** The brief asked for `verified_by` /
`verified_at` to be "preserved from the prior revision" on a correction. They are preserved when the
correction RESTATES the same value and CLEARED when it changes it — carrying a tick onto a different
number would print *"Verified by Ayşe"* beside a figure Ayşe never saw, which is a fabricated human
assertion. The old tick is not lost: it is inside the append-only correction row's `before`. ADR 0104
D5 itself says only "a `verified_by` tick per field" and does not decide this; if the founder wants
literal preservation it is a one-line change in
`document-correction.service.ts` and a test that already names both directions.

**Also stated:** `buildFromDocumentId` reads the latest revision NUMBER and replays the
`document_corrections` log, rather than serving the stored `document_revisions.layer1` blob whole.
The blob is still written on every correction (it is the append-only record and slice 4's input),
but serving it would freeze a document against a later re-extraction — the rows would change and the
page would keep showing the layer 1 captured at correction time. Replaying the log keeps a new
extraction visible and the human's corrections applied on top.

---

## Canonical document — slice 3 stop 2 (deliveries, proposals, the gates): six findings — added 2026-09-05

Found while opening the delivery's doors (ADR 0103 D1/D3/D6/D7/D9, A2/A4/A6/A10;
PR branch `feat/canonical-document-slice-3-deliveries`). All six were MEASURED.

1. **🔴 `procurement_order_items` has no `product_name` and no `unit_price`.** The first
   draft of the "this delivery differs from your order on N lines" comparison selected both.
   PostgREST answers 42703 for the WHOLE select when one name is wrong, so the comparison
   would have failed on every delivery and the method would have reported *"the comparison
   could not run"* for ever — an absence that reads as a system behaving cautiously. The
   table has `wine_name`, `vendor_sku`, `quantity` × `bottles_per_unit` (with a generated
   `total_bottles`), `quoted_unit_price` and `final_unit_price`. Caught by
   `check_read_columns_exist.py` before it shipped; the read now names the real columns and
   falls back to `quantity × bottles_per_unit` for a row written before `total_bottles`
   existed.

2. **🔴 THREE branches claimed migration version `20260905120000`, and one was invisible.**
   This slice's stop-1 migration, #310's `simpos_behaves_like_a_pos`, AND an unpushed
   `20260905120000_a_house_names_its_money.sql` in the p4 worktree — which `gh pr list`
   cannot see at all. Two files with one version break the auto-apply on merge. The sweep
   that finds all three is: open PRs (`gh pr diff <n> --name-only`), every origin branch
   (`git diff origin/main...<branch>`), **and every `git worktree list` checkout**, because
   the third only exists on a local disk. Stop 1 renumbered to `20260905231500` and stop 2
   took `20260905232000`, above all seventeen versions the sweep found; the odd minute is
   deliberate, since a round hour is what two agents pick independently.

3. **`delivery_proposals.qty_proposed` has been UNITLESS since slice 1.**
   `check_quantity_units.py` refused the DTO field: *"`qtyProposed` is a quantity that does
   not say what unit it is in … a comment is not a declaration: JSON does not carry
   comments, which is how `rejectedQty` came to book 33 bottles of live stock for a refused
   delivery."* The field is now `qtyProposedBottles` and the COLUMN carries a comment saying
   the same thing, so the next reader of the table does not have to find the DTO.

4. **`delivery_proposals.proposed_by` collides with ADR 0059's `proposed_*` convention.**
   `check_proposal_preservation.py` protects any `proposed_*` column as a machine's
   suggestion that a human answer must never overwrite. This one is the opposite: ADR 0103
   D7's table holds POSITIONS EITHER SIDE PUT ON THE RECORD, and `proposed_by` is the person
   who filed one. Allowlisted with that reason written into the guard — the mechanism
   working as designed, not a hole opened.

5. **`DeliveryClockService.schedule` could return a timer it never received.** `.single()`
   with `error: null` and `data: null` should be impossible, and the first draft dereferenced
   it anyway; a test caught the TypeError. It now returns a named failure, because reporting
   a scheduled clock that does not exist is the worst possible shape for a legal deadline.

6. **Still ZERO writers: `inventory_lots.cost_state` and `inventory_transactions.delivery_id`.**
   Measured across `apps/api-gateway/src` and `services/` on this tree, as in slice 1. ADR
   0103 A1 wants on-hand at `DELIVERED` marked `provisional` and cost final at `VERIFIED`;
   with no writer for either column, a `verify()` that set `cost_state = final` would be the
   ONLY writer — marking lots final that nothing ever marked provisional, i.e. asserting a
   cost state about a booking this code never made. `verify()` therefore writes no stock and
   says so in its response, and **consolidating `recordDoorReceipt` / `markDelivered` onto
   the delivery (ADR 0103 A5) is the next stop, named rather than half-done.**

7. **🔴 D8's headline notification was ORDER-ONLY, and the sim tenant has ZERO purchase orders.**
   Measured 2026-09-05 (`GET /procurement/orders` → `{"orders":[],"total":0}`): the founder's
   asked-for *"this delivery differs from your order on N lines"* could not fire on ANY delivery
   there, because the first draft returned `null` the moment `order_id` was null. That silences
   the notification on exactly the case ADR 0103 D5 exists for — an UNORDERED delivery, where
   nobody has a prior number and the door count against the vendor's own document is the only
   comparison available. **Fixed in the same PR:** the comparison now chooses a basis and the
   sentence says which one it used — the order when one preceded the goods, otherwise our count
   against the vendor's paperwork. `null` still means "we did not compare" and `0` still means
   "compared, nothing differed"; three tests hold the difference, and the first draft's
   behaviour turns one of them red.

**Also open, named:** the D9 ladder's escalation goes to the delivery's OWNER at 50 % and
broadcasts at 80 %; the DEPUTY column is read but the 80 % rung does not yet address the
deputy specifically (`persistForRestaurant` has `onlyUserIds`, and wiring deputy-then-venue-
owner is a small follow-up). And the Turkish `response_window` / `invoice_issuance` rows in
`vendor_terms` remain UNSEEDED per ADR 0103 A8 — every Turkish delivery therefore gets a
`blocked_unknown` timer that asks rather than a deadline that lies. That is the designed
behaviour, and it means a TR venue sees no 7-day countdown until a YMM answers.
