## Two type-checks agreeing about a file neither of them reads — added 2026-09-05

Found when `Lint TypeScript` went red on a branch that had passed every local check
(slice 3, PRs #309 and #315):

```
documents.controller.spec.ts(41,18): error TS2554: Expected 5 arguments, but got 4.
canonical-route.spec.ts(110,18):    error TS2554: Expected 6 arguments, but got 5.
```

Two specs construct `DocumentsController` **directly** — deliberately, so the class-level
`JwtAuthGuard`'s own dependency chain is not dragged into a test about route behaviour — and
the controller gained constructor dependencies. The specs were stubbing too few.

**Why nothing local caught it, which is the finding rather than the fix.**

- `apps/api-gateway/tsconfig.json` **excludes `*.spec.ts`**, so `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json`
  — the command this repo's sessions reach for — is clean while a spec is one argument short.
  Only `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.spec.json` reads them, and CI's `Lint TypeScript` is the only
  place that runs.
- **Jest does not typecheck.** It transpiles each file with swc; `new C(a, b, c, d)` against a
  five-argument constructor runs fine, because the missing dependency is `undefined` and the
  routes under test never touch it. The suite was green.

So a green `tsc` and a green `jest` were two checks **agreeing about a file neither of them was
reading** — the same shape as this repository's standing fault, one level up: not a system
reporting absence as health, but two systems whose union looks like coverage and whose
intersection is empty.

**The habit, until something mechanises it:** after touching a controller or service
constructor, run `npx tsc --noEmit -p apps/api-gateway/tsconfig.spec.json`, not just
`-p tsconfig.json`. Each stub in those two specs now carries the name of the dependency it
stands in for, so the next argument is a one-line edit rather than a puzzle. A guard that
simply ran the spec typecheck in the same pre-push sweep as the others would close it; that is
not built here, and saying so is the point.

## Vendor lens finding 5 (V5) — an acceptance that answered nothing — CLOSED — 2026-09-06

### 5. 🔴 → ✅ `accept-as-billed` accepted a line with no recorded difference — CLOSED here

**Measured live**, sim tenant `a229f22b-2aac-4e54-a8b2-033a8f93ac5e`, gateway built from `main`
`412fd9d8` on :4010, through the product doors only. Delivery
`f3771f3d-c805-47c2-af63-23a7f0b1c685`, door count `ac19b667…` (line 1 = 10 against a billed
12), invoice `b1e02edf…` linked.

`POST …/accept-as-billed { documentId: b1e02edf (the INVOICE), lineNo: 1 }` answered **201**
`alreadyAccepted:false`, and a second identical call **201** `alreadyAccepted:true` — yet
`agree` still answered **409** naming line 1 of the DOOR COUNT document. The acceptance was
keyed by a (document, line) that carried no recorded difference, so it answered nothing while
the door said "accepted". This is the standing fault of this repository in its purest form
(memory `absence-reported-as-health`): silence wearing the shape of an answer. Re-keyed by the
door-count document the same call answered 201 and `agree` then answered **201 AGREED** under
rule `both_sides_recorded`, `verify` **201 VERIFIED** — so the A11 gate itself was sound; only
the door that answers a difference was not.

Fixed in `DeliveryService.acceptAsBilled`: the (documentId, lineNo) must match a difference a
comparison actually recorded on that delivery, or the call is refused **409** and the sentence
names the differences that CAN be answered, with their document id, line and both quantities,
so the caller learns the key rather than guessing it. The key is deliberately **not** widened
to "any document on the delivery" — a difference has one home and the answer must land there.
Both doors now read the same list through one new private `recordedDifferences()`, which
`unansweredDifferences()` also uses; an `unreadable` comparison fails the acceptance rather
than reading as "nothing to answer" (ADR 0067).

Evidence: three new tests in `apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/canonical/delivery.service.spec.ts`
(wrong key names the answerable difference; a delivery with no recorded difference at all;
an unreadable comparison refuses), all three proven failing against the pre-fix service
(`Tests: 3 failed, 5 passed` on the A11 describe); the whole procurement suite is
`39 passed, 707 tests` after.

**Live confirmations from the same run**, recorded against their entries above:
- Finding **4** (repeated door count → 409): confirmed live — the identical count re-posted
  answered `409 "This count was already recorded as document ac19b667…"`.
- Finding **1** / ADR 0103 **A11** (a difference must be answered): confirmed live — `agree`
  with the difference unanswered answered `409 "1 recorded difference(s) have no answer — line
  1 of document ac19b667… (Sentetik Öküzgözü 2021 · 750 ml: 10 against 12)"`, and answered 201
  once the difference was genuinely answered.

**Not cleaned up, deliberately:** the acceptance row written against the wrong key
(`24fdf1d0-54f2-4636-a62b-2fd2dd407fb9`, delivery `f3771f3d…`, document `b1e02edf…` line 1) is
still on the sim tenant. It is harmless — it is a real human acceptance with a reason, on a
sim tenant, and it answers nothing that any gate reads — and deleting it would remove the
evidence of the defect from the tree that produced it. Left in place, and named here so nobody
later reads it as a real answer.

**Re-driven live 2026-09-06 after #331 merged** (main `249e961c`, sim tenant, doors only): the wrong key → 409 naming the one answerable difference; the right key → 201; agree → AGREED. The closure is measured, not only tested.

## Vendor lens findings 1, 3 and 4 — CLOSED — 2026-09-06

The four findings themselves are filed by the stop-3 lens PR (#329, "Vendor lens through the
delivery doors — slice 3 stop 3"); this section records what closed them and with what
evidence. Finding **2** stays **OPEN** (see below).

### 1. 🔴 → ✅ A delivery that DIFFERS agrees itself in one call — CLOSED by ADR 0103 **A11**

The founder amended D3 in session on 2026-09-06, verbatim: _"Difference must be answered —
AGREED is refused while any recorded difference (door count vs paperwork, or invoice vs PO)
has no accepted proposal or an explicit 'accept as billed' from the restaurant. Rule A stays
for deliveries with no difference."_

Built: `agree()` calls `unansweredDifferences()` **before either D3 rule** and refuses with
**409** naming every unanswered line ("line 1 of document <id> (SYNTHETIC …: 12 against 10)").
Answers are an accepted proposal covering the line, or the new door
`POST /procurement/deliveries/:id/accept-as-billed { documentId, lineNo, reason }`
(`delivery_line_acceptances`, migration `20260906163412`). The gate and the
`delivery_differs` notification read **one** comparison — `scanDifferences` — so the two can
never disagree, and that comparison has **three** answers: `compared`, `not_comparable`
(rule A untouched) and `unreadable` (refuse, 500). Rule B is gated too: a per-vendor
`signed_ticket_is_final` must not be a setting that walks past the gate.

Evidence: 12 new tests in `delivery.service.spec.ts`; the three gate tests proven failing-first
against a sabotaged gate (`3 failed, 38 passed` with the gate stubbed out; `41 passed` with it
in). Six SQL assertions on a Docker build of the migrations: all six false or erroring on a
105-migration control, all six true on the 106-migration build.

### 2. 🔴 `inventory_lots.cost_state` is `DEFAULT 'final'` with no writer — **STILL OPEN**

Untouched here, deliberately. The column is a *stock and cost booking* fact, and ADR 0103 A5's
consolidation of `recordDoorReceipt` / `markDelivered` onto the delivery is the stop that gets
to write it. Changing the default without the writer would swap one absence-as-health shape
(everything reads `final`) for another (everything reads `provisional` for ever). It belongs to
the A5 stop and is named there rather than half-done here.

### 3. 🟠 → ✅ Our door count rendered under "Billed" — CLOSED

Fixed in the **mapper**, not the page: `canonicalFromParsedDocument` now puts a
`receiving_advice`'s quantities in `received` and leaves `billed` NULL, because the columns are
read by the page, the verdict sentences and the API alike and correcting one of the three would
have left the other two saying the old thing. `hasComparisonSource` no longer reads a count as
its own comparison, and the "not compared" sentence says _counted N at the door_ rather than
_billed —_ on a document that carries no money at all (ADR 0104 D11). Evidence: a new mapper
test that fails on `origin/main`'s mapper (`1 failed, 38 passed`) and passes here, plus two web
tests.

### 4. 🟡 → ✅ A repeated door count leaked `uq_pd_restaurant_sha256` — CLOSED

`recordDoorCount` now checks the content hash before inserting and returns the EXISTING
document; the controller answers **409 Conflict** — "This count was already recorded as
document <id>…" — rather than 422. 409 because the content is not the problem: it is a second
request for a state that already exists, and a caller can tell it from a malformed body
without parsing prose. The unique index is still what makes the guarantee: a 23505 from the
race the pre-check cannot close comes back through the same sentence, and a duplicate check
that FAILS refuses the write rather than proceeding without it. Evidence: three new tests in
`door-count.spec.ts`, all three failing against `origin/main`'s intake service.

## Vendor lens through the delivery doors — slice 3 stop 3 — four findings — added 2026-09-06

One pass on the sim tenant (Sim Meyhouse `a229f22b-2aac-4e54-a8b2-033a8f93ac5e`), through the
product doors only, against a gateway built from `origin/main` at `417474e6` on :4020. No
seeded rows and no direct DB writes; the DB was read with the service role only to *check*
what the doors claimed. Two deliveries were made from door counts of the Turkish second-version
invoice `b1e02edf` (line 1 counted 10 of 12), with the invoice and delivery note `dac9a3e8`
linked to each. Screenshots: `scratchpad/lens-vendor/shots/` (listed in the stop-3 PR body).

### 1. 🔴 A delivery that DIFFERS from the vendor's own paperwork agrees itself in one call

Delivery `2e26cc6a` was created from a door count that disagreed with the linked invoice on
line 1 (10 counted against 12 billed). The gateway itself said so — `delivery_differs`,
"This delivery differs from the vendor's paperwork on 1 line(s)", persisted at 15:53:53Z. The
very next call, `POST /procurement/deliveries/2e26cc6a/agree`, returned **201** with
`agreed_rule: "both_sides_recorded"` and moved the delivery to AGREED. No proposal had been
filed and no human had reconciled anything.

D3 rule A asks only whether *a* restaurant position and *a* vendor position exist and no
proposal is open. A door count IS the restaurant's position and the invoice IS the vendor's;
the fact that the two **contradict each other on a line the system had already flagged** is
not part of the test. So the one case the whole ADR exists for — a short ship — is the case
that can pass the gate without anyone disputing it.

This is not a refusal-message problem; a refusal would be wrong too. The honest shape is that
rule A should require the recorded difference to be *answered* (accepted, countered or
explicitly waived), exactly as an open proposal already blocks it. Filed, not fixed: changing
the D3 predicate is an ADR 0103 amendment, not a stop-3 patch.

### 2. ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-06 — `inventory_lots.cost_state` was `DEFAULT 'final' NOT NULL` with no writer

Measured on production: `information_schema` gives `cost_state` `column_default 'final'::text`,
`is_nullable NO`; **165 of 165 lots across the whole database, and 54 of 54 on the sim tenant,
read `final`**, newest `updated_at` 2026-09-03 — before this lens ran. ADR 0103's own note that
the column "has no writer at all" is correct, and that is precisely the problem: with a `final`
default and no writer, every lot asserts that its cost is settled, whether or not any delivery
was ever agreed or verified. This is the absence-reported-as-health shape at the row level —
a cost state nobody computed reads exactly like one somebody did. The verify door is honest
about not writing it; the column is not honest about never having been written.

**CLOSED by the A1/A5 stop (PR on `feat/stock-at-the-door-cost-at-verified`, migration
`20260906233000`).** The column now has BOTH halves it was missing: a default that asserts
nothing (`provisional`) and two writers that assert something. `apply_stock_movement` writes
`provisional` for anything booked against a delivery and for any lot whose price is an
estimate, and `final` only where a price and an invoice/manual provenance stand behind it;
`finalise_delivery_cost` flips a delivery's lots to `final` at VERIFIED with the agreed price,
writing the prior cost to `inventory_lot_revaluations` first and touching no quantity.
**Measured before the change, read-only on production:** 165 lots, 165 reading `final`, of
which **162 carry no `unit_cost` at all** (`cost_provenance = estimated`) and 3 carry a
`manual` price. The backfill restates those 162 as `provisional` and leaves the 3 `final` —
the alternative rule ("everything no delivery ever verified") would have restated all 165,
including the three a person priced by hand, since no delivery has ever verified anything.
Proven on a 106-migration control and a 107-migration build: on the control a lot booked at
the door reads `final` and an `estimated` lot reads `final`; `inventory_lots.delivery_id` and
`finalise_delivery_cost` do not exist there.

**Named, not fixed, in the same stop — CLOSED 2026-09-11 by ADR 0104 slice 4
(`feat/canonical-document-slice-4-mapping-memory`):** an invoice line carried no
`inventory_id`, so when a delivery booked more than one item and the invoice lines named none
of them, no agreed price could be attached to a specific lot and those lots stayed
`provisional` — stated in the verify response's `costNote`, never guessed by matching a
description.

**How it closed, and how it deliberately did NOT.** The fix is not line-to-item resolution on
extraction: ADR 0103 A12 rejected description matching as a booking rule, and an extractor
that resolves the shelf is that rule wearing a different hat. It closed instead through
`POST /procurement/documents/:id/lines/:lineId/link-item` — a person names the shelf once, the
line carries `inventory_id` from then on, and migration `20260907120000` appends the act to
`document_line_mappings` so the NEXT document from the same vendor carries the shelf as a
proposal a person ticks. Nothing is booked or costed from the proposal.

Evidence: 12 SQL assertions (T1–T11 plus T3b) failing or erroring on a 107-migration control
and passing on the 108-migration build; 28 gateway tests and 7 web tests, eleven of them
proven failing-first by sabotage. The canonical read also stopped ignoring the `inventory_id`
the door had been writing since `20260906233000` — a line a person HAD linked rendered as
unlinked and they were asked again, which is the same defect one layer up.

**Filed new (2026-09-11, slice 4):** the shelf-link door writes the line and then appends the
memory; if the memory write fails the link still stands and the response says so
(`remembered: false` plus the reason). That is the honest shape, but it means a memory can be
one act behind the lines — there is no reconciler that replays lines carrying an
`inventory_id` into the memory. Owed if it is ever observed; not observed.

**Filed new (2026-09-11, slice 4) — and MEASURED, which changes how big it is:** a document
with no `provider_id` teaches the memory nothing (there is nobody to remember the pairing for)
and the door says so rather than writing a provider-less row. That is the right refusal, but
the pre-merge live drive found the condition is not an edge case: **0 of 15
`procurement_documents` on the sim tenant carry a `provider_id`**, so the mapping memory is
consulted on none of them and proposes nothing on any document that tenant holds today.

The cause is upstream of slice 4. `provider_id` is written only from what the CALLER supplies
(`document-intake.service.ts:320`, `:726`, `:790`); **intake never resolves a vendor from the
document's own seller name**, although the canonical read shows one — invoice `b1e02edf` reads
seller *"SENTETİK ŞARAP DAĞITIM A.Ş."* with `layer2.providerId: null` beside it. Until a
document can acquire its vendor, the memory is inert for every document uploaded without one.

**Owed:** resolve `provider_id` at intake from the seller party (a named, reviewable step — NOT
a silent match), or let a person name the vendor on the canonical page. Slice 4 deliberately
does not do this: guessing a vendor to key a memory on would put a guess underneath every
future proposal, which is the failure ADR 0103 A12 rejected one layer down.

**CLOSED (2026-09-11, ADR 0104 D15, PR #351)** — and closed in a way the "named, reviewable
step" framing above got slightly wrong. The founder's instruction was to *remove* the human,
not to insert one, so resolution is by IDENTITY and the human disappears from the good path
entirely: the seller's printed tax id (BT-31/BT-32) normalised to `<COUNTRY>:<VALUE>`, matched
against `providers.tax_id_normalized` under a partial unique index on
`(restaurant_id, tax_id_normalized)`. Exactly one → `matched`. None → the provider is CREATED
from the printed identity, flagged `provisional_until_first_order`. Anything weaker →
`unresolved` with the reason in words, and nothing written. Name similarity is not a rule and
does not become one, so the A12 failure this entry feared is avoided by construction rather
than by a person ratifying a guess.

Evidence, pre-merge live drive on the sim tenant (gateway :4030, `lens-slice4b/d15-live.json`):
six documents through the real upload + extraction doors, **6/6 as designed** — a printed VKN
and a printed EIN both `unavailable` (the new columns are not on the sim database until this
merges, and blaming the paper for that would have been `unresolved`); no id printed,
a VKN failing its check digit, a seller id equal to the buyer's (self-billed), and digits with
no country anywhere all `unresolved`, each with its own sentence. The canonical read of
invoice `b1e02edf` answers `vendorResolution.state: unavailable` naming the missing log table
rather than drawing a blank, and **BT-31 renders live** (`"VKN: 1234567890"`, `as_printed`
intact, `source: extracted`) on a document whose vendor did not resolve — the fallback half.

**Still owed after this, and it is the honest remainder:** `matched` and `created` have been
proven on the Docker build (15 SQL assertions) and in 49 unit tests, but **never live**, because
the sim database does not carry `providers.tax_id_normalized` or `document_vendor_resolutions`
until this migration applies. The 15 documents already on that tenant also carry no
`vendorTaxId` in their `extracted` snapshot — they were read before the contract had the field
— so re-driving them after the merge will produce `unresolved`, correctly, and the first real
`matched` needs a document read through the new contract.

**Also found and fixed in the same stop (live, 2026-09-06):** a failure in the booking step
made `POST /procurement/documents/door-count` answer **500 with no `deliveryId`**, although
the count and the delivery were both already durable — leaving the receiver to press the
button again and get a 409 on the content hash. The endpoint now answers 201 with the ids and
carries the booking's failure inside the receipt (`failed: true`, `bottlesMoved: 0`, the
reason in words).

**And one the pre-merge drive could not reach (found 2026-09-06 after the merge, fixed on
`fix/an-accepted-price-finds-its-item`):** `agreedPrices` resolved an accepted proposal's line
**only among invoice lines**, while `recordedDifferences` keys a difference to the document the
comparison found it on — the door count, which is the one document carrying `inventory_id`. A
delivery whose price *was* agreed therefore verified with *"no agreed price reaches this item"*
and its lots stayed provisional. Every attached document is now indexed for the lookup, and
only an invoice line is ever read as a price.

### 3. 🟠 The canonical document renders OUR door count under "Billed", and its own lines as "not counted"

On `/documents/54de12fb` (the door-count `receiving_advice` of delivery `65e450cf`) the line
table puts the counted quantities — 10, 24, 6 — in the **Billed** column, leaves **Received**
reading "not counted" on all three lines, and heads each verdict card "NOT COMPARED · LINE n …
billed 10 bottle. Nothing was ordered, despatched or counted against it." On the invoice's own
page the same three lines also read "not counted" although a door count sits on the same
delivery. The spine card for the count says "COUNTED AT THE DOOR / number not read".

Nothing here is *false* in the four-way sense (each column is sourced from a document role),
but on our own count the count is not in the received column, and a receiver reading this page
is told the delivery was not counted moments after counting it. Screenshots
`08-gates-and-thread.png`, `02-delivery-spine.png`.

### 4. 🟡 A repeated door count fails with a raw Postgres constraint name

Submitting the identical count twice returns **422** with
`the door count could not be recorded: duplicate key value violates unique constraint
"uq_pd_restaurant_sha256"`. Content-hash de-duplication is right; leaking the constraint name
to a receiver is not, and the message does not say the sentence that matters — *this exact
count is already recorded, here it is*. It also cannot be distinguished by a caller from any
other 422.

### What was measured and found HEALTHY (recorded so the absence is not read as a gap)

- **Verify writes no stock and no cost — confirmed by query, not by the door's own words.**
  `inventory_transactions` with `delivery_id` in the two deliveries: **0**. Rows for the sim
  tenant in the last 3 hours: **0**. Lots touched in 3 hours: **0**. The response sentence
  ("Nothing was posted to inventory or cost…") matches the database. A1/A5 hold on this build.
- The proposal thread records what was sent: `qty_proposed 10`, `money_at_risk 142`,
  `evidence` carrying the photo document id, the counter's `counters_proposal_id` pointing at
  the proposal it answers, and the answered row moved to `countered` with both rows kept.
- Gates refuse the wrong way with reasons, not codes — see the stop-3 PR body for the four
  statuses and messages.

## Vendor lens finding 6 (V6) — the item activity door was blind to delivery bookings — CLOSED — 2026-09-06

### 6. 🔴 → ✅ `GET /inventory/:rid/item/:itemId/activity` reported nothing for a booked delivery — CLOSED here

**Measured** (sim tenant `a229f22b-…`, gateway = main `d23794ff`, product doors only). A door
count with `createDelivery: true` answered 201 with `booking.booked[0] = {delta: 10,
transactionId: "ec93b44d-…"}`; the item read `stock_live` 17 → 27; the activity door then
answered **200 with every day at `out: 0` and `totalOut28d: 1`** — the ten bottles appear
nowhere in the payload. Log:
`scratchpad/lens-vendor/a5-live-2.json`, step "item activity".

**Cause, not symptom.** The reader was depletion-only and had no inbound term at all:
`inventory.service.ts:672` (pre-fix) computed
`isOut = qty < 0 || ["sale","pour","glass_pour"].includes(type)` and `continue`d on
everything else, so the booking's row — written by `apply_stock_movement` with
`quantity_change = +10`, `transaction_type = 'purchase'`, `reference_type = 'delivery'`
(`delivery-stock.service.ts:452-467`; the insert is
`supabase/migrations/20260906233000_stock_at_the_door_cost_at_verified.sql:251`) — was read,
matched, and dropped. The response had no field an inbound movement could have appeared in,
so a shelf that had just gained ten bottles was indistinguishable from one nothing had
touched. This is the **O (silent omission)** shape of [[absence-reported-as-health]]: the
row exists and is correct; the door simply does not report it.

**Two more faults found in the same read, fixed with it.**
- The failed-read path returned `{daily: [], heat: [], totalOut28d: 0}` — an outage rendered
  as "nothing moved on this shelf". It now raises, and the controller turns that into a 500
  (ADR 0067).
- The query filtered on `inventory_id` alone. `restaurantId` was accepted by the route and
  never used; it is now an `.eq("restaurant_id", restaurantId)`.

**Other writers, measured by reading them.** POS sales write `p_delta: -qty` and were already
visible as OUT (`pos-hub.service.ts:838`). What was *also* invisible and is now counted:
POS voids/returns (`p_delta: +qty`, type `return`, same line), positive manual adjustments,
and transfers in. No separate change was needed — the one inbound branch covers them.

**Fixed:** `apps/api-gateway/src/inventory/inventory.service.ts` — `daily[].in`, `totalIn28d`,
and an `includes` block that states in words what the door counted, so a zeroed series reads
as "these movements did not happen" rather than "this kind of movement is not looked at".
The heatmap stays depletion-only on purpose: it answers "when do we pour this", and a truck
at 17:00 is not a busy hour.

**Test:** `apps/api-gateway/src/inventory/item-activity-reads-delivery-bookings.spec.ts`.
It books through `DeliveryStockService.bookAtTheDoor` and derives the ledger row from the
arguments the writer actually sent to `apply_stock_movement`, so a change in the writer
breaks the test rather than passing it. Red 3/3 before, green 3/3 after (275/275 across
`src/inventory` + `src/procurement/canonical`).

**Read back live 2026-09-06 after #336 merged** (main `fed2b7ca`, sim tenant): `totalIn28d` 28 where the pre-fix door said 0. Closed on measurement.

## Retire `recordDoorReceipt` / `markDelivered` once their three clients move — OPEN — 2026-09-06

ADR 0103 A12 demoted the two older stock writers to non-second-writers (PR #333) instead of deleting them, because the web offline outbox, the orders screen and mobile still call them. Owed: move those three callers onto the one booking path (`delivery-line:{delivery}:{document}:{line}`), then delete both functions and tighten `check_no_direct_stock_writes.sh` so the allow-list shrinks by two. Until then the product has one booking path and two doors that refuse to be a second one — measured, not assumed, by the A5 tests.

## The item activity door is blind to delivery bookings — CLOSED by PR #336 (see the closed section below) — 2026-09-06

Measured live after #333 (main `d23794ff`, sim tenant): a door count booked 10 bottles onto item `a23a4595` (transaction `ec93b44d`, `stock_live` 17 → 27), and `GET /inventory/:rid/item/:id/activity` answered **0 rows**. The stock moved and the page that explains stock movements says nothing happened — absence reported as health on a read path. Fix: the activity read must include `inventory_transactions` with `reference_type = 'delivery'` (and any other reference type the one booking path writes), with a failing-first test that books through the door and reads the activity back.

## Three seal migrations read the kind list with a character class that can drop a kind — OPEN — 2026-09-11

Found by the adversarial audit of `b6d2e4b4` and fixed in ONE file only. The seal's
`chk_mcp_seal_challenges_subject_kind` CHECK is widened by read-and-append: a migration reads
the kinds the constraint admits, adds its own and writes the union back. Three of those
migrations read them with `regexp_matches(existing_def, '''([a-z_]+)''', 'g')`, at five
sites — `20260905225000_a_message_is_metered_before_it_is_billed.sql:338` and `:469`,
`20260905233000_a_house_keeps_its_own_copy_of_its_mail.sql:440`, and
`20260906070000_a_series_is_armed_by_a_person.sql:188` and `:335`. A character class silently
DROPS any existing kind whose name holds a digit or a capital: the parse skips it, the union
is written back without it, and the `< 4` floor cannot notice because the other kinds were
read. The gateway then declares a kind the database refuses, which reads as a code bug.

`20260906200000_a_document_act_takes_a_redeemed_seal.sql` was corrected in place: it reads
every quoted literal, unescapes a doubled quote, and asserts the rebuilt constraint holds
exactly the kinds it read plus its own (`:71`, `:108`, `:161`).

**CORRECTED 2026-09-12: it is no longer branch-only.** It was the last of the nineteen
migrations a defect in `20260906020000` had blocked, and it went to production through the
connector on 2026-09-12 — verified by query, it has a row in
`supabase_migrations.schema_migrations`. So the remediation sentence below ("the three files
are already applied on `main`, so the fix is a NEW migration, not an edit to the three") now
governs **four** files, this one included. Two further things follow that were not true when
this entry was written: its `DO $$` block's probe `DELETE FROM public.mcp_seal_challenges
WHERE args_hash = 'migration probe'` (`:201`, `:216`) has RUN against the live table, scoped
by a magic string rather than by a returned primary key; and any future re-apply of it runs
that DELETE again. Carried as a session task, NOT filed as a row here or in `OPEN-DECISIONS.md` -- saying "filed" would assert a filing that does not exist.

**Latent today, and that is measured, not assumed.** A scan of every `subject_kind` literal
in `supabase/migrations/*.sql` finds 8 distinct kinds and **none holds a digit or a
capital**, so nothing has been dropped yet. The cost of leaving it: the first kind anybody
names with a digit or a capital — `iso4217_upload`, `posV2_sync` — is dropped silently by
whichever of these three runs next, and the failure surfaces as a 23514 on an INSERT far from
the cause. The three files are already applied on `main`, so the fix is a NEW migration that
rewrites the constraint from a full-literal parse (not an edit to the three), plus the
behavioural probe shape: INSERT one row per kind after applying, rather than comparing
constraint TEXT with the same class the migration used — a text check that shares the
migration's blind spot reports health while the kind is gone.
## Slice 4 live re-drive: three small read-shape defects — CLOSED by `fix/slice-4-read-shapes` — 2026-09-11

Measured on main `a1e69559` (sim tenant, doors only; log `lens-vendor/slice4-live-2.json`):
1. **The remembered-shelf sentence names nobody.** `proposedSentence` reads "last confirmed by **someone here** on 2026-09-11" while `line-mappings` carries `linkedBy = e37ab823…` — a known `public.users` row. Render the person's name (ADR 0104 D5's ProvenanceHover already does this for corrections); "someone" is an absence dressed as a sentence.
2. **`GET /providers/:id` with an id that is not a provider answers 500** "Cannot coerce the result to a single JSON object" (PostgREST `.single()` leaking). A missing row is 404 with a sentence; a failed read is 500 with the reason — this is neither.
3. **`POST /procurement/documents/:id/lines/:lineId/link-item` with a malformed `lineId` answers 500** "invalid input syntax for type uuid" — validate the param (400) before the read.
Also noted, not a defect: the upload response's `document` omits `providerId` even when the caller supplied one; it is on the row after extraction.

**Closed 2026-09-11, failing-first test per defect.**

1. **Cause:** `LineMappingService.proposalsFor` took an OPTIONAL `nameFor` hook and the one real caller — `canonical-document.service.ts:~742` — never passed it, so the `?? "someone here"` fallback fired on every line (`line-mapping.service.ts`, the `who` binding). **Fix:** the service resolves the name itself through a `users` read modelled on `document-correction.service.ts` `namesFor`; the hook stays for a caller that already holds the names. Three states are now distinct and cannot collapse into one another: the name; `a person whose name is not on record` when the id resolves to none; and **no proposal at all** (`ok: false`, carried to the line as `proposalUnavailable`) when the names read FAILED — saying "not on record" after a failed read would be absence-reported-as-health inside the fix for it. Tests: `apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/canonical/remembered-shelf-names.spec.ts` (4).
2. **Cause:** `providers.service.ts` `getProvider` used `.single()`, which makes "no row" a PostgREST **error** (`PGRST116`), and `providers.controller.ts` `@Get(":id")` flattened every error to 500 — so "this id is not a provider" and "the database is down" reached the caller as the same answer. **Fix:** `.maybeSingle()` + `NotFoundException` with a sentence; the controller rethrows an `HttpException` the service chose rather than re-wrapping it. A read that genuinely failed is still 500 **with the reason** — verified by its own test, not assumed. Tests: `apps/api-gateway/src/providers/get-provider-missing.spec.ts` (4).
3. **Cause:** no path-param validation anywhere on the documents controller; a malformed uuid reached Postgres (`22P02`) and the handler's catch-all turned it into a 500. **Measured, not assumed: all 7 param positions across the four line routes carried it** — `link-item`, `link`, `PATCH lines/:lineId` (both `:id` and `:lineId` on each) and `line-mappings`. **Fix:** a `requireUuid` helper (local `UUID_RE` + explicit check — the house pattern from `procurement.service.ts` / `vendor-intel.controller.ts`; this repo uses `ParseUUIDPipe` nowhere) called BEFORE each handler's `try`, so the 400 cannot be re-wrapped as a 500. Tests: `apps/api-gateway/src/procurement/documents/line-route-uuid-guard.spec.ts` (8).

**Found while fixing (3), NOT fixed — out of this brief's scope.** The same hole is open on the `:id`-only routes of the same controller: `GET /procurement/documents/:id` and `POST /procurement/documents/:id/verify` both answer **500 `invalid input syntax for type uuid`** for a malformed id (measured against the controller with a stub answering `22P02`, 2026-09-11). `POST :id/match` could not be measured the same way — the probe's intake stub is missing the method the route calls, so its 500 is the probe's, not the route's. Fixing these is one `requireUuid` line each; it is left open because widening the guard changes routes the brief did not name.

**Read back live 2026-09-11 after #350 merged** (main `e29c8dd4`, sim tenant): 404 with a sentence, 400 with a sentence, and the proposal sentence names *Sim Owner*. Closed on measurement.

## A NULL `p_restaurant_id` is still admitted by `apply_stock_movement` — OPEN — 2026-09-12

ADR 0141 closed the cross-tenant stock write at both ends: the gateway proves the item belongs to the caller before the RPC, and `apply_stock_movement` gained `p_restaurant_id` and raises `42501` on a mismatch. **What is not closed is the default.** The argument is `DEFAULT NULL` and a NULL asserts nothing, because migrations apply when the PR merges and the gateway deploys after it — a required argument would have 404'd every stock write the old gateway made during that window. Measured in `p4-scratch/pglite-probe/adr0141-stock-write-names-its-house.mjs`: a 17-argument call from the old gateway still books against the new function. All 15 gateway call sites and `scripts/synth/seed.py` now name a house, so nothing in this repo relies on the default [CORRECTED 2026-09-12: false -- `record_stock_count` (20260902190000_a_count_is_a_record.sql) and `set_stock_absolute` (20260805131000) both call `apply_stock_movement` without `p_restaurant_id`; the gateway paths that reach `record_stock_count` now check ownership first, and `set_stock_absolute`'s only gateway caller already reads the item scoped to the restaurant] [CORRECTED AGAIN 2026-09-12, second adversarial pass on PR #361: that last clause was false too. The scoped read in `InventoryService.updateInventoryItem` kept `data` and never refused on null, so `PATCH /inventory/:restaurantId/item/:itemId` with `stockLive` handed another house's item to `set_stock_absolute`, and a PGlite probe on this tree's migrations took that house's lots from 9 to empty. It now calls `assertInventoryBelongsToRestaurant` before any write. `record_glass_pour` and `transfer_stock` are the third and fourth wrappers with the same shape; a POS mapping naming another house's item poured through `record_glass_pour` (the probe wrote a 5-glass sale into the other house). `upsertItemMapping` now refuses a foreign item before writing, and `PosHubService.applyStockEffects` / `ToastService.applyOrderSaleEffects` pour only an id a house-scoped read returned. `scripts/check_stock_wrappers_check_ownership.py` now fails on any call site of the four wrappers outside its reviewed allowlist.] — but a future caller that forgets gets the old behaviour back.

**Owed:** a second migration that makes a NULL `p_restaurant_id` refuse. It cannot be written yet, and the blocker is named: `services/agent-orchestrator/core/database.py:1077` [citation CORRECTED 2026-09-12: was `:996`, a line in unrelated name-matching code; `update_stock` is defined at `:1045` and its `apply_stock_movement` call is at `:1077`, measured by grep] (`InventoryRepository.update_stock`) calls this RPC and **cannot name a restaurant** — its signature is `update_stock(inventory_id, new_stock, reason, max_retries)` and no restaurant reaches it. It could read `restaurant_id` off the very row the function derives the tenant from, which would prove nothing while looking like a check; it passes nothing instead, and this entry is the record of that. Give that caller a restaurant (from the agent's own tenant context, not from the item), then make NULL refuse. Tracked as the `open` claim on ADR-0141 in `decisions/CLAIMS.jsonl`, whose verify goes true exactly when both halves have landed.

**Not audited:** whether any existing `inventory_lots` / `inventory_transactions` row was ever written into the wrong house. No production read was made while building ADR 0141, so no claim either way is available.

**Related, named and not fixed:** `procurement_orders.inventory_id`, `procurement_document_lines.inventory_id` and `pos_item_mappings.inventory_id` are all plain FKs to `restaurant_inventory` with nothing tying the referenced item's tenant to the referencing row's. ADR 0141 makes a mismatch refuse at WRITE time [CORRECTED 2026-09-12, second adversarial pass: not for `pos_item_mappings.inventory_id` -- nothing refused a foreign item when a mapping was written, and a POS glass sale then poured it. `upsertItemMapping` now refuses before the upsert, and the depletion paths pour only ids a house-scoped read returns]; making it unstorable is a composite-key or trigger change nobody has decided on.

## An expired session silently destroys queued door receipts — OPEN — 2026-09-12

**Where.** `apps/web/src/lib/doorOutbox.ts` — in `runFlush`, grep `const permanent =` (the classification) and `if (permanent || m.retryCount + 1 >= MAX_ATTEMPTS)` (what it decides). Any 4xx except 408 and 429 is `permanent`:

```ts
const permanent =
  status && status >= 400 && status < 500 && status !== 408 && status !== 429
if (permanent || m.retryCount + 1 >= MAX_ATTEMPTS) {
  // …record the drop, then remove the queue entry
```

`401` and `403` are inside that range, so a queued receipt that comes back unauthorised leaves the queue and the delivery is gone. The same classification is on the submit path — same file, grep the `throw err` inside `submitDoorReceipt`'s catch — where it rethrows instead of queueing.

*(Line numbers were deliberately removed from this entry on 2026-09-12: the three it carried were all stale within a day of being written, by commits on the branch that wrote them. Grep anchors move with the code.)*

**Why a 401 actually reaches it.** The api client retries a 401 once through a token refresh (`apps/web/src/services/api/client.ts:87-122`), but it re-rejects with the original 401 in two live cases: no `refreshToken` in localStorage (`client.ts:93-94`) and a refresh call that itself fails, which also clears the tokens and sends the tab to `/login` (`client.ts:100-107`). Both are exactly the state a dock phone is in after a long shift — which is also when the queue is deepest, because the phone has been offline in the walk-in.

**Why it matters more than the status code suggests.** An auth failure is the one drop cause that is (a) certain to hit every queued receipt in the same pass, not just one, and (b) trivially recoverable — the receipt would send on the next flush if it were still in the queue. Deleting it converts a sixty-second fix into a permanent loss of a delivery count, and the count is the one number the four-way match is later built on.

**Proposed fix (not applied here — this is pre-existing on `main`, and outside the branch that found it).** Treat `401` and `403` as **retryable**, alongside 408 and 429: leave the receipt in the queue, bump `retryCount`, and let the next flush send it once the user has signed in again. The existing `MAX_ATTEMPTS` budget still bounds it, so a genuinely permanent 403 (a revoked user, a restaurant the account no longer belongs to) still drops after eight passes instead of on the first. A failing-first test belongs in `apps/web/src/lib/doorOutbox.test.ts` next to the drop-classification suite already there.

**What this branch did do**, because a notice that names the wrong remedy is worse than one that names none: the flush now **records the cause** with the drop (grep `reason: 'auth' | 'refused' | 'retries' | 'unknown'`), and the legacy door screen says *"The app was signed out. Sign in again before recording another"* instead of *"tell a manager"* when every drop on the phone was an auth drop, and claims no cause at all when they are mixed (`apps/web/src/pages/receiving/DoorReceipt.tsx`, the `door:dropped` strip; tests in `DoorReceipt.test.tsx`). That changes the words, not the loss — the receipt is still deleted until the fix above lands.

**Closed on this branch, and listed here because the entry above asserted the opposite for a day:** the rebuilt door screen `apps/web/src/pages/receiving/next/DoorNext.tsx` used to hold its drop count in `useState`, so the only trace of a permanent loss died on the navigate Finish triggers. It now seeds from `readDroppedDoorReceipts(rid)` and re-reads it, like the legacy `DoorReceipt.tsx`. A receipt the outbox gives up on but **cannot record** is now KEPT in the queue rather than deleted, which is the half that prevents outright destruction. The other half — telling the porter about it — was attempted five times and withdrawn: see **ADR 0140**, which lists all five designs and the defect each shipped. The short version is that the outbox was being asked to record durably a fact whose cause is that durable storage failed, on a layer that reports failed writes and failed reads as success. The receipt survives, `pendingDoorCount` counts it, and the receiving rail lists it; nothing claims more than that until the offline-storage entry at the end of this file is closed.

**What remains open here is only the paragraph above**: the `permanent` classification still deletes a receipt that came back 401/403, on `main` and on this branch.


## The offline queue reports a write it swallowed as a write that succeeded — OPEN — 2026-09-12

**Where.** `apps/web/src/lib/offline-storage.ts`, two places, both on the localStorage
fallback that every device without a usable IndexedDB lands on:

- grep `localStorage put failed:` — `localStoragePut` catches its own
  `QuotaExceededError`, logs it, and **returns normally**. `idbPut`'s fallback branch
  awaits nothing further, so `updatePendingMutation` and `addPendingMutation`
  **resolve** having written nothing at all.
- grep `if (existing)` in `updatePendingMutation` — when the entry is missing the
  function simply does nothing, and still resolves.

**Measured, not inferred** (`apps/web/src/lib/doorOutbox.durability.test.ts`, which is why
that file mocks nothing but the network): jsdom has no IndexedDB, so the real module
takes the fallback. With `localStorage.setItem` throwing, the door flush's parking
update resolved, the queue entry was left at `retryCount: 0` with **no `lastError`**,
and the caller had no way to know. Every door test before it mocked `./offline-storage`
with a store that always applies the patch, which is exactly why 920 green tests never
saw this.

**Why it is the register's headline fault and not a logging nit.** This is
"absence reported as health" at a write path: a promise that resolves is the only
contract a caller has, and here it is a lie. Anything built on top of it that asks the
disk what happened gets a confident wrong answer. The door screens were built on top of
it for one day — the strand alarm read the mark this write was supposed to leave, so the
one condition that creates a strand was also the one condition under which the alarm
could not fire. **The door end is closed by REMOVING the alarm, not by out-thinking the
write** (ADR 0140): five designs tried to keep a durable, screen-facing witness for a
strand and each shipped a measured defect, because the fact to be recorded *is* that the
recording failed. What the door keeps instead is the receipt — the queue entry is never
deleted when its drop record could not be written — and that needs no witness at all.
The swallow underneath is untouched and reaches every other caller, and closing it is
what would make a strand reportable again.

**Who else is standing on it.** `lib/sync-manager.ts` (retry bookkeeping — grep
`updatePendingMutation` there: a swallowed write means a mutation retries forever at
the same count) and `lib/spotCountOutbox.ts` (same shape, and its call is not inside a
`try`).

**Why it is not fixed here.** The obvious fix — make `localStoragePut` rethrow — changes
the contract for every offline caller at once, including cache writes that today
degrade silently on purpose, and `spotCountOutbox`'s unguarded call would begin
rejecting mid-loop and abandon the rest of its queue. That is a change worth making
deliberately, with its own sweep of the callers, not as a rider on a branch about
motion-canvas defects. **The narrower fix, if it is wanted first:** have
`addPendingMutation`/`updatePendingMutation` (only those two) read the record back and
reject when it did not land — the queue is the store where a lost write costs a
delivery, and it is the store with the fewest callers.

Co-requisite when it is done: delete the door's in-memory ledger's justification
paragraph, or at least narrow it, since half of its reason will have gone away.

**A second consequence, filed 2026-09-12 after it was measured:** because the READ half
swallows too — `idbGetAll` catches every IndexedDB error and falls through to
`localStorageGetAll`, which catches its own and returns `[]` — a queue that is merely
unreadable is indistinguishable from a queue that is empty, at every caller. The door
outbox shipped two defects on that for a day: a strand ledger pruned against such a
read went silent, and a version that converted apparently-orphaned strands into
permanent "we gave up on this delivery" records wrote one for a receipt that was still
queued and went on to be accepted by the server — which would have sent a porter to
re-enter, by hand and under a new idempotency key, a delivery the server already had.
Both were withdrawn rather than patched, because **nothing built on this read can tell
absence from failure**, and that is the fix this entry is asking for.

## Twelve `ProviderIntelligenceService` methods read across every tenant — OPEN — 2026-09-17

**[2026-09-19, PR #391 audit M5: now also a CLAIMS row —**
**`TECHDEBT-PROVIDER-INTEL-12-UNSCOPED-READS` in `.planning/decisions/CLAIMS.jsonl`,
open, statically checked and mutation-tested (one method's fix flips it, the other
eleven do not). The PR #391 body's claim that provider-intelligence cross-house reads
are closed covered only `compareProviders`/`getSentimentTrend`; these twelve are not
closed by that PR.]**

The `fix/finish-leaks` branch closed `compareProviders`' missing restaurant filter
(`apps/api-gateway/src/providers/provider-intelligence.service.ts:449`) and stopped
there. Measured on the same file, same tree: twelve more methods reach a table whose
`restaurant_id` column is `uuid NOT NULL` in the baseline and filter on it not at all,
or on a provider/session id that is itself not restaurant-scoped.

**Five take no restaurant argument at all** — the exact fault `compareProviders` had:

| Method | Line | Table (baseline line) | Route |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getAllActivePromotions` | `:165` | `provider_promotions` (`:4808`) | `GET /providers/promotions/active` — **fetched by the live web hook** `apps/web/src/hooks/queries/usePromotionsQueries.ts:22` (`useActivePromotions`) |
| `getExpiringPromotions` | `:182` | `provider_promotions` (`:4808`) | `GET /providers/promotions/expiring` |
| `getPromoSavings` | `:203` | `provider_promotions` (`:4808`) | `GET /providers/promotions/savings` |
| `comparePromotions` | `:228` | `provider_promotions` (`:4808`) | `GET /providers/promotions/compare` |
| `getLeverageSignals` | `:510` | `provider_knowledge` (`:4727`) — corrected 2026-09-17 from an earlier draft that filed this one as `provider_promotions` too; it is not | `GET /providers/intelligence/leverage` — **also fetched by a live page**, `apps/web/src/components/providers/ProviderComparisonView.tsx:15` via `apps/web/src/services/api/provider-intelligence.ts:258` |

**Seven more filter on a provider or session id only**, never on `restaurant_id`, over
tables that carry their own `restaurant_id NOT NULL` independent of the id used:

| Method | Line | Table (baseline line) | Filters on |
|---|---|---|---|
| `getKnowledge` | `:24` | `provider_knowledge` (`:4727`) | `provider_id`, `category` |
| `getContradictions` | `:102` | `provider_knowledge` (`:4727`) | `provider_id` |
| `getPromotions` | `:141` | `provider_promotions` (`:4808`) | `provider_id`, `status` |
| `getConversationMemory` | `:257` | `conversation_embeddings` (`:2550`) | `provider_id` |
| `searchConversationMemory` | `:298` | `conversation_embeddings` (`:2550`) | `provider_id`, a text query |
| `getSessions` | `:323` | `provider_conversation_sessions` (`:4685`) | `provider_id` |
| `getSessionSummary` | `:352` | `provider_conversation_sessions` (`:4685`) | `sessionId` (its own PK) |

All twelve are wired to live controller routes in
`apps/api-gateway/src/providers/provider-intelligence.controller.ts` with no
restaurant param on any of them (`:35,53,90,106,120,135,148,168,187,215,234,378`), and
every route sits behind only `JwtAuthGuard`/`RolesGuard` — nothing tenant-scoped. A
signed-in member of any house can read any other house's provider promotions,
knowledge-graph entries, conversation history and leverage signals; `provider_id` is a
UUID, not a secret, and several of the list routes (`promotions/active`,
`promotions/compare`, `leverage-signals`) need no id at all to return every tenant's
rows in one call.

**Not filed as an ADR/OD** — a decision register entry says something needs a founder
call; this is a straightforward scope gap in an already-decided pattern
(`restaurantId` from the token, filtered in the query, same as every other reader in
this file). Filing it here costs one entry; an OD would shift ~173 citations
(`register-row-shifts-citations` memory) for a fork nobody is actually weighing.

**Fix, same shape as `compareProviders`'s already-shipped fix:** take `restaurantId`
from the authenticated request (never the body/params) in the controller, thread it
into each of the twelve service methods, and add `.eq("restaurant_id", restaurantId)`
to each query — for the seven id-scoped reads, either directly on the table (five of
seven carry the column right on the row) or, where the id only reaches its tenant
through a join (`getSessionSummary`'s `sessionId` — check whether
`provider_conversation_sessions` exposes `restaurant_id` directly before assuming a
join is needed), by resolving through the owning table first. Not measured here:
whether any of the six list-shaped routes (`promotions/active`, `expiring`, `savings`,
`compare`, `leverage-signals`, plus `getContradictions`) is called from a page that
expects platform-wide data on purpose — check each call site before scoping, the same
way `minor` finding on `provider-intelligence.service.ts:455` (this same PR) found that
`.eq("restaurant_id", ...)` alone can silently empty a page that relied on
provider-nullable shared rows.

## Three PostGIS `st_estimatedextent` overloads are SECURITY DEFINER and client-executable in production, accepted by OD-72 with no record — OPEN — 2026-09-18

**Measured 2026-09-18, read-only `SELECT` on production via the Supabase MCP.** Four
`SECURITY DEFINER` functions are `EXECUTE`-able by `anon` and `authenticated`, across
every schema except `pg_catalog` and `information_schema`. One is
`public.increment_trust_counter(uuid)`, which `20260917010400` closes once it is applied
(production's latest applied version that day: `20260917020000`). The other three are:

| Function | Owner | Extension |
|---|---|---|
| `public.st_estimatedextent(text, text)` | `supabase_admin` | `postgis` 3.3.7 |
| `public.st_estimatedextent(text, text, text)` | `supabase_admin` | `postgis` 3.3.7 |
| `public.st_estimatedextent(text, text, text, boolean)` | `supabase_admin` | `postgis` 3.3.7 |

Each ACL is `{=X/supabase_admin,supabase_admin=X/supabase_admin,postgres=X/supabase_admin,anon=X/supabase_admin,authenticated=X/supabase_admin,service_role=X/supabase_admin}`:
`PUBLIC`, `anon` and `authenticated` all hold `EXECUTE` explicitly.

**Not new: OD-72 saw them and left them on purpose.** OD-72's own migration records all
three as `SECURITY DEFINER` and `anon`-executable and leaves them alone, with the reason
that `supabase_admin` owns them, they are read-only geometry statistics, and a migration
cannot `ALTER` them (`20260825210000_od72_revoke_client_grants.sql:143-145`). That is the
only record of the decision: a migration comment, with no ADR, no register row and no
CLAIMS row. It also names `anon` only; `authenticated` and `PUBLIC` hold the same grant.
What 2026-09-18 adds is the measurement that it still holds, the full ACL, and the proof
that `postgres` cannot revoke it (below).

**Why the guard does not see it.** No migration spells a `CREATE FUNCTION` for these.
On a fresh build they arrive with `create extension if not exists postgis`
(`20260807001252_distributor_geo_foundation.sql:31`, the corpus's only PostGIS install:
`grep -rniE "create\s+extension[^;]*postgis" supabase/migrations` finds 1 line). The only
migration that names them is the OD-72 comment above (`grep -rl st_estimatedextent
supabase/migrations`: 1 file). Guard arm (c) of `scripts/check_new_tables_are_locked_down.py`
(ADR 0159) reads the static SQL text of migrations and does not expand what `create
extension` installs, so these are outside it by construction, the same way
`spatial_ref_sys` is outside arms (a) and (b). Whether production's copies came from
that statement is not measured: `if not exists` makes it a no-op where PostGIS was
already installed. **[CORRECTED 2026-09-18, round 4, source: PR #391 verifier round 3:
this paragraph cited `:31` and then said "no migration creates them" in the same
sentence. That is false on a fresh build, where `:31` is what creates them.]** **[2026-09-18, round 5,
source: PR #391 verifier round 4 and ADR 0159's round-5 correction: arm (c) is now only a
fast pre-check for common spellings; the class is decided by
`scripts/check_definer_functions_closed.py`, an end-state check on the database
`schema-parity.yml` builds. That check excludes extension members too (`pg_depend.deptype
= 'e'`), so these three overloads are outside both, and a fix for them cannot come from
either check.]**

**Why a migration cannot simply revoke it.** `postgres` is not the owner, holds no grant
option on them, is not superuser and is not a member of `supabase_admin` (all measured
the same day). A `REVOKE` run as `postgres` would remove nothing.

**Not measured:** what `st_estimatedextent` exposes when a client role calls it through
PostgREST (`/rest/v1/rpc/st_estimatedextent`). It reads planner statistics for a named
table and geometry column and returns a bounding box, so the exposure is likely
statistics-level rather than row-level, but that is an inference, not a measurement.

**Owed:** a decision, starting from OD-72's acceptance rather than from zero. Either
record that acceptance properly (an ADR or register row that names all three roles, not
only `anon`, and states the exposure once it is measured), or reverse it. Candidates for
reversing, none tried: ask Supabase / use the dashboard to revoke as `supabase_admin`;
move `postgis` out of `public` (a larger change, not scoped). Sibling of ADR 0159;
recorded there under Consequences.

## Arm (b) credits `REVOKE GRANT OPTION FOR` on a table as a full revoke — OPEN — 2026-09-18

Found by ADR 0159's round-4 mutation sweep of arm (c), which had the same misread on
functions (fixed there). `RE_REVOKE` in `scripts/check_new_tables_are_locked_down.py`
matches `revoke grant option for <privs> on <t> from anon` through its optional
`(?:grant\s+option\s+for\s+)?` group and clears the table's client-grant bit. PostgreSQL
revokes only the grant option; the privilege stays. Measured on a copy of the corpus,
2026-09-18: a planted `create table public.gof_t ...; alter table ... enable row level
security; grant select on public.gof_t to anon; revoke grant option for select on
public.gof_t from anon;` exits 0 (arm (b) reports every table locked down). The function
twin was proven a real leak on PGlite
(`p4-scratch/pglite-probe/audit-391-r4-static-misreads.mjs`: `=X/postgres` unchanged); the
table form was not probed. No migration in the corpus spells `grant option for` (grep: 0
lines), so nothing on `main` is affected today.

**Owed:** stop crediting a `REVOKE GRANT OPTION FOR` in arm (b), the same way arm (c)
now does, and pin it. Arm (b) has no `--self-test`, so pinning it means giving arm (b) one.
Left open because this lane's scope was arm (c).

## A closed SECURITY DEFINER function can still run for a client through a trigger or an aggregate — ~~OPEN~~ CLOSED 2026-09-19 (ADR 0159 round 7) — 2026-09-18

**Found by the PR #391 verifier, round 5; reproduced by the round-6 fixer, both on a PGlite
build of all 185 migrations with Supabase's default privileges.** ADR 0159's end-state
check (`scripts/check_definer_functions_closed.py`) judges `EXECUTE` on each `SECURITY
DEFINER` function and nothing else. PostgreSQL runs some functions with no `EXECUTE` check
against the caller, so a definer that check calls closed can still run, as its owner, for
`anon`:

| Row | Planted migration | `anon` did | Result | End-state check | Arm (c) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A3 | a definer trigger function, `EXECUTE` revoked from `public, anon, authenticated`, fired `before insert` on a table `anon` may `INSERT` into | `insert ... returning who` | ran as `postgres` | exit 0 | not read (the table arm exits 1 on the new table itself) |
| A9 | a definer revoked the same way, used as the `sfunc` of `create aggregate public.zz_agg(int)`, `EXECUTE` on the aggregate granted to `anon` | `select public.zz_agg(x)` | ran as `postgres` | exit 0 | exit 0 |

Why: a trigger's function is not checked for `EXECUTE` when the trigger fires (only when
it is created, against its creator), and an aggregate's support functions are checked
against the aggregate's owner, not its caller.

**Controls, measured the same way, which do check the caller** (each `permission denied
for function` for `anon`, and each is correctly not a leak): the same closed definer as a
column `DEFAULT` on a table `anon` may insert into (A10), called in a view `anon` may
`SELECT` (A11), and called in an RLS policy on a table `anon` may read (A12).

**Not reachable on this tree (measured on the same build, 2026-09-18):** the corpus's 7
`SECURITY DEFINER` functions in `public` include 3 trigger functions, each on one table:
`procurement_header_price_is_an_echo` on `procurement_orders`,
`procurement_line_price_echoes_to_header` on `procurement_order_items`, and
`ux_experiment_state_is_written_once` on `ux_experiment_state`. `has_table_privilege` for
`INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` is false on all three tables for `anon`, `authenticated`
and `authenticator`. No aggregate has a `SECURITY DEFINER` support function (`pg_aggregate`
joined to `pg_proc.prosecdef`: 0 rows), and no migration creates an aggregate (`grep -rliE
"create\s+(or\s+replace\s+)?aggregate" supabase/migrations`: 0 files). Production was not
read for this.

~~**Owed:** a decision on whether the end-state check should also judge these paths: [...]
Trigger functions that must stay definers (the three above) would then need their own
named allowlist, with the reason. Left open because the fix changes what the check calls a
leak for functions that exist today, which is the founder's call. [...]~~

**[CLOSED 2026-09-19, ADR 0159 round 7. The founder's call, in chat: "do what the optimal

now runs a fourth read-only query (`REACH_SQL`) and fails any `SECURITY DEFINER` function
PostgreSQL would run for `PUBLIC` or a judged role with no `EXECUTE` check against it, naming
the path; `CLIENT_CALLABLE` never excuses one. Not only these two paths: triggers on every

222 rows, A3, A9 and N1 move 0 -> 1, 59 leak rows and 59 closed controls. On the repaired
