**[CORRECTED 2026-09-19, round-7 repair 2, source: the round-7 verifier's re-run of the
repair: nobody had yet attacked the repaired walk, and its numbers were its builder's word.
A second adversarial pass on PGlite (27 plants, 22 controls) found six more paths on which a
closed definer ran for `anon` while the repaired check exited 0:

- `ANALYZE` of a column with no index runs its type's statistics code as the owner (a
  range's `subtype_diff`: C1);
- a foreign key's CHECK, not only its actions, runs as the other table's owner, with any
  cast between the key types (C2, C2n);
- the planner rewrites `NOT (a op b)` with the operator's negator (C3, C3m, C3i);
- builtins handed a type or a table by bare OID (`domain_in`, `record_in`, `array_in`,
  `satisfies_hash_partition`: C4, C4r, C4a, C5);
- a partition's own constraint runs its parent's partition key (C7);
- and, in plain client context, a selectivity estimator runs the operator's own function
  -- or its negator's or commutator's -- on column statistics while planning, with no
  `EXECUTE` check (C10: `anon`'s `EXPLAIN` ran the closed definer six times).

The entry stays CLOSED on that evidence: the check now seeds column statistics and
foreign-key checks, follows negators and commutators, judges an ancestor's partition key and
the type-by-OID builtins, and judges the estimator path as `PUBLIC`. Every plant gives the
wanted exit and every control exits 0; the tree and a build with the pinned storage-api
schema on top both exit 0 (0 reached, 0 BLIND). Not measured: the rest of the Supabase local
stack and PostgreSQL 17. ADR 0159, "Round 7 repair 2: the second adversarial pass".]**
## System-mail templates interpolate data unescaped — OPEN — 2026-09-19

**What.** The gateway's system-mail templates (`apps/api-gateway/src/communications/email-templates/*.ts` and `email-templates-legacy.ts`) interpolate about 95 DATA values into HTML with no escaping. These include provider, driver and staff names, free-text notes, reminder titles and descriptions, and sign-up fields. No templating engine is installed. Most of the DATA flows through the `base-template.ts` helpers: `tableRow`'s value, `alertBox`'s title and message, and `baseTemplate`'s title and header name. The live free-text path is `custom-reminder.template.ts` (via `scheduled-tasks.service.ts:835`).

**Why it is not fixed with the vendor path.** ADR 0170 (PR #399) fixed the vendor-bound builder. These templates mix DATA with HTML fragments built by other helpers, so escaping at the wrong layer double-escapes live mail. They are also about to be rebuilt by the comms-templates lane: a read-only catalogue first, then slot editing, rendered server-side and escaped. The census recommends escaping inside the helpers as the first move; in every call site found, the helper writes the surrounding markup itself.

**Tracked by** CLAIMS row `ADR-0170-TEMPLATES-ESCAPE-DATA` (`open`). It flips red once every named chokepoint escapes, which forces this entry and ADR 0170's step 2 to be closed together. **Severity:** recipients are internal (managers, staff, the account owner), so the exposure is HTML injection into the house's own mail, not into vendor mail. The one vendor-bound template, `orderInquiryTemplate`, is behind `NonProductionGuard` (`communications.controller.ts:566`).

**Addendum 2026-09-19 (PR #401 audit).** Two corrections:
- The recipient lists are configured. "Internal" describes the default, not something the code guarantees.
- The `open` row checks only gateway files. The orchestrator's internal template `email_client._render_template`, which reaches managers through `notification_agent.py`, renders data unescaped too. `_build_manager_review_html` does as well, but its only caller, `compose_manager_review_email`, is never called. They are NOT covered by that row.

So flipping the row does not close this entry. Close it only when the Python templates escape as well. It is expected to close with ADR 0173's single template source. That ADR names `template_engine.py`, so the Python renderer must be brought under it explicitly.

## Outbound MIME: what ADR 0172 does not cover — OPEN — 2026-09-19

**What.** ADR 0172 (PR #402) put every header of the two hand-built Gmail messages (`GmailService.createMimeMessage`, `sendThroughGrant`) through `communications/mime-headers.ts`. These outbound paths were checked and left as they are:

- **The Python sender's `from_name`** (`services/agent-orchestrator/services/email_client.py:175-236` `_send_via_gmail`). With a non-ASCII display name, compat32 encodes the whole `Name <addr>` string as one encoded-word, hiding the address. The default `"WineOps AI"` is ASCII, so this is latent until the brand-rename lane gives it non-ASCII text.
- **Two Python cap-alert senders**, `services/agent-orchestrator/api/onboarding_routes.py:164-179` and `services/agent-orchestrator/jobs/spend_tasks.py:107-122`. Both use `smtplib` + `msg.as_string()` (compat32), send only to the configured manager address, and have a fixed-shape subject. The onboarding one embeds `restaurant_id[:8]`, which is `request.restaurant_id` verbatim on the admin-key path. Both subjects contain "—", so compat32 encodes the whole subject; measured on Python 3.11.0, an id of `"\nBcc:e@x"` stayed inside an encoded-word and made no `Bcc` header. Safe today by that accident, not by a guard.
- **Non-ASCII bare addresses** (SMTPUTF8 or IDN) pass through `mime-headers.ts` unchanged — not punycoded, not refused. No caller produces them today.
- **`sendEmail`'s log line** `` `Subject: ${options.subject}` `` (`gmail.service.ts`, top of `sendEmail`) can still split a log line — log forging, not mail. It is also why the CLAIMS row's template-literal sweep can only match header literals at the **start** of a line.
- **The procurement send-failure message.** `sendProviderEmail` (`procurement.service.ts`) used to say "GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN may be expired" for *any* failed send, including a `MimeHeaderError` where Gmail was never called — and `isDefiniteSendRefusal` did not recognise the refusal, so `approveDraft` parked the draft as `SEND_UNCONFIRMED` ("may or may not have reached the vendor"), which was false. **Fixed in PR #402** for header refusals (`refusedBeforeSend` → `SendRefusedBeforeSendError`, classified by `instanceof`, never by the error text, which embeds the vendor's address; pinned by the CLAIMS row and three `approve-draft-concurrency.spec.ts` cases). Every *other* failure still gets the GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN hint, which is right only for an auth failure. [corrected 2026-09-25, PR #405: `refusedBeforeSend` is now the typed `EmailResult.refusal`. A `rejected` refusal (Gmail 400/403/404, SMTP 5xx, `EENVELOPE`) now says the mail service rejected the request; the GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN hint remains on `credentials` and `no-transport` refusals and on every AMBIGUOUS failure — where, since credential failures are now classified out, it is never the likely cause (`procurement.service.ts` `sendProviderEmail`, the `BadRequestException` after the `if (result.refusal)` block).]

**Tracked by** CLAIMS row `ADR-0172-MIME-HEADERS-ENCODED` (`resolved`) for the part that is fixed; the Python items have no row. **Severity:** low. The Python cap-alert senders and the Python `from_name` send only to internal, configured recipients. Non-ASCII bare addresses would go to vendors, but no caller produces one today. The log line has no recipient: its risk is a forged log line, not mail. None of these is reachable with a vendor-controlled line break today.

## A password reset or change signs no other session out and unenrolls no phone — OPEN — 2026-09-19

**Scope (founder, batch 6):** an in-app `changePassword` must revoke the same way. The current device stays signed in.

**What.** `resetPassword` changes the hash and consumes the reset rows, then stops. It deliberately revokes no session (`apps/api-gateway/src/auth/auth.service.ts:2216-2223`): `TokenBlacklistService` can blacklist only a token it is handed, and nothing tracks the tokens issued to a user. So a person who resets a password because the account was taken still leaves the taker signed in on every other device. `changePassword` has the same shape.

**Decided, not built.** The founder, 2026-09-19 (ADR 0174 D8, ADR 0175 decision 2): a reset revokes every other session, unenrolls every enrolled phone, and the reset mail says so. Revocation was never built, and phone enrollment does not exist yet. The mail's sentence must ship in the same change as the revocation, never before it (ADR 0020).

**Tracked by** no CLAIMS row. A static check cannot tell a real revocation from the removal of the comment at `:2216`, and the shape of the fix (session tracking, possibly the sessions lane's ADR 0164, not on main) is not known yet. Close this entry with a behavioural spec: reset on device A, and a request from device B's token is refused. **Severity:** account takeover persists through the owner's own recovery step.

## The nightly's artifact scrub does not cover four secret shapes, and two channels publish before it — OPEN — 2026-09-21

Found by PR #349's pre-merge audit (security angle F2–F4) and recorded here
because a commit body does not survive a squash merge, and nothing re-reads one.

**1. Four secret shapes are reported "redacted" or "clean" without being cleaned.**
Measured against `scripts/e2e/scrub_artifacts.py` at the time:

    in : authorization: Basic QWxhZGRpbjpvcGVuIHNlc2FtZQ==
    out: authorization: [redacted] QWxhZGRpbjpvcGVuIHNlc2FtZQ==   _leaks=False
    in : ADMIN_API_KEY=SUPER_SECRET_VALUE                          _leaks=False  (no match at all)
    in : x-admin-key: SUPER_SECRET_VALUE                           _leaks=False
    in : SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY=sb_secret_abc123                _leaks=False

`\bapi[_-]?key\b` cannot match inside `ADMIN_API_KEY` — there is no word
boundary at `N_A` — and `ADMIN_API_KEY` is a mapped secret sent as `X-Admin-Key`
by Wave B (`services/agent-orchestrator/tests/e2e/conftest_prod.py:183`). Because
`_leaks()` uses the same patterns, a survivor it cannot see is reported clean.
No code today prints a header dict into the upload set, so this is a latent gap,
not a demonstrated leak. **On a clock:** Supabase keys are JWTs today and the JWT
rule covers them; the day they rotate to `sb_secret_…` the coverage disappears
silently. Fix: add `\bADMIN[_-]?API[_-]?KEY\b`, `x-admin-key`,
`sb_(secret|publishable)_` and non-Bearer auth schemes to the shared
`SECRET_PAIR`/`_leaks` set, mirrored in `lib.ts` and `honest-reporter.ts`.

**2. Two channels publish before the scrub and cannot be retracted.**
`scripts/e2e/nightly_summary.py:577-580` appends to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` and
`:581` prints to stdout; every teed pytest and Playwright stdout lands in the job
log. Both are public on a public repo, both happen before the scrub step, neither
is scrubbed. They are protected by the same regex set (so no weaker than the
artifact) but they are **not fail-closed**, and gap 1 applies to them too.
Worth recording for the shape: GitHub masks *registered secrets* in the log and
summary but **not** in artifact files — so the scrub is the only control on the
channel GitHub does not cover, and the channels GitHub does cover are the ones
the scrub cannot reach.

**3. The root `test-results/` is not gitignored.** Only `*.log` is caught
(`.gitignore:55`). `apps/web/test-results/` is ignored, but the Wave H command
writes to `../../test-results/`, so a local run creates an untracked root
`test-results/` holding unredacted `record_check` evidence
(`services/agent-orchestrator/tests/e2e_gateway/conftest.py:45-66` applies no
redaction). No such directory exists in any worktree today. One line closes it.

**4. `/calendar/ical-token` is a GET that MINTS a credential.**
`apps/web/src/pages/connections/next/useConnectionsNextData.ts:589-593` says so in
its own comment. `ReadOnlyGuard` (`apps/web/e2e/nightly/lib.ts:211-234`) permits it
because it is a GET — so the read-only production walk provisions a calendar token
on a house that had none. Harmless on a simulator house; it stops being harmless
when the sims are flipped to a real house (this PR's own open fork F5).

Nothing here blocks the nightly; all four are recorded so that they are checkable
later rather than remembered.

---

## Credentials in a public repo and in the Vercel upload — OPEN, founder-owned — added 2026-09-21

Found by PR #428's security angle while it was checking an unrelated assertion in
`.vercelignore`. Not caused by #428; escalated to the founder the same session, who took
rotation and any repo-side remedy as his own. **This entry exists so the finding is not lost,
not as a task for a session to pick up.** Do not act on it without his word.

### 1. 🔴 The repository is public and one tracked file carries plaintext API keys

`gh repo view` → `isPrivate=false`, `visibility=PUBLIC`.

One tracked file holds a `SERPER_API_KEY` and a `GOOGLE_API_KEY` as literal values. Both key
names are named because rotation needs them; nothing else about the file's location is, for
the reason below. **Exposed since 2026-04-06** — the commit that added it, carrying the same
Google key by digest. Anyone on GitHub can read them now, and automated scanners reach a
public push within minutes, so treat them as taken.

[CORRECTED 2026-09-21 by this PR's own audit gate, round 3, security angle: an earlier draft
of this paragraph said "present since `47187bb68`" and named the containing directory. Both
were wrong to write. The SHA is wrong on the facts — `git show --stat 47187bb68 -- <path>` is
empty; that commit merely contains the file, it does not add it — and it understated the
exposure window by a week. The directory was wrong on intent: the paragraph claimed the path
was "deliberately not written", then supplied a directory holding two tracked files plus a
commit SHA, which walks a human reader to it in one step. The sweep below already resolves to
exactly one file and is the only pointer this entry should carry.]

**The path is deliberately not written here.** This register is itself tracked in the public
repo, and a commit naming the file would turn a passively-scraped exposure into an advertised
one. It is one file and this finds it exactly, from the repo root:

```
git grep -lE 'sk-ant-api03-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}|AIzaSy[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}|sk-proj-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}|ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}|xoxb-[0-9A-Za-z-]{20,}|-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----' HEAD
```

That file's own `root_cause` line says the Serper key already returns
`{"message":"Unauthorized.","statusCode":403}`, so that one may be dead already — that is a
reading of a debug note, not a check, and it says nothing about the Google key.

Scope was measured, not assumed: the sweep above, run over the whole tracked tree, returns
**exactly one file**. Nothing else tracked matches.

**It is never SERVED. It has reached Vercel.** Those are different bounds and an earlier
draft of this entry gave the wrong one.

[CORRECTED 2026-09-21 by this PR's own audit gate, round 4, security angle. The struck
claim was "It never reached Vercel", justified by `.vercelignore` carrying a bare
`.planning` exclusion. That reasoning is wrong at the root: `.vercelignore` governs what
the **CLI uploads**. This repo is also connected to Vercel's **Git integration**, which
builds server-side from a clone of the whole repository — and an ignore file *inside* a
repo cannot stop the repo being fetched. The ordering is visible in the builder's own
vendored code: `@vercel/build-utils/dist/get-ignore-filter.js:53-57` (`:54-58` in the currently-published
14.10.1 — cited by version-independent behavior, not a pinned line, since this same
paragraph exists to explain why a line number is the wrong shape to cite) joins `.vercelignore`
onto `downloadPath`, i.e. it is read **after** the source is already materialised. Git
deploys demonstrably ran and succeeded — `2bf07c6dc` carries
`Vercel – restaurant-ai-automation-web :: success :: Deployment has completed`. So this
file has been materialised inside Vercel's build container **on every push to `main` that
actually produced a build** since 2026-04-06 — not literally every push. The account's daily
cap has been rate-limiting Git deploys on and off all evening (`34c33a76a`, `b6a61f497`,
`3dbb435db` all read `failure :: Deployment rate limited` on both Vercel contexts; no
deployment, no clone, no container, for those three specifically), and this file's own §2
already names that cap two sections down. The corrected claim survives on `2bf07c6dc` alone,
which is confirmed `success`, and it carries **the same third-party-retention question §2
raises for `.claude/`**, not an exemption from it.

Why this mattered enough to block: the wrong bound scoped remediation to GitHub alone. A
reader acting on it would have rotated for the public-repo half and never asked the
retention question for this file at all.]

What IS established is the narrower, load-bearing fact: **it is never served.** A read-only
probe with controls — a real asset and a fabricated one — shows the origin discriminates
rather than blanket-404ing, and `/.planning/PROJECT.md` returns the same 404 as the
fabricated control. That is a point-in-time result; what makes it structural rather than
incidental is `vercel.json`'s `outputDirectory: "apps/web/dist"` — `.planning/` is not
under the served root regardless of what any ignore file does, so this is "cannot be served
by this project's config," not merely "was not observed being served just now."

`.vercelignore` does still keep it out of **CLI** uploads. That is cited as a pattern and
not as a line number, because the line moved four times inside this PR alone
(60 -> 79 -> 119 -> 144 -> 165; it is 52 on `main`) and the number was once copied forward
stale. The "three" this sentence said until round 4 was itself an unmeasured count — caught
by the same gate, inside the very sentence that exists to explain why a count was the wrong
shape here. Confirm with `grep -n '^\.planning$' .vercelignore`.

On the disclosure: yes, the sentence above reveals which tree the file sits in. Keeping it
costs ~nothing, but not for the reason an earlier draft gave. That draft traded the
disclosure for "it never reached Vercel" — a bound that was false. The real reason it is
harmless is that the `git grep -lE` sweep in this same entry already resolves to exactly
one file in one command, which is strictly stronger than naming a tree of over a thousand
tracked files. The disclosure was never the thing doing the work.

### 2. 🔴 `.claude/settings.local.json` holds live-format keys and was uploaded to Vercel

30,024 bytes, untracked (gitignored only via the founder's global `~/.config/git/ignore`), and
**21 copies on disk** (root plus one per worktree). Every number in this section is a
same-day reading of **one laptop's untracked state**, not a repo fact — unreproducible from
any tree by construction, and stated here only to size the exposure. It contains, inside Claude Code permission
allow-rules, one `sk-ant-api03-` Anthropic key and three `AIzaSy` occurrences.

The Google key in it is **the same key** as the one in the tracked file above — compared by
digest; values were never printed into any transcript, report or PR comment.

Until PR #428, `.vercelignore` had no `.claude/` entry, so every CLI `vercel deploy` uploaded
all 21 copies into that deployment's source storage. This is **credential transit to a third
party with retention, not public disclosure** — neither `vercel.json` (root) nor
`apps/web/vercel.json` sets `"public": true`, and a read-only probe confirms
`https://www.mudavym.com/.claude/settings.local.json` → 308 → **404**. Do not overstate it as
a web leak; do not understate it as nothing.

**#428 closes the ongoing half** by adding `.claude/` to `.vercelignore`, so future CLI deploys
stop re-uploading it. It does nothing about deployments already stored.

### What could not be checked

- Whether any of the three keys is currently **active**. Validating them means authenticating
  to a third party, which this session's read-only production rule forbids. Treat as live
  until rotation proves otherwise.
- Whether the CLI deploys that carried `.claude/` are still retained by Vercel. Enumerating
  them needs dashboard access, and the account is rate-limited 24h on
  `api-deployments-free-per-day`.

### Founder's ruling, 2026-09-21

Asked directly, with four options offered (redact and leave history / redact and rewrite
history / make the repo private first / he handles it). He chose **"Leave it — you'll handle
all of it."** So: no redaction PR, no history rewrite, no visibility change from any session.
#428 still lands, because its `.claude/` exclusion is the one part that stops the bleeding.

## The house day line counts a cancelled calendar occurrence as one that is happening — OPEN — 2026-09-22

Found by PR #437's pre-merge audit (D1/A3.4) and filed here because the PR's
body called this "tracked, not blocking" with no record anywhere — CLAUDE.md
§0.1/§0.2 require the record to exist, not just the sentence.

**What.** `HouseDayService.read` (`apps/api-gateway/src/house/house-day.service.ts:143-148`)
calls `CalendarService.listEvents(house, { startDate: today, endDate: today,
includeRecurring: true, limit: 100 })` with no `status` filter, and the
`calendar`/`reminders` registers' `ticksFromEvents` (`house-day.service.ts:217-243`)
filters only on `allDay`/`eventTime` — never on `status`. `listEvents`
(`apps/api-gateway/src/calendar/calendar.service.ts:276-352`) applies a status
filter only when the caller passes `query.status` (`:310-313`), which this
caller does not. Cancelling one occurrence of a recurring event sets that row's
`status` to `"cancelled"` (`calendar.service.ts:615-621`, the "Mark as
cancelled instead of deleting" comment) rather than removing it, so the
cancelled occurrence still has a `start_date`/`start_time` for today and still
produces a tick. Confirmed by direct read of all three call sites; not run
against a live database.

**Effect.** "Today's calendar" and "Today's reminders" on the house shell's day
line (sketch 119 §E) can print an event that will not happen today, with no
visual distinction from one that will.

**Tracked by** no CLAIMS row yet — the fix (either the day line dropping
cancelled rows itself, since `ticksFromEvents` already has each row in hand,
or `listEvents` gaining a "not cancelled" default for every caller) is not
decided, so there is nothing to write a static check against. Close this entry
with a `house-day.spec.ts` case: a cancelled occurrence today produces no tick.
**Severity:** low — informational drift on a page still behind the `shell`
flag (default off), not a money or tenancy defect.

## No CI check notices if `SealModule` is dropped from Ask AI — OPEN — 2026-09-22

Found by PR #437's pre-merge audit (D1, Checker A finding 6 / mutation M6) and
filed here for the same reason as the entry above: the PR body called this
"tracked, not blocking" with no record anywhere.

**What.** `AskAiService`'s `sealChallenges` constructor param is
`@Optional()` (`apps/api-gateway/src/ask-ai/ask-ai.service.ts:159`), so Nest
boots successfully whether or not `SealModule` is in `AskAiModule.imports`.
Measured in a mutation copy of PR #437's head (`cd5809e7c`) with
`SealModule` removed from `AskAiModule.imports`: the scoped gateway jest run
(`src/ask-ai src/house src/conversations src/common/seal src/auth/guards`, 16
suites, 196 tests), `scripts/check_ask_ai_is_gated.py`, and
`scripts/check_gateway_boots.sh` all still **pass**. Both sealed routes would
then fail at request time for every restaurant, in production, with no test or
CI job that would catch the regression before merge:
`POST /ask-ai/actions/:id/seal-challenge` answers 500 (`requireSeal()`,
`ask-ai.service.ts:862`), and `sealed-confirm` answers 403 "The seal could not
be checked, so nothing was applied" — `confirmSealed` re-shapes any fault
before its first write into that refusal (this same PR, so the Ask panel's card
stays usable instead of going terminal). The 403 is right for the card and
wrong for anyone watching status codes: a wiring fault reads as a stream of
refusals, visible only in the gateway's "Seal check failed before any write"
error log.

**Tracked by** no CLAIMS row — a CLAIMS check is static text matching
(memory `claims-verify-must-be-static`). It could pin that the `SealModule`
line is still in `AskAiModule.imports`, but not that the real Nest DI graph
hands `AskAiService` a `SealChallengeService` — the runtime question that
matters. That needs a `Test.createTestingModule` assertion on the real
`AskAiModule` (or an extension to `check_gateway_boots.sh` that resolves
`AskAiService` and checks its seal service is present, not just that the graph
resolves). Close this entry by adding that assertion.
**Severity:** the gap is real but the current wiring is correct
(`ask-ai.module.ts` imports `SealModule` today) — this is a missing regression
guard, not a live defect.

## `isDefiniteSendRefusal` classifies a send failure by text that embeds the vendor's address — CLOSED by `fix/send-refusal-typed-classification` — 2026-09-19

**What.** After PR #402, `ProcurementService.isDefiniteSendRefusal` (`procurement.service.ts:6001-6038`) still decides "the vendor provably did not get this" by matching regexes (`invalid_grant`, `does not exist`, `user unknown`, `/\b5\d{2}[ -]/`, `/\b5\.\d\.\d\b/`, …) against `error.message`. That message is built in `sendProviderEmail` (`:6250`) as `Email could not be delivered to ${params.to}: …`, and `params.to` is the vendor's `contact_email`, free text (`providers.dto.ts:103-105`).

**Failure.** `contact_email = "Suite 500 - Orders" <v@x.example>` (or a name holding `does not exist`, `user unknown`, `5.1.1`, …) plus an AMBIGUOUS Gmail failure (socket hang-up after Gmail accepted the message) matches a pattern. `approveDraft` then calls `releaseSendClaim` → `PENDING_APPROVAL`; a manager's re-approve sends a DUPLICATE purchase order. PR #402 closed this for header refusals only, by moving that one class to a typed `SendRefusedBeforeSendError`; every other class is still text.

**Fix.** `GmailService` returns a typed `refusal` read from the error object's fields (HTTP status, OAuth `response.data.error` code, nodemailer `code` / `responseCode`), never from `message`; `isDefiniteSendRefusal` is `instanceof` only. Tracked by CLAIMS row `ADR-0172-SEND-REFUSAL-BY-FIELDS`; ADR 0172 addendum. Closed on that branch. Gmail HTTP 5xx stays AMBIGUOUS by decision (a gateway error can follow an accepted message); only SMTP 5xx replies are definite.

## The scheduled auto-send sweep releases an ambiguous failure as a re-approvable draft — OPEN — 2026-09-19

**What.** `processScheduledAutoSends` (`procurement.service.ts`, the `catch` after its `sendProviderEmail` call) calls `revertScheduledToDraft` on ANY thrown error and tells the manager "back in your queue for one-tap approval". Unlike `approveDraft`, it does not tell a proven refusal from an ambiguous failure, so a socket hang-up after Gmail accepted the reply becomes a `PENDING_APPROVAL` draft; approving it sends the vendor a second copy. Found while closing the entry above; the typed `SendRefusedBeforeSendError` it now needs is in place. The manual reply (`:6575`) and the deal confirmation (`:7141`) propagate the error to the caller without recording a state, which is a different (retry-by-hand) shape and is not part of this entry.

**Superseded by ADR 0175 decision 5** (merged to main after this was filed): every vendor send needs a person's tap, so this sweep is to be removed, not patched (CLAIMS row `ADR-0175-VENDOR-AUTO-SEND-RETIRED`, `open`). Close this entry when that row flips. Until then it is live code.

**Not fixed here** (one operation per branch). If the sweep were kept, the fix is the `approveDraft` shape: release on `instanceof SendRefusedBeforeSendError`, otherwise park the row in an unconfirmed state, which needs a decision on which status an `AUTO_SENDING` row parks in. **Severity:** medium; needs an ambiguous failure on an auto-send-enabled house, and the window is the manager's next tap.
